### G09 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Type constraints must be body static_asserts, not requires-clauses
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Interface design (declarations header, design-r3.txt:232-234, :240; build boundaries, :283); 7. Child storage and access (:399-401)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N14 N03
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: cpp-interface
- Claim: The design states type constraints for the new owners and views (even rows, columns divisible by chunk_size, T is bf16 or const bf16, a 16,384-byte packed-K owner) but never says how they are expressed. The natural C++20 form, a requires-clause on the class template, breaks two other rules of the same design. First, kv_cache_fwd.hpp forward-declares the five templates without constraints and may include only standard headers, so it cannot name chunk_size, which is defined only in the intrinsics header tron/simd/auto.hpp; a definition with a requires-clause would then be a redeclaration with a different template-head, which is ill-formed. Second, the child's std::conditional_t names k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size> for every head size the book declares (64, 128, 256), while k_vnni::layout_on is true only for 128; if k_vnni_tensor carries a class constraint such as Cols == 128, naming it with a non-dependent head size 64 is ill-formed even in the unselected branch. The reviewer's sketch uses static_asserts inside the class bodies for exactly these checks.
- The round-3 design text said: "Type constraints are positive even rows and positive columns divisible by chunk_size. Keep stricter operation constraints, including the packed append width. [...] template <size_t Rows, size_t Cols> class v_vnni_tensor; template <typename T, size_t Rows, size_t Cols> class v_vnni_view; [...] Include only the standard headers needed for sizes and integer sequences."
- Evidence given in round-3: design-r3.txt:232 (five forward declarations, no requires-clauses), :234 (standard headers only), :240 (Restrict T to bf16 or const bf16), :283, :399 (using k_storage = std::conditional_t< k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>, k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size>, native_k_storage>), :401 (a 16,384-byte owner for 64 tokens by 128 dimensions). h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@2880c3aa9b:6 (#include <immintrin.h>) and :86 (static const size_t chunk_size = TRON_CHUNK_SIZE;). h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:17-22 (existing unconstrained forward declarations). h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:91-97 (layout_on<head_size> = (head_size == HEAD_SIZE_128)). kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:536-537 (kv_block forward-declared unconstrained, instantiated per declared geometry); t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:812-814 and :1568 (geometry_64, geometry_128, geometry_256 pages). C++20 [temp.pre] (all declarations of a template must have equivalent template-heads including the requires-clause; clang: 'requires clause differs in template redeclaration'); C++20 [temp.names]/8 (a simple-template-id naming a constrained class template with non-dependent arguments must satisfy its constraints; clang: 'constraints not satisfied for class template'). Reviewer sketch (comment 5765866077, evidence/github-review.json): static_assert(Tokens > 0 && Tokens % 2 == 0) inside v_vnni_layout and static_assert(std::same_as<std::remove_const_t<T>, bf16>) inside vnni_view.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add to section 5 'Build and include boundaries' after 'Type constraints are positive even rows and positive columns divisible by chunk_size.': 'Express every owner and view type constraint (even rows, columns divisible by chunk_size, T is bf16 or const bf16, and the child's 64-token by 128-dimension owner size) as a static_assert inside the class body, never as a requires-clause on the class template. kv_cache_fwd.hpp declares these templates without constraints and cannot name chunk_size, which lives in the intrinsics header tron/simd/auto.hpp; a redeclaration with a different requires-clause does not compile. The child's std::conditional_t names k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size> for every declared head size, and an unsatisfied class-template constraint is an error even in the branch that is not selected.' Repeat the last two sentences under the k_storage snippet in section 7.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. One verifier reproduced the redeclaration error with g++ 11.4 ("redeclaration ... with different constraints"); clang 19 was not available on this host. Corrections: the block has three class forward declarations plus two alias templates, not five templates; the std::conditional_t hazard is conditional on a class-level constraint that fails for a declared geometry (the design's stated constraints hold for all of 64, 128 and 256). The codebase precedent cuts both ways: kv_cache.hpp:538-540 forward-declares scaled_v_expr with its requires-clause repeated, which works only because that header can name chunk_size.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Keep the finding and its recommendation. Apply these narrowings and citation fixes:

1. Evidence "five forward declarations" at design-r3.txt:232: the block has three class forward declarations (v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view, v_vnni_row) and two alias template definitions (native_k_view, const_native_k_view). Alias templates cannot be forward-declared. Say "three class forward declarations".

2. Replace the unverified clang diagnostic with the one measured here: g++ 11.4.0, -std=c++20, "error: redeclaration 'template<long unsigned int Rows, long unsigned int Cols> class v_vnni_tensor' with different constraints". Clang was not available on this host; keep its wording only if labelled unverified.

3. Add the codebase precedent, which strengthens leg 1: kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:538-540 forward-declares scaled_v_expr WITH `requires(head_size % chunk_size == 0)` and repeats the clause at the definition (:1988-1990). That is the local convention for a constrained class template. kv_cache_fwd.hpp cannot follow it because it cannot name chunk_size (design-r3.txt:234; h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@2880c3aa9b:6, :86).

4. Narrow leg 2. The claim "naming it with a non-dependent head size 64 is ill-formed even in the unselected branch" is right (confirmed with g++ 11.4.0: kv_block<64, 64> fails when k_vnni_tensor has `requires(Cols == 128)`), but it applies only to a constraint that head size 64 or 256 fails. The design's V constraints at :283 (even rows, columns divisible by chunk_size) are satisfied by every declared geometry (64, 128, 256 at t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp@2880c3aa9b:62-64), and Ben's sketch K constraints (Rows % 16 == 0, Cols % 32 == 0) also pass. The hazard is an implementer turning design-r3.txt:401 ("a 16,384-byte owner for 64 tokens by 128 dimensions") into a class-template constraint. Reword the recommendation's last sentence to: "an unsatisfied class-template constraint is an error even in the branch that is not selected, so the 64-token by 128-dimension owner check of section 7 must be a static_assert on the selected instance (or on the kernel boundary), never a constraint on k_vnni_tensor itself."

5. Section reference: the k_storage snippet is at design-r3.txt:399 and the requirements sentence at :401; the "Build and include boundaries" bullets are :281-284. Cite :232-234, :240, :283, :399, :401 as the finding does; all match the text.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Three narrowings, keeping the finding and its recommendation.

1. Strengthen the first argument: it is not only chunk_size that kv_cache_fwd.hpp cannot name. The TRON_CHUNK_SIZE macro is also defined inside the intrinsics header [h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@2880c3aa9b:17-21], so no requires-clause spelling of "columns divisible by chunk_size" is available to that header. Cite the g++ 11.4 result: "redeclaration ... with different constraints" (scratchpad/agent-notes/g09/a_redecl.cpp).

2. Narrow the second argument to a conditional hazard. The constraints the design states (even rows, columns divisible by chunk_size) [design-r3.txt:213, :283] and the sketch's K checks (Tokens % 16, Dims % 32) hold for every declared geometry (page_size 64; head sizes 64, 128, 256 at t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:158, :812-814, :1568). The conditional_t error appears only if the implementer adds a class constraint that some declared geometry fails, for example Cols == 128. Say that this is plausible because the moved mapping constants hard-code 64 tokens by 128 dimensions [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:52-61] and the design asks for a 16,384-byte 64-by-128 owner [design-r3.txt:401]. Cite the g++ result: "template constraint failure" at kv_block<64, 64> (scratchpad/agent-notes/g09/b_conditional.cpp); the static_assert form compiles (c_static_assert.cpp).

3. Adjust the recommendation text in two places. (a) Replace "kv_cache_fwd.hpp ... cannot name chunk_size, which lives in the intrinsics header tron/simd/auto.hpp" with "kv_cache_fwd.hpp cannot name chunk_size or the TRON_CHUNK_SIZE macro; both are defined in the intrinsics header tron/simd/auto.hpp". (b) Add one sentence for section 7: "Put the 16,384-byte owner check and sizeof(k) == sizeof(v) in kv_block as static_asserts, as the existing static_assert(sizeof(k) == sizeof(v)) at kv_cache.hpp:2205 does; do not make k_vnni_tensor a 64-by-128-only class, because the std::conditional_t names it for every declared head size." Also note that the repo's own precedent of repeating a requires-clause on a forward declaration [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:539-540] does not transfer here, because that header includes the intrinsics and kv_cache_fwd.hpp may not.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow and fix five points. (1) kv_cache_fwd.hpp forward-declares three class templates (v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view, v_vnni_row) plus two alias templates (native_k_view, const_native_k_view) [design-r3.txt:232]; alias templates carry no constraints and are not redeclared, so write "three class templates", not "five templates". (2) The K types are defined in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp [design-r3.txt:391]; the design only implies that the child later adds their declarations next to the V ones ("v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions", :284). Say the redeclaration rule applies to the V types in the parent and to the K types when the child adds their forward declarations. (3) Strengthen the "cannot name chunk_size" sentence: the macro TRON_CHUNK_SIZE is also defined only in h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@2880c3aa9b:18 and :20, so no spelling of the columns constraint exists in an intrinsics-free header. (4) Make the conditional_t point conditional and precise: it bites only when the owner carries a shape constraint that some declared head size fails, such as Cols == 128; the reviewer's sketch constraints for the K layout (Tokens % 16 == 0, Dims % 32 == 0) pass for every declared head size. The declared head sizes in t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b are 64, 128, 256 (:158-160) and also 512 (:203), not only 64/128/256. Replace "non-dependent head size 64" with "the check runs when kv_block<64, 64> is instantiated" (the argument is dependent inside the kv_block template). (5) The quoted diagnostics are clang's; g++ 11.4 says "redeclaration ... with different constraints" and "template constraint failure". Keep the recommendation; add one clause: "This follows the reviewer's sketch (static_asserts inside v_vnni_layout and vnni_view) and the repository's view primary template (h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:27-29)."

### G10 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Five view members required 'by expression consumers' that no consumer reads; valid() implies a null view the design does not provide
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Interface design, Owner and expression reads (design-r3.txt:242)
- Kind: scope | found by lenses: simplicity
- Claim: The design orders dim, element, const_element, shape0() and valid() on the packed view 'as required by expression consumers', but no expression consumer in the repository reads any of them. The only named consumer, host-tensor construction, uses operator[] alone. valid() additionally implies a default-constructed (null) view, which the design never provides, since it forbids a public raw-pointer constructor and says rows are constructed only by the matrix view. The round-2 text said 'Keep ... consistent with existing views'; round 3 turned that into a requirement claim.
- The round-3 design text said: "Supply dim, element, const_element, shape0(), and valid() as required by expression consumers. Leave the rank-1 expression concept unchanged. The named V expression consumer is the ordinary host-tensor construction check in section 8."
- Evidence given in round-3: h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:268-274 (tensor(expr<B,d0,d1,...> const& rhs) loops std::construct_at(&data[i], rhs[i]) and uses nothing else); h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:68-74 (data[i] = other[i] only); h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:147-170 (v_in[i + offset0] and an optional .mat member only). Search of h/tron/tensor, h/tron/kernels and h/tron/hardware for ::dim, ::element, ::const_element and .valid(): only h/tron/hardware/permuted.hpp:120-121, a local alias view<float>::dim, not an expression parameter. shape0() readers: views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:250-253 (array_views, needs views<> inheritance, which design-r3.txt:241 forbids) and hardware/tensor.hpp:486-490 (matmul activations), neither a V consumer. view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:56-58 (valid() means data != nullptr) and :43 (explicit constexpr view(T* data = nullptr)); self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1775, :1810 (only cache use: a default-constructed K view). design-r3.txt:248 (no public raw-pointer constructor, no default constructor). evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:213 (round-2 wording).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the first sentence of design-r3.txt:242 with: 'The matrix view provides operator[] returning the row expression; the named consumer, tensor<R,D> constructed from expr<B,R,D> (tensor.hpp:268-274), needs nothing else. Add dim, element, const_element, shape0() or valid() only when a named caller reads them. valid() would also need a default-constructed null view, which this design does not provide.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: round 2 already claimed the three aliases were required; round 3 extended the claim to shape0() and valid(). The host tensor constructor is at tensor.hpp:270-275. valid() implies a null view state the design never specifies (it does not forbid a default constructor). Two K test readers at t_llama_unit.cpp:703-709 use dim and valid(), but they read the native K view, which the parent keeps.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Four narrowings, none changing the verdict. (1) History sentence: the round-2 text already claimed a consumer requirement for the aliases: "Supply the shape and element aliases required by the existing expression consumers. Keep dim, element, const_element, shape0(), and valid() consistent with existing views" [evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:213]. Round 3 extended the "required" claim from the three aliases to shape0() and valid() and dropped the "consistent with existing views" framing [design-wdiff.txt:545-547]. Reword to: "Round 2 claimed the aliases were required by existing consumers; round 3 extends that claim to shape0() and valid()." (2) "uses operator[] alone" is true at the matrix level only. The rank-1 host tensor built from each row reads rhs.chunk(i) and, when N % 16 != 0, rhs.last() [tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:79-85]; the design already specifies chunk and bf16_chunk for the row [design-r3.txt:245-246], and every supported head size (64, 128, 256, 512) is a multiple of 16, so last() is not needed. Reword the recommendation's "needs nothing else" to "needs only operator[] from the matrix view and chunk()/bf16_chunk() from the row, which lines 245-246 already specify". (3) Citation: the rank-2 tensor constructor spans tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:270-275 (template line 270, signature 272, loop 273-274), not 268-274. (4) The valid() reasoning: the design does not forbid a default constructor; it does not specify one. Replace "which the design never provides, since it forbids a public raw-pointer constructor" with "which the design does not specify: the no-raw-pointer rule at design-r3.txt:248 does not exclude a default constructor, but no sentence provides one, so valid() has no defined false state". Two additions that strengthen the evidence: the only place shape0() is truly required is the views<>/const_views<> CRTP bases, which call self().shape0() [views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:88-91, 135-138], and design-r3.txt:241 forbids that base; and t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:704-709 reads ::dim and valid() on the native K view returned by page->k, which the design keeps as the ordinary view alias [design-r3.txt:231, 262], so that test is not a packed-view consumer.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Three narrowings. (1) Round-2 history: the round-2 text already said "Supply the shape and element aliases required by the existing expression consumers" and separately "Keep dim, element, const_element, shape0(), and valid() consistent with existing views" [evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:213]. Round 3 did not create the requirement claim; it extended it from the three aliases to shape0() and valid() and dropped the "consistent with existing views" justification [design-r3.txt:242; design-wdiff.txt:545-547]. Reword the finding's last sentence accordingly. (2) Citations: the host tensor constructor is at h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:270-275 (operator= at 277-282), not 268-274. valid() is at view.hpp:57-59 (rank 2) and :139-141 (rank 1); the default nullptr constructor is at :43 and :125-126. Extend the evidence list with the readers outside the three searched directories: ::dim at h/tron/files/safetensors.hpp:516, 838-840 and t/t_llama_unit.cpp:704; .valid() at t/t_llama_unit.cpp:707-709. All are K-view or owner-tensor sites, so the conclusion is unchanged. (3) Recommendation: keep the proposed replacement sentence, and add the alternative the round-2 wording allowed: the design may keep the five members "for consistency with existing views", but then it must say when valid() returns false (a default constructor, or a move constructor that nulls the source as view.hpp:46-47 does), or drop valid(). Note that the existing K-view test default-constructs a const view and checks valid() [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:706-709]; a V test that mirrors it would need the default constructor the design does not provide.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Four narrowings. (1) The design does not "forbid" a default constructor for the view. design-r3.txt:248 forbids a public raw-pointer constructor, a data member, and a pointer conversion, and says rows are constructed only by the matrix view; it is silent on a default constructor for the view. Reword the second claim to: "valid() implies a null view state, which the design never specifies." (2) The evidence's "only cache use" of a default-constructed view misses t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:703-709 (STATIC_REQUIRE on decltype(rows)::dim; `decltype(const_rows) lazy_rows;` then REQUIRE_FALSE(lazy_rows.valid()) and REQUIRE after assignment). Both readers are K views, which the parent keeps ordinary [design-r3.txt:262], so they do not weaken the claim for V; they matter only if the child's packed K view is expected to pass that test (child scope, outside this finding). (3) Round 2 already carried a requirement claim: design-revised-text.txt:213 said "Supply the shape and element aliases required by the existing expression consumers" and then listed the five members as "consistent with existing views". Round 3 merged the two sentences and attached "required" to all five [design-wdiff.txt:545-547]. Say "extended the requirement claim to all five members", not "turned it into a requirement claim". (4) Keep the recommended replacement, and offer the lighter alternative codex may prefer: keep the five members but state the true reason ("for parity with the existing view's member set [view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:36-38, :56-62]; no consumer in this design reads them"), and decide the null-state question in the "View copy and conversion" row [design-r3.txt:248] with one of two sentences: "v_vnni_view has no default constructor and no valid()", or "A default-constructed v_vnni_view is null; valid() reports it; every other member asserts valid()".

### G12 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] bf16_chunk override and decltype test protect a test-only path; the decltype check belongs in the header, not the suite
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Interface design, operation table (design-r3.txt:247); 8. Behavior checks, Expression reads row (:440)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N13
- Kind: scope | found by lenses: simplicity
- Claim: The design admits the expression base's bf16_chunk fallback is bit-exact and that only a test constructs a host tensor from the packed view. The mandated override therefore speeds up a path with no production consumer. The round-3 text then adds a decltype assertion inside the test suite to prove the override exists. That is an assertion about structure with no consumer or failure mode, which the repository's test rules forbid, and it differs from what N13 asked for (a static_assert in the header or an instruction record).
- The round-3 design text said: "Check decltype(&Row::bf16_chunk) against bf16s (Row::*)(size_t) const noexcept to distinguish the derived override from an inherited fallback. Use an existing hardware-tensor fixture only if it already accepts this input. | Broken expression compatibility or a missing direct-bit override. Bit patterns alone cannot detect the bit-exact fallback."
- Evidence given in round-3: design-r3.txt:242 ('The named V expression consumer is the ordinary host-tensor construction check in section 8. Production V paths continue to use bulk operations.'), :440. h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:110-117 (if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk) ... else fp32s_to_bf16s(self().chunk(i))). h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp@2880c3aa9b:92-108 (fp32s_to_bf16s changes bits only where the exponent field is 0x7f800000 and the low 16 bits are nonzero); kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2000-2007 (even_part shift and odd_part mask leave the low 16 bits zero), so the fallback is bit-exact; the round-2 N13 lead note confirms it. AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:35-41 ('Do not use tests merely to document the current implementation. Assertions about names, values, defaults, ordering, fields, or structure require an identifiable consumer, user-visible behavior, compatibility requirement, or failure mode'). r2-findings.md N13 recommendation: static_assert(std::is_same_v<decltype(&v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::bf16_chunk), bf16s (v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::*)(size_t) const noexcept>) in the header, or the instruction record, as the detection. design-wdiff.txt:1600 (decltype sentence new in round 3).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Pick one and say so. Option A (smallest): in design-r3.txt:247 replace the row with 'v_vnni_row does not override bf16_chunk. The expression base's fallback fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)) is bit-exact for stored bf16, because even_part and odd_part leave the low 16 bits zero. The only consumer is the section-8 host-tensor check.' and delete the decltype sentence at :440. Option B: keep the override for speed and move the check into the header: 'v_vnni.hpp asserts, next to the class, static_assert(std::is_same_v<decltype(&v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::bf16_chunk), bf16s (v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::*)(size_t) const noexcept>);' and delete the decltype sentence from the section-8 table.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: the repository rule (AGENTS.md:35-38) allows structural assertions with an identifiable consumer or failure mode rather than forbidding them; the design's named consumer never calls bf16_chunk (host tensors read rows through chunk(i), tensor.hpp:81-83), so the decltype check in the suite protects a path nothing in the parent's test plan exercises. Either option in the recommendation closes it.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three narrowings; the substance and both options stand. (a) Replace "which the repository's test rules forbid" with "which the repository's test rules allow only with an identifiable consumer, compatibility requirement, or failure mode [AGENTS.md:35-38@2880c3aa9b]; the design names no production consumer of the bf16 read [design-r3.txt:242], so the check protects only the speed of a test-only read". (b) Add the fact that makes the point sharper: the rest of the row never exercises bf16_chunk at all. The host tensor<N> constructs and assigns from an expression through store_chunk(i, rhs.chunk(i)), the fp32 path [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:80-84, 119-124@2880c3aa9b]; only dmatensor<bf16> calls rhs.bf16_chunk(i) [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:272-276@2880c3aa9b], and the row makes that fixture optional ("only if it already accepts this input", design-r3.txt:440). So the decltype sentence is the row's only bf16_chunk detection, and the "exact bf16 copies" check passes with or without the override. (c) Add that N13's alternative detector is also absent: the instruction-comparison list at design-r3.txt:486-488 does not name the bf16 row read, so the design provides neither the header static_assert nor the instruction record N13 asked for. For Option B, spell the header as h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp (the design's V header, design-r3.txt:372), not vnni.hpp. For Option A, also note that design-r3.txt:328 ("bf16_chunk() preserves stored bits") stays true under the fallback and needs no change, and that section-9 rows :517 and :542 ("Correct override/fallback behavior", "Correct bf16 override detection") should then say the override was dropped rather than corrected.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow three parts of the finding. (1) Replace 'speeds up a path with no production consumer' with 'speeds up a path that nothing in the parent's test plan calls: the named host-tensor consumer reads through chunk(i) only [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:81-83, 272-274], and the only bf16_chunk callers at main are the DMA hardware tensor [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:276, 296, 316, 329] and slice_expr [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp@2880c3aa9b:715-716], of which the design makes the hardware-tensor fixture optional [design-r3.txt:440]'. (2) Replace 'which the repository's test rules forbid' with 'which the repository's test rules allow only with an identifiable consumer or a failure mode important to preserve [AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:35-38]; the design's own text removes the failure mode by calling the fallback bit-exact [design-r3.txt:247, :440], and a header static_assert would sit outside the test policy'. (3) Replace 'adds a decltype assertion inside the test suite' with 'does not say where the member-pointer check lives: section 5 says only "Verify the member-pointer type" [design-r3.txt:247], and section 8 lists the decltype check as evidence from the existing tensor/cache cases [design-r3.txt:440], which reads as a suite assertion; round-2 N13 asked for a static_assert in the header'. Keep both options. Add to Option A the precedent that the ordinary contiguous bf16 view defines no bf16_chunk override at main (git grep bf16_chunk at 2880c3aa9b over h/, src/, t/ finds definitions only in dmatensor.hpp:367, permuted.hpp:53, expr.hpp:111/322/715). Add to Option B the cost sentence the design dropped from N13: 'The override saves a shift, a NaN check, and a narrow per chunk; the fallback is bit-exact.' Also note this is a new round-3 simplicity finding, not an N13 residual: N13 asked for the override and the check, and N52 said to keep the row's bf16_chunk body [r2-findings.md N13, N52].

(1) Replace "which the repository's test rules forbid" with "which is hard to justify under AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:35-38: structural assertions need an identifiable consumer or a failure mode that matters, and the only failure mode here is a slower read on a path the design itself calls test-only [design-r3.txt:242]; the suite does hold type-level assertions elsewhere [t/t_fp32.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1329-1342; t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:333-337]". ### G13 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] even_part and odd_part are private members of scaled_v_expr; the row's chunk() cannot reuse them as written
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Interface design, pair chunk paragraph (design-r3.txt:251)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N52
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: handoff
- Claim: even_part and odd_part are private member functions of detail::scaled_v_expr in kv_cache.hpp, and odd_part reads the member hi_16_of_32. kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp, not the reverse, so v_vnni_row::chunk() in v_vnni.hpp cannot call them. The round-3 edit changed 'Reuse this extraction pattern' to naming the two functions, and does not say whether to hoist them into v_vnni.hpp or copy the two intrinsics. For bf16_chunk the closer existing template is get_v(bf16*)'s cvtepi32_epi16 narrowing, which the design does not point at.
- The round-3 design text said: "Reuse the existing even_part left shift and odd_part high-half mask for fp32 reads. Narrow the selected halves for bf16 reads."
- Evidence given in round-3: h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1995-2005 (inside struct scaled_v_expr: private: const __m512i hi_16_of_32; fp32x16 odd_part(bf16x32 x) const noexcept { return _mm512_castsi512_ps(_mm512_and_epi32(x, hi_16_of_32)); } fp32x16 even_part(bf16x32 x) const noexcept { return _mm512_castsi512_ps(_mm512_slli_epi32(x, 16)); }); :2452-2463 (get_v bf16: _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16(both) and _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16(_mm512_srli_epi32(both, 16))). design-r3.txt:284 ('v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions') and :373 (kv_cache.hpp is the includer). evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:225 ('Reuse this extraction pattern'); design-wdiff.txt segments @old 2663-2686.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the two sentences with: 'Copy the two-intrinsic extraction of scaled_v_expr's private even_part (_mm512_slli_epi32 by 16) and odd_part (_mm512_and_epi32 with 0xffff0000; kv_cache.hpp:1997-2005) into v_vnni.hpp, or hoist both helpers into that header and have scaled_v_expr call them; kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp, not the reverse. For bf16_chunk follow get_v(bf16*)'s narrowing, _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 after the shift or mask (kv_cache.hpp:2452-2463).'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3 (one at note). Corrections: round 2 already named even_part and odd_part with the words "Reuse this extraction pattern"; round 3 merged the sentences and dropped "pattern", so the text no longer hints that the two private, unused helpers are copied, not called. The better pointer is get_v(float*) (kv_cache.hpp:2416-2431), whose loop the design already moves into v_vnni.hpp as load_row.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow the claim: the design's sentence at design-r3.txt:251 names operations (shift, mask), not a call, so no wrong build follows. The defect is that it points at scaled_v_expr's private helpers [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1996-2007] instead of the non-private, identical extraction in page::get_v(float*) [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2416-2431], whose loops the design already moves into v_vnni.hpp as load_row [design-r3.txt:259]. Fix the citation "1995-2005" to "1996-2007". Cite design-r3.txt:392 (not :373) for the include direction. Replace the recommendation with: "Replace the two sentences at design-r3.txt:251 with: 'row.chunk(i) reuses one iteration of the load_row loop moved from get_v(float*) (kv_cache.hpp:2416-2431: _mm512_slli_epi32 by 16 for an even token, _mm512_and_si512 with 0xffff0000 for an odd token). bf16_chunk(i) reuses one iteration of the load_row loop moved from get_v(bf16*) (kv_cache.hpp:2452-2463: _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 after the shift or the srli). scaled_v_expr's private even_part and odd_part (kv_cache.hpp:1996-2007) stay in kv_cache.hpp; kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp, not the reverse.'" Keep relates_to N52; the N52 verifier correction asked for the get_v pointer as an optional sentence.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow two points. (1) The round-2 text already named both functions: 'The existing even_part shifts each 32-bit lane left by 16 bits; odd_part masks the high 16 bits. Reuse this extraction pattern' [evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:225]. The round-3 edit did not introduce the names; it merged the two sentences into 'Reuse the existing even_part left shift and odd_part high-half mask' [design-wdiff.txt segments @old 2663-2686], which removed the word 'pattern' and so removed the only hint that the code is copied rather than called. Reword the claim: 'both revisions name the two functions; the round-3 wording dropped the word pattern and still does not say the helpers are private and must be copied or hoisted, which round-1 F35 correction (b) asked for.' (2) The bf16_chunk part is smaller than stated: the design already moves the get_v loops, which contain the cvtepi32_epi16 narrowing [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2449-2464], into v_vnni.hpp as load_row [design-r3.txt:259], so the narrowing template will sit in the same header. What is missing is only one sentence that bf16_chunk(i) is one iteration of that load_row loop. Also fix the evidence citation from '1995-2005' to '1996-2007' (private: at 1996, even_part closes at 2007) and add the new fact that even_part and odd_part have no caller at 2880c3aa9b, so 'reuse the existing' points at unused code. Suggested replacement text for design-r3.txt:251: 'A 16-lane pair chunk contains 32 bf16 values in 64 bytes. For fp32 reads, copy the two intrinsics of scaled_v_expr's private, unused even_part (_mm512_slli_epi32 by 16) and odd_part (_mm512_and_epi32 with 0xffff0000) [kv_cache.hpp:1996-2007] into v_vnni.hpp, or hoist both into that header; kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp, so the row cannot call them in place. For bf16 reads, bf16_chunk(i) is one iteration of the get_v(bf16*) loop that load_row moves: _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 of the raw pair load for even rows, and of the load shifted right by 16 for odd rows [kv_cache.hpp:2449-2464].'
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the claim: drop the sentence "The round-3 edit changed 'Reuse this extraction pattern' to naming the two functions"; the round-2 text already named even_part and odd_part with the same "Reuse this extraction pattern" instruction [design-revised-text.txt:225], and round 3 only merged two sentences [design-wdiff.txt @old 2663-2686]. State the relation to N52 as: N52 was a NOTE with recommendation "No change"; its get_v pointer was optional. Fix citations: `private:` at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1996, hi_16_of_32 at :1997, odd_part :2000-2002, even_part :2005-2007; get_v(bf16*) body :2447-2463 with the two cvtepi32_epi16 lines at :2455 and :2461. Reduce the recommendation to one optional clarifying sentence after design-r3.txt:251: "Copy the two intrinsics into v_vnni.hpp (even: _mm512_slli_epi32 by 16; odd: _mm512_and_epi32 with 0xffff0000); scaled_v_expr's private helpers stay in kv_cache.hpp:1996-2007 because kv_cache.hpp includes v_vnni.hpp. For bf16_chunk select the half first and then narrow, as get_v(bf16*) does (kv_cache.hpp:2447-2463): even = _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16(pair), odd = _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16(_mm512_srli_epi32(pair, 16)); do not narrow the fp32 output of the shift or mask, whose low 16 bits are zero." Add: the section-8 check "exact bf16 copies with independent patterns" [design-r3.txt:440] detects a wrong narrowing, so the risk if the sentence is not added is low.

### G15 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] fill_random is called a 'poison fill'; it writes finite normal-distribution values
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Existing callers table, book::fill_random row (design-r3.txt:274); 8. Storage and lifecycle row (:441); context :262
- Relates to earlier id(s): M02
- Kind: factual error | found by lenses: behavior-arena, regression-diff, disposition-checker
- Claim: The new fill_random row tells the implementer to keep the function's 'poison-fill behavior' and section 8 lists 'poison fill' among the lifecycle cases. book::fill_random draws every element from std::normal_distribution<>(0.0, 1.0) through fill_storage_slot and casts each double to bf16. No NaN is written. The design uses 'poison' for NaN everywhere else (:300, :416, :436), and the NaN poison in the existing tests is written through page::set_v with a quiet_NaN row, not through fill_random. The word 'poison' for this fill is new in round 3. An implementer may add NaN filling to fill_random, look for a poison constant, or expect NaN from it.
- The round-3 design text said: "| book::fill_random , fill_storage_slot | Preserve the layout-blind whole-block fill as the explicit test-support exception above. | Keep its poison-fill behavior and existing lifecycle-test callers. Do not expose a new public pointer. |"
- Evidence given in round-3: kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1054-1065 (comment 'Fill all kv_block data with random values', 'bypasses the zero-init V slot invariant'; :1059 std::normal_distribution<> dis(0.0, 1.0); for_each_active_slot(... fill_storage_slot<geometry>(kv_storage_offset(slot), dis, gen))); :1365-1375 (data[i] = static_cast<bf16>(distribution(gen))). t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:784-785 and :2008 (the only fill_random callers); :1919-1925, :2701-2705, :2788-2792 (NaN poison via set_v with a quiet_NaN buffer). design-r3.txt:262 (correct description: 'fills an entire K/V block without interpreting coordinates'), :300, :416, :436, :441. design-wdiff.txt:883 (INSERT @old 3183 / new 4305: the row and 'poison-fill' are NEW). Round-2 text mentions 'poison' only at design-revised-text.txt:285, :395, :473, none about fill_random; M02's recommendation said 'byte fill of the whole trivially copyable block'.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace 'Keep its poison-fill behavior and existing lifecycle-test callers.' with 'Keep its random finite-value fill (std::normal_distribution, kv_cache.hpp:1059; garbage that bypasses partner zeroing and must not be read) and its two existing callers (t_llama_unit.cpp:784-785, :2008 at 2880c3aa9b). NaN poison in tests is written through page::set_v with a NaN source row, not through fill_random.' In the section 8 Storage and lifecycle row (:441), replace 'poison fill' with 'random whole-block fill', or name the NaN set_v cases if those are meant.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: kind is terminology inconsistency rather than factual error ("poison" can mean any sentinel); the word is new in round 3; drop "must not be read" from the replacement text, because TEST_CASE "apply and join page ranges" reads the random values back as attention inputs (t_llama_unit.cpp:2026-2041). The design's own uses at :300 and :436 mean NaN; :416 means the child's 0xa5a5 sentinel.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three narrowings, none changing the verdict. (1) The recommended replacement wording "garbage that bypasses partner zeroing and must not be read" is wrong for one caller. TEST_CASE "apply and join page ranges" [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1953] calls bk.fill_random(gen) at :2008 and then reads the filled values back through page->k(...) and page->get_v(...) into random_ks/random_vs "for emulation::compute_attention" [:2026-2041]. The random values are the test's ordinary K/V input, not unread garbage. Reword to: "Keep its random finite-value fill (std::normal_distribution, kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1059-1061, 1365-1374). It is ordinary random test data that the tests read back through the typed accessors. It bypasses partner zeroing and must not establish readiness. Keep its two callers: TEST_CASE 'fixed KV layout addresses each slot geometry' (:761, calls at :784-785) and TEST_CASE 'apply and join page ranges' (:1953, call at :2008)." (2) "The design uses 'poison' for NaN everywhere else" overstates :416. That sentence refers to t_k_vnni_layout, whose poison is the 0xA5A5 sentinel [t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:51, :108, :191] plus the NaN 0x7FC1 sentinel [:257, :287]. Reword to "a deliberate sentinel bit pattern (NaN at :300 and :436; 0xA5A5 or NaN 0x7FC1 at :416)". A random normal-distribution fill is neither. (3) "existing lifecycle-test callers" in the design row is also loose: neither caller is a lifecycle test. Their tags are [kv_data][book append] (:761) and [page_range] (:1953). For the section-8 row [design-r3.txt:441], keep the finding's hedge: "poison fill" there may mean the NaN set_v sections of TEST_CASE "scaled_v padding NaN" [:2658, sections "append_v preserves poison" :2670 and "single-token dirty-buffer reuse" :2779] rather than fill_random. Ask the design to name which cases it means.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow in three places. (1) Kind: "terminology inconsistency (ambiguity)" rather than "factual error". In loose engineering usage "poison" can mean any garbage sentinel, and the operation is a fill with values that must not be read; the defect is that the design's own three uses [design-r3.txt:300, :416, :436] and the existing tests [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1919-1925, :2696-2705, :2785-2789] all mean NaN, and the Words table [:8] defines NaN but not "poison". (2) Risk: drop "may add NaN filling to fill_random". Line 262 already says "Preserve book::fill_random ... Retain its comment about bypassing partner zeroing", and :274 says "Preserve the layout-blind whole-block fill ... Keep", so a change to the function is unlikely. The realistic effect is at :441: an implementer cannot tell whether "poison fill" names the two fill_random cases [t_llama_unit.cpp:784-785, :2008], which never use the word, or the NaN set_v cases that the V-append row at :436 already covers, so one lifecycle case may be dropped or double-counted. (3) Recommendation: keep as proposed for :274 (name std::normal_distribution at kv_cache.hpp:1059 and the two callers). For :441 write "random whole-block fill (fill_random)" if the fill_random cases are meant, or "NaN partner cases" if the set_v cases are meant. If the sentence about how test NaN is written must cover both widths, say "through page::set_v (16-lane) or page::v().set (8-lane)" [t_llama_unit.cpp:1922-1925].
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Four narrowings to the finding text and its recommendation. (1) "The design uses 'poison' for NaN everywhere else (:300, :416, :436)" -> ":300 and :436 mean NaN; :416 refers to the child's t_k_vnni_layout sentinel fill, which is a fixed bit pattern (POISON_0XA5A5 = 0xa5a5, a finite bf16, t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:50-51, :108, :191) or NaN 0x7fc1 (:257, :287). Neither meaning fits a normal-distribution fill." (2) The recommended replacement text says the fill is "garbage that bypasses partner zeroing and must not be read". Drop "must not be read": TEST_CASE "apply and join page ranges" [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1953, fill at :2008] reads the filled values back through page->k(...) and page->get_v(...) as attention inputs [:2027-2040]. Suggested row text for design-r3.txt:274: "Keep its random finite-value fill (std::normal_distribution, kv_cache.hpp:1059, 1373) and its two callers: TEST_CASE 'fixed KV layout addresses each slot geometry' (t_llama_unit.cpp:761, fill at :784-785, distinct pre-existing content for the append check) and TEST_CASE 'apply and join page ranges' (:1953, fill at :2008, read back as K/V inputs at :2027-2040). It writes no NaN; NaN poison in tests goes through page::set_v with a quiet_NaN row (:1919-1925, :2701-2705, :2788-2792)." (3) "existing lifecycle-test callers" [design-r3.txt:274] is also loose: the two callers are tagged [kv_data][book append] and [page_range], not lifecycle; the section 8 row's own list ("mixed geometry", "whole-page and partial-page append") covers the first caller, so the row at :441 should say "random whole-block fill (fill_random)" and, if the NaN set_v cases are meant as well, name them separately, since :436 already covers them. (4) Line nits: the fill_random comment is :1054-1056 and the function :1057-1065; fill_storage_slot spans :1365-1375 with the assignment at :1373 (the finding cites 1365-1375 correctly but attributes the assignment to the range, not the line).

### G16 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] No stated forward-declaration path for k_vnni_view in the intrinsics-free kernel interface header
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Build and include boundaries (design-r3.txt:284); 7. Child adaptation (:391-392) and Copy and score readers row (:407)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N58 N03
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: cpp-interface, child-needs
- Claim: The child's typed kernel declaration qk_vnni_128x4(k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128> k_page, ...) lives in h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp, which the design keeps free of intrinsics. A by-value parameter in a declaration needs at least a declaration of the class template. The parent's kv_cache_fwd.hpp declares only the V types and the native-K aliases. The design defines k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view and k_vnni_row in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp, which also receives the AVX-512 scatter, gather and block-store bodies, so the interface header cannot include it. The design never says that the child adds the K forward declarations to kv_cache_fwd.hpp, or where else amx_attn_iface.hpp gets them. An implementer must guess the place or include the intrinsics header.
- The round-3 design text said: "amx_attn_iface.hpp includes kv_cache_fwd.hpp and remains free of intrinsics. v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions. [...] Define k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and k_vnni_row for the proposed logical expression reads in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp."
