### G17 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Header home of the child's typed K storage operations and of detail::k_vnni_access is not stated; 'k_vnni.hpp' is named without a path
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:390-392); 5. Internal storage access row (:249) and Build and include boundaries (:284); Q1 (:95)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N58 N03 M07 N59
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: child-needs, disposition-checker
- Claim: The round-2 text placed 'the K mapping and storage operations' in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp. The round-3 text assigns only the three types, the mapping constants, layout_on and index to that header, and says the kernel header 'retains software score arithmetic'. The typed store_row, load_row, store_block, token copy and plane copy that rows 405-407 call are assigned to no header. The friend accessor detail::k_vnni_access is placed 'in its own header', where 'its' can mean the child or the struct. If an implementer keeps scatter_row, gather_row and store_block in h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp (their location today), kv_cache.hpp must keep including the kernel header, which the round-2 split was meant to end, and 'The cache includes the K tensor header' is not sufficient. After the port two headers share the basename k_vnni.hpp; line 284 names 'k_vnni.hpp' without a path. Line 95 says 'Keep the shared access plumbing small' while line 249 defines no shared access struct.
- The round-3 design text said: "Define k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and k_vnni_row for the proposed logical expression reads in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp. Move mapping constants, k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>, and the existing constexpr free function k_vnni::index there. [...] The cache includes the K tensor header. h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp includes that header and retains software score arithmetic. [...] The child uses a separate detail::k_vnni_access in its own header. [...] v_vnni.hpp and later k_vnni.hpp supply definitions."
- Evidence given in round-3: evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:369 ('This header contains the K mapping and storage operations without including attention kernels'); design-wdiff.txt segments @old 4908-4924 (OLD 'mapping ... storage operations without including attention kernels' -> NEW 'tensor header. h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp includes that header and retains software score arithmetic'), OLD segment at :1332. Child locations today: h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:139 scatter_row, :194 store_block, :221 gather_row, :248 qk_group (310 lines); h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:27 (#include "tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp"), :1705 set_k_row calls k_vnni::scatter_row, :1729 set_k_block calls k_vnni::store_block, :1741 get_k_row calls k_vnni::gather_row, :2111-2113 copy_storage_slot calls gather_row and scatter_row. design-r3.txt:95, :249, :258 and :372 (parent's v_vnni.hpp holds append, load, token copy), :284, :391, :392, :405-407, :416, :514 (section 9 'fix header ownership'). Round-2 N59 recorded the duplicated basename as acceptable; the unqualified reference at :284 is new in round 3.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): After 'and the existing constexpr free function k_vnni::index there.' (design-r3.txt:391) add: 'Also define the typed K bulk operations there: store_row and load_row (today's scatter_row and gather_row), store_block, the token copy, and the plane copy, together with detail::k_vnni_access, the K family's friend accessor. h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp keeps only qk_group. kv_cache.hpp then includes h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp and drops its include of h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp.' At :249 write 'in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp' instead of 'in its own header'. At :284 write 'h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp and, in the child, h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp supply definitions; h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp is the child's existing kernel header.' At :95 write 'Keep each family's access plumbing small'.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: drop "which the round-2 split was meant to end"; the round-2 aim was only to avoid a tensor/kernel include cycle, and kv_cache.hpp already includes a kernel header (expr.hpp). The defect is that round 2 gave the five typed K operations a header and round 3 took it away (design-wdiff.txt @old 4908-4924), while the S10 row still ties "vector operations" to h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp. If the operations move, the three detail helpers (pair_base, pair_row_offsets, transpose_16x16_epi32) move with them; qk_group uses only the constants.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three narrowings. (1) Drop the clause "which the round-2 split was meant to end". The round-2 text's stated aim was only "avoiding a tensor/kernel include cycle" [design-revised-text.txt:369], and kv_cache.hpp already includes a kernel header at main (tron/kernels/expr.hpp) [kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:26]. Replace it with: "which round-2 N58's corrected recommendation asked the design to state ('kv_cache.hpp includes only that header')". (2) In the recommendation, "h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp keeps only qk_group" is right for the child's functions, but add that the three detail helpers move with the storage operations: pair_base [k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:113, :119], pair_row_offsets [:126] and transpose_16x16_epi32 [:153] are used only by scatter_row [:140, :144], gather_row [:222, :225] and store_block [:204]; qk_group [:248-308] uses only the constants and intrinsics. (3) Weaken the detail::k_vnni_access part to a wording nit. The parallel V sentence says the V accessor is "a friend declared with the V types" [design-r3.txt:249], and section 9 says "one home per layout family" [design-r3.txt:529], so h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp is the inferable reading; one path word at :249 is still the fix. Keep the :95 point: "shared" reverses the cost statement that round 2 approved for Q1 ("duplicated conversion and access plumbing" [r2-findings.md:76]); write "Keep the duplicated access plumbing small" or "Keep each family's access plumbing small".
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow and strengthen the finding as follows.

1. Drop the clause "which the round-2 split was meant to end". The round-2 sentence said only that the tensor header would not include attention kernels [design-revised-text.txt child step 4]; it did not say kv_cache.hpp must stop including the kernel header. Round-2 N58 verifiers made the same correction. Replace with: "Line 392 says the cache includes the tensor header but not 'only', so keeping the kernel include stays legal under the text."

2. Add the strongest evidence: the design's own S10 row ties "vector operations" to h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp#L42-L310 [design-r3.txt:574; design-r3-links.tsv:172], and line 390 says "Port K mapping/vector operations" with no destination. Read literally, the design leaves the bulk operations in the kernel header, the opposite of the round-2 text.

3. Downgrade the :284 sub-claim to a wording nit. The sentence supplies definitions for the forward declarations in kv_cache_fwd.hpp; the kernel header defines no types, so an implementer would infer the tensor header. Keep the path fix.

4. For the :95 sub-claim, cite the word diff: round 2 said "duplicated conversion and access plumbing"; round 3 changed "duplicated" to "shared" [design-wdiff.txt:268-274]. The proposed wording "Keep each family's access plumbing small" is fine.

5. Extend the recommendation by one sentence. Line 249 lets detail::v_vnni_access be used "inside typed tensor operations and V-specific kernels", but for K it says only "in its own header". Since qk_group receives k_vnni_view by value [design-r3.txt:407], it needs the accessor to reach the plane. Add: "detail::k_vnni_access may be used only inside the typed K operations and the K-specific kernels qk_group and qk_vnni_128x4."

6. relates_to: N58 and M07 are the direct residuals (header placement of the operations and the accessor); N59 covers the basename. N03 (owner selection) is addressed at design-r3.txt:397-398 and is not part of this finding; drop it from relates_to or mark it as context only.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the claim and fix two clauses.

1. Delete "which the round-2 split was meant to end". Replace with: "The round-2 text's stated aim was only to avoid a tensor/kernel include cycle [design-revised-text.txt:369]. kv_cache.hpp already includes a kernel header at main (tron/kernels/expr.hpp) [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:26]. So keeping the kernel include is legal under the design. The defect is that the design gives the five typed K operations no citable header, after round 2 had given them one."

2. Replace "'The cache includes the K tensor header' is not sufficient" with: "'The cache includes the K tensor header' [design-r3.txt:392] is sufficient only if the typed K operations live in that header. The text should say so."

3. For :249, change the claim from ambiguity to wording: "'in its own header' is resolvable through section 9's 'one home per layout family' [design-r3.txt:529] and the K family's header at :391. Write the header name at :249 anyway."

4. For :95, add the evidence: round 2 wrote "duplicated conversion and access plumbing" [design-revised-text.txt:82]; round 3 changed it to "shared" [design-r3.txt:95], which contradicts :74 ("repeats a small amount of view plumbing") and :249 ("separate"). Either "duplicated" or "each family's" fixes it.

5. Add one supporting fact to the recommendation: "h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp keeps only qk_group" is consistent with the child source. qk_group [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:246-308] calls no detail:: helper. detail::pair_base, pair_row_offsets [:109-133] and transpose_16x16_epi32 [:148-180] serve scatter_row, gather_row and store_block and move with them. Optionally note that the child's comment block at kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:655-702 names tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp and the raw functions, so the port updates that comment.

Keep severity minor. Keep kind "ambiguity". Keep relates_to N58 N03 M07 N59.

### G19 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] for_each_active_slot visits logical slots, not physical; 'logical alias' is undefined and cannot occur in a fixed layout
- Where in the round-3 design: 6. Arena construction without clearing, heterogeneous bullet (design-r3.txt:350)
- Relates to earlier id(s): M04 N25
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: behavior-arena, disposition-checker
- Claim: The heterogeneous bullet says for_each_active_slot 'dispatches the physical slot to its compile-time geometry' and ends with a caution about a 'logical alias' that the design never defines. In the code, for_each_active_slot iterates slot indices into kv_slot_specs and keeps only slot < logical_slot_count(); its comment calls these logical slots; callers form the physical offset with kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does. A fixed (heterogeneous) layout maps each logical slot to exactly one physical offset: make_kv_slot_offsets computes one prefix per slot and storage_location reads offsets.values[offset.i]. The only aliasing mechanism in book is storage_view_offset_, set by set_storage_view (the EAGLE view shift), read only in the uniform accessor; EAGLE requires uniform geometry, so it never enters the heterogeneous enumeration. For logical slots the two numbers coincide, so the effect is unchanged, but the bullet opens with 'enumerate physical storage offsets', so a reader may look for a physical-offset loop the helper does not provide, or add a deduplication structure for a case that cannot occur. The alias sentence is carried over from round 2 and no earlier round raised it.
- The round-3 design text said: "Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which dispatches the physical slot to its compile-time geometry. ... Do not construct the same region again for a logical alias."
- Evidence given in round-3: kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1243-1249 (for_each_active_slot: if (slot < active_slots) function.template operator()<slot, geometry>()), :918-922 (comment 'logical K/V slots'), :1060-1061 (fill_random forms kv_storage_offset(slot)), :654-670 (make_kv_slot_offsets: per-slot prefix sums, one arena_offset per slot), :727-735 (storage_location: offsets.values[offset.i]; slot_specs[offset.i] at :732), :892-901 (set_storage_view writes storage_view_offset_), :1286-1294 (storage_view_offset_ used only inside the uniform kv_block accessor), :771 (eagle_compatible requires layout_t::uniform_geometry), :1158-1159 (the only extra physical slot, EAGLE, cannot exist in a heterogeneous layout). design-wdiff.txt:1071 ('again for a logical alias' is OLD context). grep of r1-findings.md and r2-findings.md for 'logical alias': 0 hits. design-r3.txt:348, :350, :521 (section 9 N25 M04 row), S16 links (design-r3-links.tsv:200-201).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the two sentences with: 'Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which calls the lambda once per active logical slot index with that slot's compile-time geometry (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1249). Form the physical offset as kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does (:1060-1061). A fixed layout maps each logical slot to one distinct physical region, so no deduplication is needed.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. This is the loose fact inside the M04 fix, so M04 is graded partly this round. Corrections: storage_view_offset_ is also read by refresh_kv_blocks_alias (kv_cache.hpp:1222-1227), not only by the uniform accessor; layer-to-slot sharing exists one level above slot indices, so say "no two slot indices map to one physical region" rather than "no aliasing". M04's corrected recommendation asked for "each active logical slot" and the design wrote "physical slot".
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Keep the finding as minor with four precision edits. (a) 'read only in the uniform accessor': storage_view_offset_ is also read in refresh_kv_blocks_alias (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1222-1227), which derives kv_blocks_alias for the uniform_reclaimability branch of the same accessor (:1284-1289); cite :1222-1227 and :1277-1300 together. The conclusion (only the requires(uniform_geometry) accessor is shifted; kv_block_at_storage<geometry> at :1309-1335 is not) stands. (b) 'its comment calls these logical slots': for_each_active_slot has no comment; the phrase 'logical K/V slots' is the logical_slot_count() comment (:918-919). Reword to 'the logical_slot_count() comment (:918-919) names them logical K/V slots'. (c) 'A fixed (heterogeneous) layout': heterogeneous_kv_cache is defined with fixed_kv_layout (heterogeneous_kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:36-37; allow_layer_prefix = false at kv_cache.hpp:745), so the wording is right; note that the one-slot-one-offset property holds for every kv_layout because make_kv_slot_offsets (:651-670) runs per slot regardless of allow_layer_prefix. (d) Add the one plausible intended meaning and dismiss it: Note [KV slot retention] (:113-115) says several operations can consume one slot, and the Words table (design-r3.txt:32) says a slot may be 'shared by several layers'; a per-slot loop visits such a slot once, so the caution is needless under that reading too. Recommendation addition: also define in the Words table 'logical slot = an index into slot_specs, 0 to logical_slot_count()-1 (kv_cache.hpp:918-922); storage offset = kv_storage_offset, equal to the logical index for logical slots, plus the EAGLE extra slot at logical_slot_count() (:906-907)', and delete the alias sentence or fold it into the next bullet (design-r3.txt:351), which already forbids reconstructing the retained EAGLE slot. Note for the section-9 row 'N25 M04' (design-r3.txt:521): the row says 'Use existing geometry dispatch and filter by arena'; the body adopted M04's helper name and filter but not the corrected recommendation's 'as fill_random does' and 'at slot_base(kv_storage_offset(slot))' clauses (r2-findings.md '### M04', design-text lens correction (d)), which is the gap this finding fills.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Three narrowings; the verdict and the recommended replacement text stand.

(a) Narrow "cannot occur in a fixed layout". Sharing does exist in fixed layouts, one level above slots: the design's own Words table says "A slot is a cache region used by one layer or shared by several layers" [design-r3.txt:32], and Note [KV slot retention] says "Several operations can consume one slot" [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:113-116]. That is a layer-to-slot or operation-to-slot mapping. for_each_active_slot iterates slot indices [:1243-1249], not layers or operations, so it visits each slot once. Restate the claim as: "no two slot indices of the enumeration map to one physical region, and layer-to-slot sharing happens above the slot index, so it cannot cause double construction in a slot-indexed loop."

(b) Fix the storage_view_offset_ citation. It is not "used only inside the uniform kv_block accessor". It is also read by refresh_kv_blocks_alias [:1224, :1227], which sets kv_blocks_alias, and kv_blocks_alias is read by the uniform_reclaimability branch of the uniform accessor [:1285-1288]. The substantive point survives: kv_block_at_storage [:1311-1336], the path the heterogeneous construction and fill_storage_slot [:1366-1373] use, calls slot_base(offset) directly and never consults storage_view_offset_.

(c) Add one fact to the evidence: the round-2 M04 recommendation (which section 9 row "N25 M04" [design-r3.txt:521] claims to have applied) said "for each active logical slot, as fill_random does"; the design's "dispatches the physical slot" is a departure from that corrected text, so this is a small slip introduced by the round-2 fix, not an error inherited unchanged.

Recommended text (unchanged in substance, one citation added): "Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which calls the lambda once per active logical slot index with that slot's compile-time geometry (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1249). Form the storage offset as kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does (:1060-1061). Filter on layout_t::storage_location(kv_storage_offset(slot), logical_slot_count()).reclaimable. A fixed layout maps each slot index to one distinct physical region (make_kv_slot_offsets :654-670), so no deduplication is needed." Delete "Do not construct the same region again for a logical alias." The EAGLE bullet [design-r3.txt:351] already carries the only real double-construction caution and needs no change.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Keep the claim and recommendation. Narrow two evidence citations: (a) the "logical K/V slots" comment is at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:917 with the function at :918-921, not :918-922; (b) storage_view_offset_ is not "read only in the uniform accessor": refresh_kv_blocks_alias also reads it [:1222-1227] to set kv_blocks_alias [:1128], which the uniform_reclaimability branch of the accessor then reads [:1285-1288]. The accessor itself carries requires(layout_t::uniform_geometry) [:1276-1277], so the substance (the EAGLE view shift touches only uniform-geometry access) stands. Add to the claim that M04's corrected recommendation asked for the words "each active logical slot" and the design substituted "physical slot", so this is a small deviation from the corrected M04 text, and that the same bullet group uses "physical KV slot" for the EAGLE slot [design-r3.txt:351], which is the code's meaning of physical (kv_storage_offset beyond the logical count). Recommended replacement text is acceptable as written; add one clause so the implementer keeps the arena filter and the EAGLE bullet unchanged: "Keep the storage_location(...).reclaimable filter and the following EAGLE bullet as they are."

### G21 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Adding reinterpret_cast to the rg completion search makes it unusable
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Implementation steps, caller search (design-r3.txt:380-382)
- Relates to earlier id(s): M02 M07
- Kind: process | found by lenses: regression-diff
- Claim: Round 3 added reinterpret_cast to the search pattern that the implementer runs over h, src and t before and after the accessor migration. The design says the second run 'checks for obsolete calls and comments'. At round-2 main that one alternative alone matches 979 lines in 74 files across the whole tree. The other alternatives match a few dozen lines. The check therefore stops being a completion test unless the implementer narrows it on their own.
- The round-3 design text said: "rg -n "v_base|v_data|set_v|get_v|append_v|scaled_v|fill_storage_slot|fill_random|reinterpret_cast|qk_rowmajor_128x4|weights_times_v_128x4" h src t"
- Evidence given in round-3: git grep -c reinterpret_cast 2880c3aa9b -- h src t, summed: 979 matching lines in 74 files. design-wdiff.txt: REPLACE @old 4685 / new 6118 added fill_storage_slot|fill_random|reinterpret_cast to the pattern (the round-2 pattern had none of the three). design-r3.txt:380 ('The second search checks for obsolete calls and comments').
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Keep fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern. Move reinterpret_cast to a second, scoped command and say what it is for: rg -n "reinterpret_cast" h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp -- after migration the only V-plane casts left must be inside the typed operations and the documented fill_storage_slot exception.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Measured at 2880c3aa9b over h, src and t: the round-2 pattern already matched 499 lines in 71 files through substring hits (get_vocab_size, set_valid); adding reinterpret_cast raises it to 1,491 lines in 124 files, 944 of them in 70 files the parent never touches. The search was a review list, not a zero-hit test, before and after; the defect is that the design gives no purpose or file scope for the added alternative.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow two statements and refine the recommendation. (a) Replace "The other alternatives match a few dozen lines" with measured numbers at 2880c3aa9b over h, src and t: without reinterpret_cast the pattern matches 514 lines in 74 files (get_v 136 lines, of which 122 are get_vocab_size, get_value, get_vocabulary_data and similar substrings, not get_v calls; set_v 112; v_base 82; v_data 79; scaled_v 70; fill_random 12; append_v 10; qk_rowmajor_128x4 9; weights_times_v_128x4 7; fill_storage_slot 3). With reinterpret_cast the whole pattern matches 1491 lines in 124 files. So reinterpret_cast supplies 979 of 1491 lines (66 percent) and adds 50 files that no other alternative touches, such as t/t_hwattention.cpp (17 lines) and t/t_mwaitx.cpp (12 lines). (b) Replace "stops being a completion test" with "was never a zero-hit test and now triples in size". The pattern keeps fill_random and fill_storage_slot, which the design says to preserve [design-r3.txt:262, 274], so a non-empty result is expected even after a correct migration. The defect is that the design does not say what the implementer should look for among the reinterpret_cast hits. (c) Refine the scoped command with the expected residue. At 2880c3aa9b, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp has 27 reinterpret_cast lines. 17 touch the V plane and should disappear or move into typed operations: 1371 (fill_storage_slot, kept as the documented exception), 1681, 1689, 1706, 1716 (v_data forms), 1878, 1881, 1950, 1953 (V token copies), 2239 (v_base), 2296, 2335, 2376, 2414, 2448 (set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v), 2455, 2463 (scaled_v stores). 10 are K-plane, block-array or EAGLE casts that stay: 1288, 1297, 1333 (kv_block_t arrays), 1749, 1756 (EAGLE x), 2017, 2086, 2101 (bf16x32 K reads), 2211, 2218 (K views). The 5 casts in h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp (594, 598, 655, 656, 869), the 1 in h/tron/models/model.hpp (1185) and the 2 in t/t_llama_unit.cpp (2363, 2382) are query, hardware or device casts, not V-plane casts. So the design should say: "rg -n reinterpret_cast h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp; after migration the only V-plane casts left are inside the typed operations and the fill_storage_slot exception; the K-plane, block-array and EAGLE casts remain." (d) Note on the recommendation's "Keep fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern": the round-2 design-text-lens correction to M02 said not to add those names to the pattern [r2-findings.md, M02 design-text-lens correction]. Either choice costs at most 15 lines of output, so this does not affect the verdict, but the finding should not present keeping them as required.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim to: "Round 3 added reinterpret_cast to the search pattern without a purpose or a file scope. At 2880c3aa9b that alternative adds 979 lines in 74 files (h, src, t). The rest of the pattern already matched 514 lines in 74 files through substring hits such as get_vocab_size and set_valid. With reinterpret_cast the output is 1491 lines in 124 files, and 944 of the added lines are in 70 files the parent does not change. The design never says what the reinterpret_cast hits are for, and its definition-of-done rule about packed physical indexing [design-r3.txt:495] names no command that checks it." Drop the phrases "The other alternatives match a few dozen lines" and "stops being a completion test": the search was a review list, not a zero-hit test, before and after the round-3 edit [design-r3.txt:380; expected surviving names at :270-271, :274-276, :278]. Keep the recommendation to leave fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern. Reword the second command so it covers every parent file that holds a cast today and states the expected survivors: `rg -n reinterpret_cast h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp h/tron/models/model.hpp t/t_llama_unit.cpp` with the sentence "After migration the only V-plane casts must be inside v_vnni.hpp's typed operations and in book::fill_storage_slot (the documented exception at design-r3.txt:262). Casts of the K plane, of the block array on allocation/restore, and the non-cache casts in self_attention.hpp:594-656, model.hpp:1185 and t_llama_unit.cpp:2363/2382 (all at 2880c3aa9b) are expected to remain." The finding's two-file scope (kv_cache.hpp + v_vnni.hpp) is sufficient for V-plane casts at 2880c3aa9b, because none of the 8 casts in the other parent files touches V (self_attention.hpp:594/598/655/656 wrap Q rows and activations, :869 an fp32 hardware head, model.hpp:1185 a uint16 view of an array, t_llama_unit.cpp:2363/2382 a PosDevice pointer); naming the parent's files anyway keeps the command valid if step 4 moves a V cast into the kernel call sites.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow three parts. (1) Replace "The other alternatives match a few dozen lines" with measured numbers: at 2880c3aa9b the round-2 pattern already matched 499 lines in 71 files, because several names are substrings (get_v 136 lines, 22 whole-word; set_v 112/38; v_data 79/23; v_base 82/61; scaled_v 70/40); the round-3 pattern matches 1491 lines in 124 files, so reinterpret_cast triples the lines and adds 53 files. (2) Replace "stops being a completion test" with "adds hits the design gives no criterion for": the second search was never a zero-hit test, because the design keeps the names set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v, fill_random and fill_storage_slot [design-r3.txt:269-274] and removes only v_data and v_base [design-r3.txt:272]. (3) Fix the recommendation's completion criterion. "The only V-plane casts left must be inside the typed operations and the fill_storage_slot exception" is too strict: the design keeps the 8-lane page::v views (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1681, 1689, 1706, 1716, inside the #else at :1674-1725) and the 8-lane copy_storage_slot V memcpy (:1950, :1953, inside the #else at :1947-1956) [design-r3.txt:271, :273], plus the K-plane k_view casts (:2211, :2218), the EAGLE x() casts (:1749, :1756) and the block-array casts (:1288, :1297, :1333). The casts that must leave kv_cache.hpp are the 16-lane ones: append_v pair copy (:1878, :1881), scaled_v_expr packed reads (:2017, :2086, :2101), packed set_v/get_v bodies (:2296, :2335, :2376, :2414, :2448, :2455, :2463) and v_base (:2239). Suggested design text after design-r3.txt:382: "Then run rg -n reinterpret_cast over the parent's step files (h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp, model.hpp, self_attention.hpp, h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp, h/tron/tensor/v_vnni.hpp, the four tests). After migration, no cast of a 16-lane packed V plane may remain outside v_vnni.hpp except fill_storage_slot's whole-block cast (kv_cache.hpp:1371). The 8-lane page::v, v_ptr and copy_storage_slot casts, and the K, EAGLE and block-array casts, stay." Keep fill_storage_slot|fill_random in the main pattern only if the design wants them there; note that the M02 design-text-lens correction said not to add them, which is a M02 grading matter, not this finding.

### G22 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] The t_amx_numerics PV case cannot keep its hand-packed std::vector V page; no permitted typed route is named
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Implementation steps, step 6 (design-r3.txt:377); 5. Interface design, view rules (:248-249) and kernel row (:275, :277); 6. Arena construction (:352)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N22 N15
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: cpp-interface, handoff
- Claim: The PV numerics case builds its V page by hand in a std::vector<tron::bf16> with the interleave formula and passes v_page.data() to weights_times_v_128x4. With the typed signature (:277) it must pass a v_vnni_view<const bf16, 64, 128>. The design forbids a public raw-pointer constructor (:248), forbids tests from using detail::v_vnni_access (:249), and forbids casting existing storage into the owner (:352). Step 6 says only 'explicit aligned row wrappers', which fits the K array (an explicit const_native_k_view wrap is legal per :267) but not V. The implementing agent at t_amx_numerics.cpp:226 cannot tell which permitted route to take (an owner filled by 64 append_v_row calls, an owner filled by element writes, or a memcpy of the hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner) or what to do with the test's own interleave formula, which then duplicates production layout knowledge. Round-2 N22 covered only the K array of this test.
- The round-3 design text said: "| 6. Existing tests and fixtures | t/t_llama_unit.cpp , t/t_amx_numerics.cpp , t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp , t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp | Update real/fake signatures, explicit aligned row wrappers, and the typed-access concept probe. Add alignment to the heterogeneous V fixture. Extend existing cases only for uncovered contracts."
- Evidence given in round-3: t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:198-211 (std::vector<tron::bf16> v_page(PAGE_TOKENS_64 * HEAD_SIZE_128) filled as v_page[(pair * HEAD_SIZE_128 + d) * 2 + j]), :226-227 (weights_times_v_128x4(v_page.data(), &exp_rows[0][0], PAGE_TOKENS_64, o_amx)), :236-239 (reference reads v_page by the same formula), :66 and :158-159 (K array in->k, whose explicit wrap design-r3.txt:267 keeps legal). design-r3.txt:248, :249, :252, :267, :277, :352, :377, :416 (the child's t_k_vnni_layout gets owner instructions: fill with typed row saves, copy the owner's bytes to a test buffer).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add to step 6, or to the 'AMX kernels and test substitutes' row: 'The PV case of t/t_amx_numerics.cpp (:198-227) cannot keep its hand-packed std::vector<bf16> page: the typed view has no pointer route and tests do not use detail::v_vnni_access. Hold the page in a v_vnni_tensor<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> (16,384 bytes; heap-allocated or an aligned local), fill it with 64 append_v_row calls from 64-byte-aligned bf16 rows (the bf16 input path keeps exact bits), pass owner.as_view() to the kernel, and keep the logical V array as the reference. Keep the independent pair-formula check only by copying the owner's bytes into a plain bf16 buffer, as section 7 prescribes for t_k_vnni_layout. Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner. The QK case keeps its raw K array through an explicit const_native_k_view<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> wrap.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 2 of 3. The design-text verifier refuted the second half: a permitted route is derivable from the fixture rule at :317 (direct element writes) plus owner.as_view(). The source and intent verifiers hold because the design never says that the PV case's std::vector page itself must move into an owner, which fill route to use, or what happens to the test's own interleave formula. Two judges list this among the items to settle before implementation. Corrections: :352 is an arena rule, not a test rule; a memcpy of hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner is legal C++, so the design should state whether tests may do it rather than the finding forbidding it.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three narrowings. (a) Reword the reading of step 6: "explicit aligned row wrappers" [design-r3.txt:377] is not K-only. "Explicit ... wrap" is the N22 language for the K plane, and "aligned row wrappers" is the N15 language for append_v_row source rows. The phrase therefore hints at the append_v_row route for V, but step 6 never says that the PV case must replace its hand-packed std::vector page with an owner, which fill route to use, or what happens to the test's own interleave formula and its pair-formula reference. Replace "which fits the K array ... but not V" with "which names a K plane wrap and V source-row wraps but does not say that the PV case's std::vector page itself must move into an owner". (b) Soften the :352 citation: "Do not construct in an accessor or cast an existing SIMD array object into the new owner" [:352] is an arena-construction rule about kv_block's SIMD array; a memcpy of bytes into a trivially copyable owner is not a cast, so the design text leaves the memcpy route unaddressed rather than forbidden. The finding's advice "Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner" stands as a recommendation, not as an existing design rule. (c) Fix two line ranges: the hand-packed fill starts at the comment on :197 (cite :197-211, not :198-211), and the reference read is :238-239 (not :236-239). Optional addition to the recommendation: the reference loop can read the logical float array v through bf16_rne (vv = bf16_to_float(bf16_rne(v[t * HEAD_SIZE_128 + d]))), which needs no layout formula at all; the byte-copy comparison is then the only place the pair formula survives.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow to a note on step 6 [design-r3.txt:377]: "The PV case of t/t_amx_numerics.cpp [2880c3aa9b:203-227] replaces its std::vector<bf16> page with a v_vnni_tensor<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> owner (16,384 bytes, 64-byte aligned, default-constructed). Fill it through the public typed operations, either element writes [design-r3.txt:317] or append_v_row from 64-byte-aligned bf16 rows [design-r3.txt:252-257], and pass owner.as_view() to weights_times_v_128x4. Keep the case's independent physical-layout formula [2880c3aa9b:207, :239] as a check by copying the owner's bytes to a test buffer, as section 7 prescribes for t_k_vnni_layout [design-r3.txt:416]. The QK case keeps its raw K array through an explicit const_native_k_view wrap [design-r3.txt:267]." Drop the citation of design-r3.txt:352 (an arena rule, not a test rule) and drop "Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner" (not a design rule; the design is silent on it). Relates to N22 (K wrap) and N15 (aligned rows); neither asked for this V-test text.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Two narrowings and one softening. (1) The clause "forbids casting existing storage into the owner (:352)" over-reads the text. design-r3.txt:352 says "Do not construct in an accessor or cast an existing SIMD array object into the new owner" and sits in the arena-construction section; it addresses the kv_block SIMD array, not test-owned storage. Reword to: "the design states no rule for test-owned storage; :352 covers only the cache's existing SIMD array." (2) In the recommendation, replace "Do not memcpy the hand-packed buffer into the owner" with "State whether a memcpy of hand-packed bytes into the trivially copyable owner is allowed in tests. Prefer the append_v_row route, which keeps the pair formula inside the type, and keep any independent formula check on bytes copied OUT of the owner, as :416 prescribes for t_k_vnni_layout." A memcpy into a trivially copyable owner is legal C++, so the design should choose, not the finder. (3) Keep the rest: the evidence lines are right (v_page fill at t/t_amx_numerics.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203-211, kernel call :226-227, reference read :239), the owner size 64 x 128 x 2 bytes = 16,384 bytes is right, and an alignas(64) owner works as a local or through std::make_unique (C++17 aligned new). Add to the finding that t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp's fakes (@2880c3aa9b:180-185) need only the signature change, so the PV case is the single test that needs a named V input route.

### G23 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Worker-index step under-counts the LoopyTronSpec assertions that change
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Worker-index request (design-r3.txt:424); 10. S18 link [Generated-call expectations] (:582)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N33
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: child-needs
- Claim: The design tells the implementer to update 'both exact-call expectations' in ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs. The child spec has two test cases that assert the generated save calls, and each case holds four assertions that change when save_k_helper is merged into save_k. The count of 'outer_state.template save_k<' is asserted to be 1 in both cases; after the merge run() and run_main_help both emit that text, so the count becomes 2. The count of 'outer_state.template save_k_helper<' is asserted to be 1 in both cases and becomes 0. The run() call text ',k,n_workers);' becomes ',k,0,n_workers);'. Only the two exact-call strings are named by the design, and the S18 link covers only the first case (lines 2808-2818). An implementer who follows the sentence literally leaves six failing assertions.
- The round-3 design text said: "Generated run() passes 0, n_workers. Generated run_main_help passes worker_ix, n_workers. Update both exact-call expectations in ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs and direct three-thread calls in t/t_llama_unit.cpp. Search all old positional calls, which passed only n_workers. Run bin/ingest-cabal test."
- Evidence given in round-3: ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs@30c4ac82cb:2801 (Txt.count "outer_state.template save_k<" cpp `shouldBe` 1); :2808 (run() call text ending q_batch->outer_batch,k,n_workers);); :2812 (Txt.count "outer_state.template save_k_helper<" cpp `shouldBe` 1); :2813-2816 (helper call text ending k,worker_ix,n_workers);). Second case: :3225 (count of save_k< = 1); :3227 (",k,n_workers);" isInfixOf); :3231 (count of save_k_helper< = 1); :3232 (",k,worker_ix,n_workers);" isInfixOf). Generator sites: ingest/src/TronCpp.hs@30c4ac82cb:2338 ("save_k_helper") and :2406-2408 ("save_k" with ExprLit "n_workers"). design-r3-links.tsv line 225 (S18 link -> LoopyTronSpec.hs#L2808-L2818 only).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace 'Update both exact-call expectations in ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs' with: 'Update every save_k assertion in ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs. Two test cases assert the generated calls (lines 2801-2818 and 3225-3232 at 30c4ac82cb). In each case: the count of outer_state.template save_k< becomes 2, because run() and run_main_help both emit it; delete the save_k_helper< count line; the run() call text ends ,k,0,n_workers);; the helper call text ends ,k,worker_ix,n_workers);.' Extend the S18 link [Generated-call expectations] to both cases (#L2801-L2818 and #L3225-L3232).
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3 (one at note). Corrected counts: seven assertions change (case 1: :2801, :2808, :2812, :2813-2816; case 2: :3225, :3227, :3231); the :3232 fragment stays true; the design names two. bin/ingest-cabal test and the required Test ingest job catch the leftovers loudly, which is why this is minor.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow two counts. (1) "each case holds four assertions that change" is right for case 1 (:2801, :2808, :2812, :2813-2816) but case 2 has three, not four: :3232 `",k,worker_ix,n_workers);" isInfixOf` stays true after the merge because the merged helper call in run_main_help still ends `,k,worker_ix,n_workers);` under the design's "save_k(..., worker_ix = 0, n_workers = 1)" shape [design-r3.txt:420, :424]. Only :3225 (count 1 -> 2), :3227 (`",k,n_workers);"` no longer appears; the run() text becomes `,k,0,n_workers);`) and :3231 (count 1 -> 0) change in case 2. (2) "leaves six failing assertions" should read "leaves five": seven assertions change in total, and the design's "both exact-call expectations" names two of them. Recommendation wording: in the replacement sentence, say for case 2 "the helper fragment at :3232 needs no change", and keep the S18 link extension to #L2801-L2818 and #L3225-L3232. Also note that the comment lines :2802-2807 and :3228-3230 mention save_k_helper and should be reworded, but they are comments, not assertions.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow two counts. (1) Case 2 has three changing assertions, not four: the assertion ",k,worker_ix,n_workers);" [ingest/test/LoopyTronSpec.hs@30c4ac82cb:3232] stays true after the merge, because run_main_help's call through save_k keeps the tail ",k,worker_ix,n_workers);". The S18 link range L2808-L2818 already contains :2812, so a reader who follows the link and fixes the two exact-call strings leaves 4 failing assertions (:2801, :3225, :3227, :3231), or 5 if :2812 is overlooked. (3) Add to the finding that the design's "Run bin/ingest-cabal test" sentence [design-r3.txt:424] is a working safety net: the leftover assertions fail loudly in the required "Test ingest" check (F29 verifier, r2-findings.md N33), which is why the severity is minor and not major. ### G24 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Child port says nothing about its test-cost records
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:386-395); 8. Child only row (:443); 8. Commands and execution hosts (:478)
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: child-needs
- Claim: The child diff adds a config/test-benchmarks.json record for t_k_vnni_layout (only in the granite_rapids_6962p block) and changes the t_llama_unit record of that block from 3.38 s to 13.436 s at one slice. The repository test skill requires bin/slice bench --new or --update <name> for new or materially changed tests. The design's adaptation of t_k_vnni_layout to typed owners and of the save_k case to the merged save_k materially changes both tests. Section 8 states the bench rule and its authorization for the parent's t_llama_unit only. The child section does not say whether the child's records are carried over, re-measured, or which platform block is affected.
- The round-3 design text said: "On delphi-3bda, bin/slice bench --update t_llama_unit refreshes the granite_rapids_6962p platform block only. Name the other platform keys, genoa96, genoa32, and granite_rapids_6960p, as unrefreshed. Bench requests the whole host and measures one, two, and four slices, so it requires user direction."
- Evidence given in round-3: git diff c7844ca2ce 30c4ac82cb -- config/test-benchmarks.json: t_llama_unit times {"1":3.38,...} -> {"1":13.436,"2":13.388,"4":13.618}, max_rss_mb 329 -> 643; new row {"name":"t_k_vnni_layout",...,"times":{"1":0.709,"2":0.697,"4":0.696}}. config/test-benchmarks.json@30c4ac82cb:365 (granite_rapids_6962p block holds both changed rows: :426 t_llama_unit, :480 t_k_vnni_layout); the other three blocks (:3 genoa96, :153 genoa32, :260 granite_rapids_6960p) have no t_k_vnni_layout row. .agents/skills/run-tron-tests/SKILL.md@2880c3aa9b:69-70 ('New or materially changed tests require bin/slice bench --new or bin/slice bench --update <name> after the test passes'). design-r3.txt:416 and :424.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add a bullet after 'Implement the worker-index change below.': 'Cost records: carry the child's config/test-benchmarks.json rows for t_k_vnni_layout and t_llama_unit into the port. After the typed-owner adaptation, refresh both with bin/slice bench --update t_k_vnni_layout and --update t_llama_unit on delphi-3bda under the section 8 authorization rule. Only the granite_rapids_6962p block is refreshed; the other three platform keys stay unrefreshed. Do not insert estimated durations.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: section 8 states the refresh rule generically at :478 and names only the parent's t_llama_unit; the child section names none of its three changed rows (t_llama_unit, t_amx_numerics, t_k_vnni_layout, all in the granite_rapids_6962p block). Consequence the verifiers added: the required Build Tron job runs bin/slice bench --check, which fails when a registered test has no record, so a port that drops the t_k_vnni_layout row fails the child's CI.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three narrowings and one addition.

1. Narrow the claim "Section 8 states the bench rule and its authorization for the parent's t_llama_unit only." The first sentence of design-r3.txt:478, "Refresh changed test-cost records only through authorized measurement," is generic and covers any changed record. Only the named example is t_llama_unit. Reword to: "Section 8 states the bench rule generically and names only the parent's t_llama_unit record (design-r3.txt:478). Neither the child section (:384-427) nor the Child only row (:443) names the child's two records."

2. Add the concrete consequence. "Main's granite_rapids_6962p t_llama_unit row still reads 3.38/3.38/3.64 s [config/test-benchmarks.json@1400fa4481:421]. The parent's refresh (design-r3.txt:478) rewrites that row. The child's diff rewrites the same row to 13.436/13.388/13.618 s [config/test-benchmarks.json@30c4ac82cb:426]. The port therefore conflicts on that row. The conflict bullet at design-r3.txt:389 names only common.hpp and self_attention.hpp." Recommend adding config/test-benchmarks.json to the named conflict files at :389, with the rule "resolve by re-measuring on the ported child, never by keeping either old number."

3. Fix the flag detail in the recommendation. bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:6444-6455 defines `--update TEST...` as "bench only these tests; merge into existing JSON" and `--new` as "bench only tests in the ctest manifest absent from the JSON". So the recommended text should say: "If the child's t_k_vnni_layout row is carried into the port, use `bin/slice bench --update t_k_vnni_layout t_llama_unit` (one call; `--update` takes several names). If the port drops that row, `bin/slice bench --new` measures it as a test absent from the JSON."

4. Complete the evidence. The child's diff also re-measures the t_amx_numerics row in the granite_rapids_6962p block (0.737/0.758/0.788 s to 0.804/0.745/0.728 s). State that this row is carried over as-is and needs no refresh unless the port materially changes t_amx_numerics.

Suggested replacement bullet for design-r3.txt after :394: "Cost records: config/test-benchmarks.json is a shared file at port time. Carry the child's granite_rapids_6962p rows for t_k_vnni_layout and t_amx_numerics into the port. The child's t_llama_unit row conflicts with the parent's refreshed row; resolve it by re-measurement, not by keeping either number. After the typed-owner adaptation, on delphi-3bda under the section 8 authorization rule, run bin/slice bench --update t_k_vnni_layout t_llama_unit (or --new if the t_k_vnni_layout row was not carried). Only the granite_rapids_6962p block is refreshed; genoa96, genoa32 and granite_rapids_6960p stay unrefreshed. Do not insert estimated durations."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim. Drop "does not say whether the child's records are ... re-measured, or which platform block is affected": design-r3.txt:478 states both generically for any changed record, and :386 implies carry-over. Keep: the child's two records (t_k_vnni_layout and the child's own t_llama_unit change) are not named, and the stale-row question is not addressed. Fix the evidence wording "from 3.38 s to 13.436 s at one slice" to "at all three slice widths (3.38, 3.38 and 3.64 s to 13.436, 13.388 and 13.618 s)". Optionally add that the child also changes the t_amx_numerics row (0.737 s to 0.804 s at 1 slice) [config/test-benchmarks.json@30c4ac82cb:478]. Recommend one clause, either appended to :478 or as a bullet after :394: "The same rule applies to the child. After the port, run bin/slice bench --update t_k_vnni_layout and bin/slice bench --update t_llama_unit on delphi-3bda under the same authorization. The child's carried granite_rapids_6962p rows (t_k_vnni_layout 0.709 s, t_llama_unit 13.436 s at 1 slice) are measurements of the old child head and will conflict with the parent's refreshed t_llama_unit row. Resolve by re-measuring, not by keeping either old value." Citations: config/test-benchmarks.json@30c4ac82cb:426,480; config/test-benchmarks.json@1400fa4481:421; SKILL.md@2880c3aa9b:69-70; design-r3.txt:386, :389, :474, :478.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the claim: section 8 line 478 states the refresh rule generally ("Refresh changed test-cost records only through authorized measurement") and names t_llama_unit as the example; it does not restrict the rule to the parent. The omission is that the child section (design-r3.txt:386-395, 416) does not name config/test-benchmarks.json among the child's focused commits or say that its three changed rows (t_llama_unit, t_amx_numerics, t_k_vnni_layout; all in the granite_rapids_6962p block) are carried and then refreshed. Add the consequence: the required Build Tron job's bin/slice bench --check [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:483-485] fails when a registered test has no record in any platform block [bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:1815-1820], so a port that drops the t_k_vnni_layout row fails the child's CI. Reword the recommended bullet, placed after "Implement the worker-index change below." (design-r3.txt:394): "Cost records: carry the child's config/test-benchmarks.json rows (t_llama_unit, t_amx_numerics, t_k_vnni_layout; granite_rapids_6962p block only) in the same focused commit as the tests they describe; a registered test with no row in any block fails Build Tron's bin/slice bench --check. The t_llama_unit row conflicts with the parent's refreshed row at rebase; resolve it by re-measurement, not by keeping either number. After the typed-owner and merged save_k adaptation, refresh the child's rows under the section 8 rule (delphi-3bda, --update <name>, user direction, no estimated durations)." ### G25 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Successful Slice case logs: location, runner, 10-run pruning and fallback are not stated
- Where in the round-3 design: 8. CI ownership and execution evidence (design-r3.txt:469-470); Definition of done (:498)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N36
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: process-ci, handoff
- Claim: The design makes the per-case Catch2 starting and Catch2 passed lines from the Slice logs-*/run-* files the proof that the required Test host job executed the new cases, and says successful logs are not uploaded and must be preserved or linked explicitly. It does not say where those files are (REPO_ROOT/logs-<runner hostname>/run-NNNN/<test>.log on the self-hosted runner), that the GitHub job log for a passing run carries no per-case output (only a final 'Logs: <run_dir>' line), that Slice deletes the oldest run directory once 10 run directories exist, how to identify the runner, where the test-runtime and environment store paths it asks to record can be read, or what to do when the runner is unreachable. An implementing agent who looks for the evidence after a few more CI runs finds the directory gone and has only the green check, the false-evidence case N36 warned about.
- The round-3 design text said: "Record the required GCP Nix Test host run URL and attempt, source key, test-runtime and environment store paths, case names, and selected command/filter. Preserve the existing Catch2 starting and Catch2 passed lines from the corresponding Slice logs-*/run-* files. These prove which cases executed. Successful logs are not automatically uploaded: the reviewed upload step runs only on failure. Preserve or link successful runner logs explicitly. [S19]"
- Evidence given in round-3: nix/tron-test-environment.nix@2880c3aa9b:31-35 and .github/workflows/gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:571-573 (Test host runs tron-test-host = exec bin/slice run --filter=host). bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:81 (LOGS_DIR = REPO_ROOT / f"logs-{HOSTNAME}"), :2770 (run_dir = LOGS_DIR / f"run-{next_num:04d}"), :3172-3187 (log_f = open(log_path, "w") ... subprocess.Popen(cmd, ..., stdout=log_f, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)), :114 (RETENTION = 10), :2766-2768 (allocate_run_dir deletes the oldest run directories while 10 or more exist), :4181 (success console shows only 'Logs: <run_dir>'), :2992-3016 (emit_failures echoes tails only for failed tests). gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:581-588 ('Upload Slice test logs', if: failure() && env.RUN_TESTS == 'true', path logs-*/run-*), :535 (runner labels [self-hosted, fpga, test, avx512]), :492-500 and :549-550 (store paths are job outputs of the Build Tron handoff step). t/testlog.cpp@2880c3aa9b:17-25 (per-section Catch2 lines). .agents/skills/run-tron-tests/SKILL.md@2880c3aa9b:78 (names only the local logs-$(hostname)/latest/ location); README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b has no log-retrieval instruction (grep 'logs-': no match). design-r3.txt:469, :498.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the last two sentences of the bullet with: 'A successful run uploads nothing and its GitHub job log shows only Slice's progress and a final line Logs: <checkout>/logs-<runner hostname>/run-NNNN. The per-case Catch2 lines exist only in that directory on the self-hosted runner (labels self-hosted, fpga, test, avx512; bin/slice:81, 2770, 3172). Slice deletes the oldest run directory once 10 exist, so read the runner name from the job page and copy the t_llama_unit log and the tron log files under that run directory off the runner right after the run, then attach or link them in the PR. If the runner is not reachable, record the job URL and attempt number and add a local run of the same test binary and filter with its Catch2 lines, labeled local execution, not CI execution. Read the test-runtime and environment store paths from the Build Tron job's step Select test reuse and publish handoff, which exports them as job outputs. Making the workflow upload on success is a separate CI decision for the user and CI owners.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: in CI the binding limit is not Slice's 10-run pruning but actions/checkout's default clean, which deletes the untracked logs-<hostname>/ directory at the next job on that runner workspace; the job log shows the Logs: line and one status line per test binary, but no Catch2 case lines. Whether the runners are ephemeral is Insufficient data.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow the claim in three places and fix two citations. (a) Replace "the GitHub job log for a passing run carries no per-case output (only a final 'Logs: <run_dir>' line)" with: "the GitHub job log for a passing run carries no per-case output: it shows the 'Logs: <run_dir>' line printed before the tests start [bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:4180-4181], one status line per test with the test name, instance and duration but no case names [:2924, plain print when stderr is not a TTY :6168, :6188-6191], and a final passed/skipped/failed count [:4545-4550]; log tails and case counts are echoed only for failed tests [:2929-2934, :2992-3016]." (b) Replace "Slice deletes the oldest run directory once 10 run directories exist" with: "the run directory lives under the job workspace [upload path ${{ github.workspace }}/logs-*/run-*, gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:587] and is deleted by the next job that checks out on that runner, because the workflow's actions/checkout@de0fac2e step [gcp-nix.yml:540-542] keeps the action default clean: true (git clean -ffdx && git reset --hard HEAD; action.yml lines 57-59 at that commit) and logs-*/ is gitignored [.gitignore@2880c3aa9b:68]; Slice also keeps at most 10 run directories [bin/slice:114, 2766-2768; doc/slice.md@2880c3aa9b:246]". (c) Cite log_f = open(log_path, "w") as bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:3174 (Popen with stdout=log_f, stderr=STDOUT at :3181-3187), and add the job-outputs declaration gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:446-448 (test_runtime_store_path, test_environment_store_path from steps.handoff) next to :492-500 and :549-550. Reworded recommendation for the design bullet: "A successful Test host job uploads nothing. Its job log shows Slice's line Logs: <workspace>/logs-<runner hostname>/run-NNNN (printed before the tests run), one status line per test without case names, and a final count line. The per-case Catch2 starting and Catch2 passed lines exist only in <run dir>/t_llama_unit.log and <run dir>/t_heterogeneous_scheduler.log on the self-hosted runner (labels self-hosted, fpga, test, avx512). That directory is removed by the next job's checkout on the same runner (git clean -ffdx; logs-*/ is gitignored), and Slice keeps at most 10 run directories. Copy those log files off the runner right after the run and attach or link them in the PR; the runner name is in the job's Set up job step. If the runner is not reachable, record the job URL and attempt number and add a local run of the same test binary and filter with its Catch2 lines, labeled local execution, not CI execution. Read the test-runtime and environment store paths from the Build Tron job outputs, exported by the step Select test reuse and publish handoff (gcp-nix.yml:446-448, 492-500). Making the workflow upload logs on success is a separate CI decision for the user and CI owners."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim and fix two facts in the recommendation.

Claim, narrowed: "The design [design-r3.txt:469] requires the per-case Catch2 lines from the Slice logs-*/run-* files as case-execution evidence and says to preserve or link successful runner logs, but it does not say where those files are, that they exist only on the self-hosted runner, how short their life is, how to identify the runner, where the store paths it asks to record are printed, or what to do when the runner is unreachable." Delete "(only a final 'Logs: <run_dir>' line)": the job log prints the Logs: line before the tests and then one result line per test binary, for example "passed t_llama_unit inst 1,8 4.3s" [bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:2924, :4181; doc/slice.md@2880c3aa9b:121-127; live job 105939764467 log lines 313, 344, 369]. Say instead: "the job log proves the test binary ran and passed, but carries no Catch2 case lines (0 in two executed jobs)."

Recommendation, replacement text for the last two sentences of the bullet at design-r3.txt:469: "A successful run uploads no logs. Its job log shows one result line per test binary (for example: passed t_llama_unit inst 1,8 4.3s) and the line Logs: <checkout>/logs-<machine hostname>/run-NNNN, printed before the tests. The per-case Catch2 lines exist only in <that run directory>/t_llama_unit.log and t_heterogeneous_scheduler.log on the self-hosted runner [bin/slice:81, 3148, 3179]. The runner is any host labeled self-hosted, fpga, test, avx512, not necessarily delphi-3bda; the job log's first lines give 'Runner name' and 'Machine name' (observed: andoria-09-0/andoria-09, delphi-3af6-0/delphi-3af6). The checkout of the next job on that runner runs git clean -ffdx, which deletes the untracked logs-<host>/ directory, and Slice also keeps at most 10 run directories [bin/slice:114, 2766-2768]. Copy the two log files off the runner right after the run and attach or link them in the PR. If the runner is not reachable, record the job URL, attempt number and the per-test result lines, and add a local run of the same test binary and filter with its Catch2 lines, labeled local execution, not CI execution. Read the test-runtime and environment store paths from the Test host job's step Restore exact Nix test runtime, which prints 'Preparing test runtime: <path>; environment=<path>' [test_runtime.py:133-136]. Making the workflow upload on success is a separate CI decision for the user and CI owners."

Drop from the original recommendation the pointer to the Build Tron job's handoff step outputs; job outputs are not printed in a log, and the Test host job prints the same two paths itself.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): 1. Loss mechanism: in CI the binding limit is not Slice's 10-run pruning. Every job on the fpga test runners uses actions/checkout@v6 with no `clean:` input [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:444-457, :535-542, :602-607]. That action's `clean` input defaults to true and runs `git clean -ffdx && git reset --hard HEAD` before fetching [actions/checkout action.yml at de0fac2e4500dabe0009e67214ff5f5447ce83dd, lines 57-59, read via gh api]. `git clean -ffdx` removes untracked and ignored directories, so logs-<hostname>/ under the checkout is deleted by the next job that checks out in the same runner workspace (Wait for lab cache, Test host, or Test FPGA on that runner). Reword: "the directory survives only until the next job checks out in that runner workspace; Slice's own 10-run retention [bin/slice:114, 2766-2768] applies to local checkouts." Insufficient data: whether the runners are configured ephemeral cannot be read from the repository; either way the window is at most one job. 2. Job-log content: a passing run's GitHub job log shows the "Logs: <run_dir>" line [bin/slice:4181] and a final "N passed, M skipped, 0 failed, makespan" count line [:4546-4549]; it shows no test names and no per-case Catch2 lines. Replace "only a final Logs line" with that. 3. Store paths: they are readable closer than the Build Tron job. The Test host job's step "Restore exact Nix test runtime" carries TRON_BUILD_STORE_PATH and TEST_ENVIRONMENT_STORE_PATH as step env from needs.tron-build.outputs [gcp-nix.yml:549-556], which the job log prints in the step header, and the uploaded artifact tron-test-runtime (test-runtime.json) carries the receipt [:508-514]. 4. Soften "the false-evidence case N36 warned about": the N36 verifier correction established that a green Test host with an executed (not skipped) Test host step is execution evidence at the test-binary level; what is missing without the Slice log is only the case-level proof the design itself demands. 5. Shorter recommended design text for design-r3.txt:469 (replace the last two sentences): "A successful run uploads nothing. Its job log shows Slice's Logs: <checkout>/logs-<runner hostname>/run-NNNN line and a pass count, no case names. The per-case Catch2 lines exist only in that directory on the self-hosted runner, and the next job's checkout removes it. Read the runner name from the job's Set up job step, copy the t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler logs from that run directory right after the run, and attach or link them in the PR. If the runner is not reachable, record the run URL, attempt number, the executed Test host step, and the store paths from the Restore exact Nix test runtime step, then add a local run of the same binaries and filter with its Catch2 lines, labeled local execution, not CI execution. Uploading successful logs is a separate CI decision for the user and CI owners."

### G26 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Definition of done does not carry the test-cost record refresh
- Where in the round-3 design: 8. Definition of done for the parent (design-r3.txt:495-499)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N36 N38 N41
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: process-ci
- Claim: The definition of done does not name the required CI checks that must be green on the final commit, does not mention the format and lint gate, does not carry the repository's test-cost refresh obligation, and does not state the PR label state. Each is a repository rule the design cites elsewhere, and each is checkable, so leaving them out lets an implementer declare the parent done while a rule is unmet. The refresh obligation matters because CI's cost-data check only reports missing records, so a stale record for a materially changed t_llama_unit does not fail CI and nothing else forces the refresh.
- The round-3 design text said: "Required tests collect and execute new behavior coverage with the named evidence. AMX-enabled, Debug, and limited 8-lane checks are reported separately. Baseline failures, placeholders, and skipped cases are named explicitly."
- Evidence given in round-3: README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:76-79 (required checks Lint, CI machinery tests, Fresh Cabal plan, Prepare Tron build inputs, Build ingest, Test ingest, Build model traces, Generate model plugins, Wait for lab cache, Build Tron, Test host, Test FPGA, Test), :171-176 (CI Lint = nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain -L --no-update-lock-file); AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:67-69 (flake.nix checks authoritative), :14-18 (label rules). t/AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:23-26 ('After adding or materially changing a test, refresh config/test-benchmarks.json with bin/slice bench --new or targeted bin/slice bench --update <name>'). gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:482-485 (bin/slice bench --check in Build Tron); bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:1810 (coverage_gaps), :6435 (--check reports coverage gaps only). Live check (gh, 2026-09-22): PR 4479 merged with only GCP Nix, deb-smoke, nix-eval, weeder and Graphite checks, so the legacy build/lint/test-host names are not required, which supports design-r3.txt:471. design-r3.txt:477 (make format, make lint), :478 (bench rule), :156 (labels).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add four bullets to the definition of done: (1) 'The final parent commit shows every check that README.ci.md names as required (Lint, CI machinery tests, Fresh Cabal plan, Prepare Tron build inputs, Build ingest, Test ingest, Build model traces, Generate model plugins, Wait for lab cache, Build Tron, Test host, Test FPGA, Test) as passed. Test host is proven by the execution evidence above, not by a reused passage marker.' (2) 'make format leaves no diff and make lint passes. make lint is the local subset of the CI Lint check; the CI check is nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain -L --no-update-lock-file.' (3) 'config/test-benchmarks.json: the granite_rapids_6962p block for t_llama_unit was refreshed on delphi-3bda under user direction, or the deferral and the user's direction are recorded in the PR description. CI's bin/slice bench --check reports only missing records, so a stale record does not fail CI.' (4) 'The parent PR carries the Skip benchmarks label and no Run CI label.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 2 of 3; the design-text verifier refuted three of the four items because the labels (:156), the format and lint gate (:477) and the bench rule (:478) are stated as binding rules elsewhere on the page, and the intent verifier narrowed the finding to the cost-record item. The lead follows that narrowing and keeps minor: CI's bin/slice bench --check verifies only that a record exists, so a stale record for a materially changed t_llama_unit fails nothing, and only the definition of done can force the refresh or its recorded deferral. The risk judge also asks the definition of done to require the AMX-on build before the parent leaves draft (see section 8).
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow the premise: write "three of the four items are stated in the design body (make format and make lint at design-r3.txt:477, the bench refresh at :478, the labels at :156 and :395); the required-check name list of README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:76-79 appears nowhere in the design, which names only the Test host job (:469-470) and the manual legacy workflow (:471)".

Recommendation (2): replace "make lint is the local subset of the CI Lint check" with "make lint is the local form of the required Lint check (README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:655-664 lists make build-test, make format, make lint under Reproducing CI Locally). The CI Lint job (gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:43-56) runs bin/ci/run-lint-checks.sh, which runs nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.lint-toolchain -L --no-update-lock-file --no-link (run-lint-checks.sh@2880c3aa9b:7,18)". I did not verify that make lint (GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:693-: whitespace diff against origin/main, the <cassert> ban, clang-format-19 --dry-run, Rust formatting) is a strict subset of the Nix check, so drop the word "subset". This matches the round-2 N41 design-text verifier wording.

Recommendation (3): add the trigger from t/AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:23-26, "after adding or materially changing a test". The parent adds cases to t_llama_unit and rewrites its compile-time probe [design-r3.txt:441, 466-467], so the rule applies. Cite bin/slice_bench_manifest.py@2880c3aa9b:100-119 for "names only", next to bin/slice@2880c3aa9b:1792-1832.

Recommendation (4): add "unless the user explicitly directed otherwise", because AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:14-16 allows removing Skip benchmarks with explicit user approval.

Evidence: the live check also shows Ingest Code Coverage and Cursor Bugbot on PR 4479, and the branch-protection settings were not readable (HTTP 404, rulesets empty), so state that "required" rests on README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:76-79 plus the observed check set of the merged PR 4479, not on the protection settings themselves.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the finding to one note-level addition and drop the rest. ### G28 [round-3 NEW finding, severity MINOR] Marker and instance rule cited to lib-guard.sh, which contains neither
- Where in the round-3 design: 8. Commands and execution hosts, last bullet (design-r3.txt:481)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N37 F48
- Kind: factual error | found by lenses: process-ci, regression-diff
- Claim: The bullet about the second-socket assignment, --instance 1,2, and the /bill-has-instance-0,2 marker carries the [Host guard] citation to exec/lib-guard.sh. That script contains no marker logic and no instance rule. The rule lives in exec/bill-share.sh, which the design does not link. An implementer who follows the citation cannot find the meaning of the marker or which half of the machine is theirs. The facts in the sentence are right; the source is wrong. The guard conditions listed in the two bullets above it are correctly cited.
- The round-3 design text said: "Preserve the second-socket assignment, --instance 1,2, and the other user's /bill-has-instance-0,2 marker. A Tron binary driving hardware must have an explicit instance. Whole-machine measurements require user direction. [Host guard] <../../../exec/lib-guard.sh>"
- Evidence given in round-3: ~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/lib-guard.sh (339 lines): no occurrence of 'instance' or 'bill-has'; the only 'bill' text is a uid example in a comment at :70; the listed guard conditions (busy lease, other active user, blackout, campaign lock, runtron process, rinzler serving, --allow-serving) are at :146-182. ~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/bill-share.sh:4-16 ('first half = instance 0 of 2 of the machine (tron's --instance 0,2), the half Bill uses; second half = instance 1 of 2, where jhan's harness always runs; marker = the file /bill-has-instance-0,2 on delphi-3bda'; 'Our second-half campaigns run either way; they never look at the marker. take happens only on jhan's word, never from a campaign script'), :24 (MARKER=${BILL_MARKER:-/bill-has-instance-0,2}). grep: the marker string appears in exec/bill-share.sh and result pages only. design-r3.txt:481 is a NEW round-3 segment (design-wdiff.txt: REPLACE @old 6231 / new 8718 and following).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Change the citation of this bullet to [Sharing protocol] <../../../exec/bill-share.sh> and keep [Host guard] on the two guard bullets above it. Add one sentence: 'Campaign and test scripts never create or remove the marker; only the user changes it with bill-share.sh take or release.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade minor): Holds 3 of 3. Corrections: the bullet is not new (round 2 had it with the [F48] citation); round 3 swapped the citation to [Host guard], which is the wrong file. The two guard bullets above it carry no link at all, so move [Host guard] to the campaign_guard_acquire bullet (:479) and cite exec/bill-share.sh for the marker and the instance halves. lib-guard.sh mentions Bill only in comments at lines 16, 70 and 305.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow the evidence and fix the recommendation. Evidence: say 'lib-guard.sh mentions Bill only in comments at lines 16, 70 and 305; none states the marker or instance rule' instead of 'only at :70'. Say 'the marker string appears in bill-share.sh, in campaign scripts that only log its presence (for example exec/wedperf-20260916/campaign.sh:147, exec/canon-ci-20260918/dut.sh:56), and in result pages' instead of 'bill-share.sh and result pages only'. Recommendation: replace 'keep [Host guard] on the two guard bullets above it' with 'move the [Host guard] link <../../../exec/lib-guard.sh> to the bullet at design-r3.txt:479, which is the bullet that describes campaign_guard_acquire; the bullets at 479-480 currently carry no link'. Keep the rest: change the citation at design-r3.txt:481 to [Sharing protocol] <../../../exec/bill-share.sh>, and add the sentence 'Campaign and test scripts never create or remove the marker; only the user changes it with bill-share.sh take or release' [bill-share.sh:11-12, 38-52].
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Keep the finding, with five corrections. (1) Not a new bullet: the round-2 text already had it at design-revised-text.txt:435, cited "[F48]". Round 3 reworded it and replaced "[F48]" with "[Host guard]" (design-wdiff.txt: REPLACE @old 6251 / new 8743, OLD "Never launch the real driver there without an explicit instance. [F48]", NEW "[Host guard]"). The mis-citation is new; the bullet is not. Round-1 F48 had asked to cite notebook handoffs/check-CI.md and delphi-3bda-guard.md (r1-findings.md:449). (2) Evidence: "Bill" appears in lib-guard.sh at :16, :70 and :305, not only at :70; none of the three is the marker or instance rule, so the claim is unchanged. (3) The two bullets above (design-r3.txt:479-480) carry NO citation; "[Host guard]" at the end of :481 is the only source link of the three-bullet group, and its content (refusal conditions lib-guard.sh:146-182, ci_took_dut :316, lease semantics :5-10) matches :479-480. Reword the recommendation to: attach "[Host guard] <../../../exec/lib-guard.sh>" to :479 (or :480) and give :481 its own citations. (4) "appears in bill-share.sh and result pages only" is wrong: campaign scripts read the marker for logging (exec/vnnik6-20260916/campaign.sh:117, exec/wedperf-20260916/campaign.sh:147, exec/q4b-rt8u-20260922/campaign.sh:127, exec/canon-ci-20260918/dut.sh:56, exec/l8b-levers-20260919/dut.sh:56) and whole-machine campaigns call bill-share.sh take/release (exec/canon-ci-20260918/launch.sh:5,20 take on jhan's order; exec/canon-ci-20260918/campaign.sh:49,111 release; exec/ci-mimic-20260918/campaign.sh:113 release; exec/l8b-levers-20260919/launch.sh:30 take; exec/l8b-8u4k-20260920/campaign.sh:96,194 release). (5) The proposed added sentence "Campaign and test scripts never create or remove the marker" is false for whole-machine campaigns (item 4). Replace it with: "Second-half campaigns never change the marker. A whole-machine campaign, only when the user directs it, removes the marker with bill-share.sh take before launch and re-creates it with bill-share.sh release at the end [bill-share.sh:9-16]." Corrected citations for :481: "[Sharing protocol] <../../../exec/bill-share.sh>" for the marker, and "[Instance rule] ~/workspace/notebook/handoffs/delphi-3bda-guard.md" (lines 11-14 and 85-95) for the second-socket assignment and the explicit --instance requirement, since bill-share.sh states the instance rule only in passing (:4-5).
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Keep the finding; narrow four points. (1) Evidence: lib-guard.sh mentions Bill at three comment lines (:16, :70, :305), not only :70; none describes the marker or the machine halves, so the central claim stands. (2) The two guard bullets (design-r3.txt:479-480) carry no citation link at all; bullet 479 names the script path only as inline code text. Replace "keep [Host guard] on the two guard bullets above it" with "move the [Host guard] link to the campaign_guard_acquire bullet (design-r3.txt:479), whose refusal list matches lib-guard.sh:146-182". (3) The bullet is not new in round 3. The round-2 text already had it with the same substance, cited to the round-1 finding [F48] (evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:435). Round 3 reworded it and swapped the citation from [F48] to [Host guard] (design-wdiff.txt segments at old 6240-6251 / new 8723-8743). So the defect is a wrong citation introduced by the citation swap, not a new bullet. (4) Round-1 F48 asked to cite notebook handoffs/delphi-3bda-guard.md; that file's Rule 2 (lines 58-77) documents the socket-1 cards and --instance 1,2 but never mentions the marker (grep for bill-has-instance: no match). Recommended citations for bullet 481: [Sharing protocol] <../../../exec/bill-share.sh> for the marker and the halves, and optionally the handoff Rule 2 for the socket-to-instance mapping. The added sentence should follow bill-share.sh:9-16 exactly: "Second-half campaigns never read the marker. Removing it (take) happens only on the user's word, never from a campaign or test script. After whole-machine work ends, release re-creates it."
