### G27 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Debug row: 'make BUILD_TYPE=Debug <test>' does not reconfigure an existing gen/, and 'Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo' holds for one path only
- Where in the round-3 design: 8. Build configurations table, row 'NaN-preserving Debug check, 16 lanes' (design-r3.txt:455; worktree rule :449)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N39 N60 N02
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: process-ci, disposition-checker
- Claim: The row tells the agent to run targeted conversion cases with make BUILD_TYPE=Debug and the test target. That command changes the build type only when Make re-runs the CMake configure step, and Make re-runs it only when the gen/config/test stamp is missing or one of its listed inputs changed; none of those inputs tracks BUILD_TYPE. So in a gen/ already configured as RelWithDebInfo, make BUILD_TYPE=Debug t_llama_unit keeps the cached RelWithDebInfo and TRON_IGNORE_NAN stays defined; the agent would test the macro-defined policy while believing it tested the Debug policy. Conversely, in a worktree configured by hand with the row's cmake line, the first plain make target re-runs cmake with Make's default -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo and silently undoes the Debug configuration, while in a Make-configured Debug worktree a later plain make keeps Debug. The sentence 'Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo' is true for the first path and false for the second, and the row does not say which. The t_% Make target also builds and then runs the whole binary with no Catch2 filter. The design's separate-worktree rule (:449) and 'Confirm the macro is absent' limit the damage, so the risk is low.
- The round-3 design text said: "Run targeted conversion cases using make BUILD_TYPE=Debug with the applicable test target, or build with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit after Debug configuration. Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo. Confirm the macro is absent."
- Evidence given in round-3: GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:80 (BUILD_TYPE := RelWithDebInfo), :538-543 (gen/config/test depends on $(CMAKELISTS), config/models.yaml, t/proxy/ctest-trampoline.sh, the FUSE and preset stamps and the plugin-bundle stamps, then runs cmake --preset $(CMAKE_PRESET) -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=$(BUILD_TYPE) ...), :299-303 (preset stamp tracks CMAKE_PRESET only), :289-290 (FUSE stamp tracks ENABLE_FUSE_STATS only), :880-888 (gen/t_%: gen/config/test $(HEADERS) $(SOURCES) runs cmake --build gen --target; t_%: gen/t_% runs gen/$@ with no arguments). CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:34-38 (native preset fixes binaryDir gen and CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE RelWithDebInfo). design-r3.txt:449, :455, :437, :513 (section 9 N02 N12 row).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the row text with: 'Configure a separate worktree with this row's cmake line. Build with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit and run gen/t_llama_unit with a Catch2 name filter for the conversion cases. Do not switch an existing gen/ with make BUILD_TYPE=Debug: the Make configure recipe (gen/config/test) runs only when its stamp is missing or a CMakeLists.txt, models.yaml or preset input changed, and it does not track BUILD_TYPE. In a hand-configured Debug worktree the first plain make target (make build-test, make test-host, make t_llama_unit) re-runs cmake with Make's default -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo. In a Make-configured Debug worktree a later plain make keeps Debug. Confirm TRON_IGNORE_NAN is absent from compile_commands.json for the binary you run.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 2 of 3; lowered to note by the lead. The design's prescribed path (a fresh worktree per configuration, :449) makes make BUILD_TYPE=Debug correct, because a hand-configured gen/ has no Make stamp. The hazard is only when a Make-configured RelWithDebInfo gen/ is reused, plus the sentence "Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo", which is true after a hand configure and false after a Make configure. make t_<name> also runs the whole binary with no Catch2 filter.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow "an existing gen/" in the title and claim to "a gen/ that a Make target already configured (gen/config/test present and up to date)". A gen/ configured only by the row's hand cmake line has no stamp, so `make BUILD_TYPE=Debug t_llama_unit` there DOES re-run cmake with Debug [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:538-543]. On the design's prescribed path (fresh worktree per configuration, design-r3.txt:449) the row's `make BUILD_TYPE=Debug` instruction is therefore correct; the defect is (1) the unqualified sentence "Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo" [design-r3.txt:455], which is true only after a hand configure and false after a Make configure, and (2) the unstated hazard of reusing a Make-configured RelWithDebInfo gen/. Replace "silently undoes" with "re-runs cmake with RelWithDebInfo; the only sign is the ordinary configure output" (the recipe prints cmake's stdout; no warning names the change). In the recommendation, replace "absent from compile_commands.json for the binary you run" with "absent from the compile lines of the test's translation units in gen/compile_commands.json (CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS is on in the base-ninja preset [CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:22])", and extend "a CMakeLists.txt, models.yaml or preset input changed" to also name t/proxy/ctest-trampoline.sh, ENABLE_FUSE_STATS and the plugin-bundle stamps [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:538]. Suggested one-sentence design fix for the row: "Make re-runs cmake only when gen/config/test is missing or one of its listed inputs changed; it does not track BUILD_TYPE. So a hand-configured Debug worktree loses Debug on the first plain make target, and a Make-configured worktree keeps whatever build type its first make used. Do not reuse a gen/ from another row."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow G27 to a note with two wording changes in the Debug row [design-r3.txt:455]. (1) Replace "Run targeted conversion cases using make BUILD_TYPE=Debug with the applicable test target, or build with cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit after Debug configuration." with "In this row's fresh worktree, build with `make BUILD_TYPE=Debug gen/t_llama_unit` or with `cmake --build gen --target t_llama_unit` after the cmake line. Then run `gen/t_llama_unit` with the conversion case names as arguments. The `make t_<name>` target runs the whole binary with no Catch2 filter (GNUmakefile:886-887 at 2880c3aa9b)." (2) Replace "Plain Make defaults can restore RelWithDebInfo. Confirm the macro is absent." with "A Make target reconfigures gen/ with -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo whenever gen/config/test is missing or a CMakeLists.txt, config/models.yaml or preset input changed (GNUmakefile:538-543 at 2880c3aa9b). Before running, confirm CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug in gen/CMakeCache.txt and no -DTRON_IGNORE_NAN in gen/compile_commands.json." Drop the finding's claim that the row "does not say which" path restores RelWithDebInfo: the design's "can" [design-r3.txt:455] plus "may reconfigure it. Confirm the cached feature values after invoking them" [design-r3.txt:449] already cover both paths, and the finding's sentence "In a Make-configured Debug worktree a later plain make keeps Debug" holds only while no configure input changes. Drop the "existing gen/" scenario as a defect: design-r3.txt:449 requires a separate worktree with its own gen/ for every configuration pair, and the Debug row is one of those configurations.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the title to: "Debug row: make BUILD_TYPE=Debug does not reconfigure a gen/ that Make already configured (the configure stamp does not track BUILD_TYPE), and make t_<name> runs the whole binary with no case filter". Keep the mechanism as stated [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:80, :289-290, :299-303, :538-543, :880-888]. Drop the consequence clause "the agent would test the macro-defined policy while believing it tested the Debug policy" or qualify it: this happens only if the agent ignores the separate-worktree rule and the macro check the design already gives [design-r3.txt:449, :455]. Replace the long recommendation with one addition to the row at design-r3.txt:455: "Make's configure step (gen/config/test, GNUmakefile:538-543) does not track BUILD_TYPE. It runs only when the stamp is missing or a CMakeLists.txt, models.yaml or preset input changed. Use make BUILD_TYPE=Debug only in a fresh worktree or directly after this row's cmake line. make t_llama_unit runs the whole binary; for the targeted cases run gen/t_llama_unit with a Catch2 case name. Confirm -DTRON_IGNORE_NAN is absent from gen/compile_commands.json (CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS is on in the base-ninja preset, CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:18)." Severity note; no change to the disposition of N02, N12 or N60.

### G35 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Three role labels for one person (issue author, issue assignee, the user) are never tied together
- Where in the round-3 design: 1. Words used here (omission); 2. (design-r3.txt:46, :71, :76, :90, :99, :105, :107, :120); 8. (:156, :465, :468, :478); 10. (:586)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N11 N08
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: fidelity, disposition-checker
- Claim: The design gives jhan three different role labels and never states they are one person. Section 2 uses 'the issue author' (:71, :76, :99), 'the issue assignee, jhan-positron' (:90) and 'the assignee' (:107, :120); sections 8 and 10 use 'the user' (:468, :586). Two decisions hinge on the mapping: D5's rejection path 'requires the issue author's direction' (:99), while the decision record is owned by 'the issue assignee' (:90). The Words table defines 'Reviewer' by name and role (:45) but has no row for the author, assignee or user. On GitHub the author and the assignee are the same login, and the issue was created by codex under that login at jhan's direction, so 'issue author' is accurate only with that context. ### G37 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Section 2 does not answer jhan's step-4 premise that the child's packed-K change becomes local to the new type
- Where in the round-3 design: 2. The reviewer's comments and proposed decisions (design-r3.txt:62-76, omission); 7. (:425)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N56 F51
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: fidelity
- Claim: jhan's request states the premise that, after the split, the VNNI-K change of PR #4424 'will be a local change within the new tensor data type, not at main function'. The design's own child plan says the opposite for a large part of the child: storage selection stays in the cache, score arithmetic stays in kernels, and worker coordination, generated calls, scratch callbacks, build options, workflow settings and tests 'remain outside the tensor' with layout knowledge 'necessary' there. The reviewer's sketch agrees. Section 2 is where the design answers jhan's request (it confirms his reading of the reservation at :71), but it never tells him that the step-4 premise is met only for storage, address mapping, row and block saves and kernel argument types.
- The round-3 design text said: "Storage selection stays in the cache. Score arithmetic stays in kernels. Worker coordination, generated calls, scratch callbacks, build options, workflow settings, and tests remain outside the tensor. Their knowledge of the selected layout remains necessary."
- Evidence given in round-3: codex-add-tensor-type.md:17-19 ('update PR #4424 to be atop the new PR (this means the VNNI-K change as introduced in PR #4424 will be a local change within the new tensor data type, not at main function)'). design-r3.txt:425, :392-394, :407-408; section 2 (:62-101) has no sentence on this, :71 answers only the step-2 reading. Reviewer comment 5765866077 section 5 (github-review.json comments[3].body): 'Conceptually, kv_block selects its storage at compile time' and 'Internally, kernels extract the pointer once and retain the existing instruction sequence.'
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add one bullet after the Native K bullet at :76: '- Issue author's step-4 expectation: the request assumed the child's packed-K change becomes local to the new type. That holds for storage selection, the address mapping, row and block saves, and the kernel argument types. It does not hold for the QK score algorithm, the shared block save and its generated calls, the FPGA scratch callback, the build option, or the tests; section 7 lists what stays outside the tensor.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note. This is the N56 residual seen from jhan's request: the step-4 premise that the child's packed-K change becomes local to the new type holds for the owner, the mapping, the row and block save bodies, the plane view and the kernel argument types, and not for the QK algorithm, the shared block save, the generated calls, the scratch callback, the build option or the tests. Correction: the std::conditional_t storage selection itself stays in kv_block, so do not list it as local.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Keep the finding as the N56 residual, not as a separate new finding: record it under N56 (status partly) with a cross-reference from the fidelity lens, or mark G37 "duplicates N56 residual". Fix the recommended bullet: replace "That holds for storage selection, the address mapping, row and block saves, and the kernel argument types" with "That holds for the packed storage owner k_vnni_tensor, the address mapping in k_vnni_view, the typed store_row/load_row/store_block operations, and the kernel argument types (design-r3.txt:391, :399, :405-406, :407); the std::conditional_t storage selection itself stays in kv_block (:399, :425)". Replace "the FPGA scratch callback" with the design's own term "the hardware::page_info K scratch callback (:408)". Placement: one sentence is enough, either after the Native K bullet at :76 (section 2, where jhan's steps 1 and 2 are answered at :67 and :71) or as the first sentence under the :418 heading, with a link between the two places; round-2 N56 asked for it in section 7, so either location satisfies both findings.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Three corrections to the finding text.

1. Fix the recommended bullet: it says the premise "holds for storage selection". That contradicts the design, which keeps storage selection in the cache: design-r3.txt:425 "Storage selection stays in the cache" and :399-401 (std::conditional_t on k_vnni::layout_on inside kv_block). Adding the bullet as written would create a contradiction. Replace "storage selection" with "the packed-K owner type (k_vnni_tensor), its address mapping (k_vnni::index and the layout_on constant), the row scatter/gather and block-store bodies, the plane view and row expression, the typed token and plane copies, and the kernel argument types" [design-r3.txt:391, :404-407].

2. Narrow the claim: the design is not silent on the split, it is silent on jhan's premise. Section 7 already gives the one-place outside list at :418 and :425 and the inside list at :391. Reword the claim to: "The design lists what moves into the K type (:391) and what stays outside it (:418, :425), but no sentence tells the requester that his step-4 premise holds only in part."

3. Record the overlap: this is the N56 partly-residual (round-3 checker, agent-notes/B03-regrader-source.md:4; round-2 N56 intent-lens correction asked to "make the paragraph answer jhan's expectation explicitly"). Offer both placements: one bullet after design-r3.txt:76 in section 2, or one opening sentence before :425 in section 7. Either satisfies N56 and G37 together. Minor citation fix: the saved JSON nests the comment list, so the path is github-review.json comments.comments[3].body, not comments[3].body.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Three corrections, none refuting. (1) Fix an internal slip in the recommendation: it says the premise "holds for storage selection", but the finding's own claim, the design [design-r3.txt:392-394, :425], the reviewer's sketch [comment 5765866077, section 5], and the round-2 N56 source-lens correction all place the storage selection (the std::conditional_t on k_vnni::layout_on inside kv_block, in kv_cache.hpp) outside the tensor type. Reword the "holds" list as: "the owner type k_vnni_tensor, the address mapping (k_vnni::index and layout_on move to h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp [design-r3.txt:389]), the row and block save bodies, the plane view with its row expression, and the kernel argument types". Move "the kv_block storage selection" to the "does not hold" list. (2) Grade this as the residual of round-2 N56 (its intent-lens correction asked for exactly this opening sentence) and mark N56 "partly" in the disposition, rather than counting G37 as a wholly separate new defect; G37 is the same omission relocated to section 2. (3) Placement: either the proposed bullet after design-r3.txt:76 or an opening sentence before design-r3.txt:425 satisfies the finding; if the sentence goes into section 7, one clause in section 2 ("section 7 lists what stays outside the tensor") is enough. Define "FPGA scratch callback" in the bullet as the hardware::page_info K callback [design-r3.txt:408], the term the design uses.

### G38 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Packed accessor names follow different patterns: page::v_view versus page::k_packed
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Existing callers table, page::v_data row (design-r3.txt:271); 7. Child interface table, page::k row (:404)
- Kind: page quality | found by lenses: cpp-interface
- Claim: The parent's packed V accessor is named page::v_view and the child's packed K accessor is named page::k_packed. Both return the read-only packed view of the same shape family. The names follow different patterns ('_view' versus '_packed'), so a reader of section 7 cannot infer the K name from section 5, and the child's name differs in style from the existing native pair page::k / kv_block::k_view.
- The round-3 design text said: "Add the const method page::v_view<geometry>(slot, head) in the 16-lane build only. [...] Replace the raw packed-plane accessor with page::k_packed<geometry>(slot, head) , constrained to packed storage."
- Evidence given in round-3: design-r3.txt:271 (page::v_view returns v_vnni_view<const bf16,page_size,geometry.head_size>), :404 (page::k_packed returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,page_size,geometry.head_size>), :267 (existing page::k and kv_block::k_view keep their names). kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1689 (the child's raw accessor is k_vnni_plane).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Use one pattern for both families. page::k_vnni_view would clash with the type name, so prefer page::v_packed<geometry>(slot, head) and page::k_packed<geometry>(slot, head); update lines 271, 404 and section 8 accordingly.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note. Both names follow review advice: page::v_view was round-2 N18/N23's recommendation, page::k_packed follows Ben's sketch, which uses both k_packed and v_packed. If the design wants one pattern, v_packed matches the reviewer's expectation; the lines to change are :271, :404 and :520.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow and re-anchor the finding as follows. (1) The name page::v_view is not codex's own choice. Round-2 findings N18 and N23 both recommended it: N18 said "Rename to page::v_view<geometry>(slot, kv_head) (or v_plane)" [r2-findings.md:327] and N23 said "Add page::v_view<geometry>(slot, kv_head) const under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 only, returning v_vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, geometry.head_size>" [r2-findings.md:358]. The design's section 9 records the adoption: "Return a read-only packed v_view only at 16 lanes" [design-r3.txt:520]. A recommendation to rename v_view to v_packed therefore reverses the round-2 name recommendation, and the finding must say so. (2) The name page::k_packed is new in this revision (round-2 text only said "page::k_vnni_plane Remove the raw-pointer accessor. Its replacement returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128>" [evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:377]; the word diff shows the NEW segment "with page::k_packed(slot, head)" [design-wdiff.txt:1384]). It matches the reviewer's sketch exactly: Ben's comment shows `auto k_packed(kv_slot_id slot, size_t head) const noexcept { return kv_block<geometry>(slot, head).k.as_view(); }` and the usage example `qk_vnni_128x4(page.v_packed(...), ...); // Compile error` [evidence/github-review.json, Ben's sketch comment: k_packed 2 hits, v_packed 1 hit, v_view 0 hits, k_view 0 hits]. So the reviewer's own vocabulary uses "_packed" for both accessors. This is the strongest reason to prefer v_packed / k_packed, and the finding should cite it instead of style alone. (3) Replace "cannot infer the K name from section 5" with "the two accessors use different suffixes for the same kind of return value, and the design gives no reason". Both names are stated explicitly [design-r3.txt:271, :404], so no inference is needed. (4) Replace "update lines 271, 404 and section 8" with "update lines 271, 404 and the section 9 disposition row at line 520". Section 8 [design-r3.txt:429-504] does not mention v_view or k_packed; the only other mention is the N16-N24 row [design-r3.txt:520]. (5) Optional supporting point: the parent merges before the child [design-r3.txt:124 "Add the tensor interface to main first"], so a v_view name frozen in main would need a second rename PR if the pair is later made consistent. Decide the shared suffix before the parent PR is opened. Keep the "page::k_vnni_view would clash with the type name" remark as a reason to avoid that option (a member named k_vnni_view inside page would hide the namespace-scope class template of the same name within page's scope); it is not central. The child accessor path in the evidence should read h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1689, not kv_cache.hpp alone.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Keep the finding with four changes. (1) Add the origin of each name: page::v_view follows the round-2 review's own recommendation N18/N23 [r2-findings.md:327, :358], which reversed round-1 F39's page::v_tensor; page::k_packed follows the reviewer's sketch, comment 5765866077, which names both accessors k_packed and v_packed [evidence/github-review.json]. The finding should say the review is reversing its own round-2 name if it now asks for v_packed. (2) Replace "section 8" in the recommendation with "section 9 row N16 N17 N18 N23 N24 [design-r3.txt:520]". Section 8 spans design-r3.txt:429-504 and does not name the accessor. (3) Offer the design two ways to close it: rename to page::v_packed so both accessors match the sketch, or add one sentence at design-r3.txt:404 stating why K does not use a "_view" name (kv_block::k_view is the native-only accessor per design-r3.txt:401, so a packed page::k_view would be confusing). (4) Weaken the "existing native pair" argument. Both new accessors are page members. The page-level precedent is the bare name page::k [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1549-1595]; k_view is a kv_block member [:2208, :2215]. Neither new name follows the page-level pattern, so the argument should rest on the V/K mismatch and the sketch, not on the native pair.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow and fix the finding as follows. (1) Add that both names follow round-2 recommendations: N18 asked for page::v_view, N03 offered "the sketch's k_packed" [r2-findings.md, N18 and N03 RECOMMENDATION]; the inconsistency is a review artifact, so the design is not at fault for following instructions. (2) Add the evidence that Ben's sketch uses both k_packed and v_packed [github-review.json, comments[3] body: "auto k_packed(kv_slot_id slot, size_t head) const noexcept" and "qk_vnni_128x4(page.v_packed(...), ...); // Compile error"]; this makes v_packed the name most likely to match the reviewer's expectation. (3) Drop "update ... section 8 accordingly": section 8 [design-r3.txt:429-504] mentions neither accessor. The lines to update are design-r3.txt:271 (section 5 row), :404 (section 7 row), and the section-9 disposition row at :520 ("Return a read-only packed v_view only at 16 lanes"). (4) Reword "page::k_vnni_view would clash with the type name": a member function named k_vnni_view would hide the namespace-scope type name inside page, which is an inconvenience rather than a compile clash; the point still favors k_packed. (5) Note that this contradicts the earlier N18 recommendation, so the review page should say the new preference (v_packed) supersedes N18's v_view.

### G39 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Dropped sentence mapping append_v_row to the sketch's store_row
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Interface design, One cache row-write rule (design-r3.txt:253-262)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N19

An implementer comparing the design with the reviewer's sketch has no sentence explaining the asymmetry. The semantics themselves are still stated. This does not re-open the rejected N19 (the name itself); it asks only to restore the explanatory sentence.
- The round-3 design text: omission
- Evidence given in round-3: design-wdiff.txt: DELETE @old 2720 / new 3701, OLD text: 'append_v_row is this design's name for the sketch's V store_row. The name records its cache semantics: an even destination zeros its odd partner; an odd destination preserves its even partner. There is no generic packed row.set(expr) in the parent.' grep append_v_row design-r3.txt: lines 255, 258, 268, 554 only; none maps the name to store_row. design-r3.txt:405 (child K row 'Call typed store_row / load_row'). Reviewer sketch names store_row/load_row for both layouts (evidence/github-review.json comment 5765866077; facts-r1.md sketch summary). r2-findings.md N19 (rejected: the rename itself).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add one sentence at the start of 'One cache row-write rule': 'append_v_row is this design's name for the sketch's V store_row; the different name records that a V cache row write has partner-zeroing semantics, while the child's K store_row does not.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note. Only the sketch-name mapping was lost; the partner-zeroing semantics survive at :258 and the no-generic-row.set rule at :288. The sketch spells store_row for K and says the V overloads correspond.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Keep the claim. Adjust three details. (a) Evidence wording: the sketch spells out store_row/load_row only for k_vnni_layout and says "Corresponding overloads for v_vnni_layout" [github-review.json comment 5765866077, body lines 135-146]; write "the sketch gives store_row/load_row for K and says the V overloads correspond" instead of "names store_row/load_row for both layouts". (b) Add to the evidence: the N19 rejection's first reason quoted the deleted sentence [r2-findings.md:1061-1063], and the design still says N19 remains rejected [design-r3.txt:554]; no round-2 finding asked for the deletion [r2-findings.md:412, 1061-1063 are the only references]. (c) Extend the recommendation by one clause so store_row is defined in the design: after the restored sentence, add "For K, store_row / load_row [design-r3.txt:405] are the typed replacements of the child's k_vnni::scatter_row and gather_row [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp:139, 221 @30c4ac82cb], called from set_k_row / get_k_row [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1706, 1742 @30c4ac82cb]." Round 3 dropped the earlier clause that carried this [design-wdiff.txt:1394-1395]. Suggested severity: minor.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim: only the sketch-name mapping was lost, not the semantics or the no-generic-row.set rule (those survive at design-r3.txt:258 and design-r3.txt:286). Note that design-r3.txt:554 ("Keep append_v_row's explicit cache semantics", linking the votes page whose N19 entry is titled "store_row renamed append_v_row") makes the mapping recoverable one link away. The recommended sentence is factually right and can stand: the child's scatter_row writes "Only this token's 64 pair slots" [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp:135-140 @30c4ac82cb], and the design already says K saves "Never read, merge, and rewrite the whole 64-byte line" [design-r3.txt:410]. Suggested placement: as the first bullet of "One cache row-write rule" [design-r3.txt:257], or in the Decision cell of the "#3: interface sketch" row [design-r3.txt:69] so the name map sits next to the sketch reference. Suggested wording: "append_v_row is this design's name for the sketch's V store_row. The different verb records that a packed V row write zeros or preserves the partner token's half of the pair, while the child's K store_row writes only the token's own lanes."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the evidence: the sketch spells out store_row/load_row only for k_vnni_layout and says "Corresponding overloads for v_vnni_layout" [github-review.json comment 5765866077 body lines 133-145]; it does not literally write a V store_row. Add: the deleted sentence's third clause ("There is no generic packed row.set(expr) in the parent") survives at design-r3.txt:288, so only the name-mapping clause is missing. Add: the N19 rejection cited the deleted sentence (design.txt:229) as its evidence that the name is deliberate and explained [r2-findings.md:1061-1063]; the remaining trace is design-r3.txt:554. Recommendation wording: "append_v_row is this design's name for the sketch's corresponding V store_row overload. The different name records that a V cache row write has partner-zeroing semantics (even destination zeros its odd partner, odd destination preserves its even partner), which the child's K store_row does not have." Place it as the first bullet under design-r3.txt:253 or fold it into the bullet at design-r3.txt:258.

### G40 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Row const-conversion left ambiguous by the new 'constructed only by the matrix view' rule
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. Interface design, View copy and conversion row (design-r3.txt:248); 8. Typed function boundaries (:438)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N20
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: disposition-checker
- Claim: The same table row says 'Allow writable-to-read-only conversion only' and then 'Rows are constructed only by the matrix view'. Section 8 asks tests to 'Accept writable-to-read-only conversion' without saying for which types. A converting constructor v_vnni_row<const bf16,D>(v_vnni_row<bf16,D>) is a constructor the matrix view does not call, so an implementer cannot tell whether rows convert (which needs a friend declaration across the T parameter, as the reviewer's sketch does for views) or do not convert (then a test that converts a row fails to compile). No named consumer needs the conversion: read-only rows come from a const matrix (:245).
- The round-3 design text said: "| View copy and conversion | Copying a view rebinds access. Allow writable-to-read-only conversion only. Owners, views, and rows expose no public raw-pointer constructor, data member, or pointer conversion. Rows are constructed only by the matrix view. |"
- Evidence given in round-3: design-r3.txt:248, :240, :245, :249, :438, :518 (section 9 'Restrict row construction'). Reviewer sketch (comment 5765866077): template <typename, typename> friend class vnni_view; for the view conversion.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Add one clause to design-r3.txt:248, either 'Rows have no cross-constness conversion; obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view' or 'v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> is constructible from v_vnni_row<bf16,D> through a friend declaration', and say in :438 that the accepted conversion is between views (and rows, if chosen).
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note. This is the unaddressed part of N20's corrected recommendation, so N20 is graded partly. The heading "View copy and conversion" and "Rows are constructed only by the matrix view" make no-row-conversion the likelier reading; the design should say so in one clause and make :438 name views.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow and sharpen in four places. (1) State that the literal reading of design-r3.txt:248 already leans one way: a converting constructor is not construction "by the matrix view", so rows do not convert under that reading; the ambiguity is between that literal reading, the untyped test instruction at design-r3.txt:438, and the N20 corrected recommendation that asked for the row friend. (2) Add the disposition fact: round-2 N20's verifier corrections (design-text lens and intent lens) both asked for `template <typename, size_t> friend class v_vnni_row;` so that v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> accepts v_vnni_row<bf16,D>; the design's section-9 row for N20 (design-r3.txt:518, "Restrict row construction") records no decision on that item, so grade N20 as partly, not resolved. (3) Cite Ben's sketch precisely: comment 5765866077 body lines 73-77 (the `requires(std::is_const_v<T> && std::same_as<U, bf16>)` converting constructor) and lines 90-91 (`template <typename, typename>` / `friend class vnni_view;`), which the finding quotes as one line. (4) Add design-r3.txt:401 ("Apply the V owner's lvalue-only as_view(), const conversion, and private packed-pointer rules to K too") as a second site that inherits the same ambiguity for the child's K row; one clause at :248 settles both. Recommended wording for :248, preferred option given no consumer: "Rows have no cross-constness conversion; obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view. Writable-to-read-only conversion applies to views." Then at :438 replace "Accept writable-to-read-only conversion" with "Accept writable-to-read-only view conversion; reject row-to-row conversion" (or "accept" it, if the friend option is chosen), and add the chosen option to the N20 row at :518.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Keep the claim and recommendation. Three narrowings. (1) Attribute the gap to N20: this is the unaddressed part (1) of N20's corrected recommendation (batches/B09.md:19-20, "give the row a template <typename, size_t> friend class v_vnni_row; declaration" or otherwise make the allowed conversion compile with a private constructor). The disposition checker should grade N20 "partly", not "resolved", unless the design adds either clause; the private-constructor and view-as-only-producer parts of N20 are resolved by design-r3.txt:248. (2) State that the view side is not ambiguous: design-r3.txt:73 retains the sketch's "mutable-to-read-only conversion", and the sketch supplies the mechanism for views (comment 5765866077 body lines 73-75, 90-91); only rows are open, because the sketch has no row type. (3) Replace "then a test that converts a row fails to compile" with "then a STATIC_REQUIRE that asserts row convertibility fails, or a conversion expression in the test does not compile" to match the STATIC_REQUIRE / STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE form design-r3.txt:438 prescribes. Preferred fix text for design-r3.txt:248, given that no consumer needs it: "Rows have no cross-constness conversion. Obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view (a const matrix or a v_vnni_view<const bf16,R,D>)." Add to design-r3.txt:438: "The accepted and rejected const conversions are between views; rows are checked only for const-row mutation."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the claim: the row heading "View copy and conversion" and the sentence "Rows are constructed only by the matrix view" (design-r3.txt:248) already make "rows have no cross-constness conversion" the more likely reading, so the design is not silent, only implicit. The residual defects are (a) design-r3.txt:438 does not say the accepted and rejected const conversions are between views, and (b) the section-9 row for N20 (design-r3.txt:518) neither adopts nor declines the round-2 verifier clause that asked for a row-to-row friend declaration (r2-findings.md, N20 verifier corrections). Recommended fix, one clause each: in :248 add "Rows have no cross-constness conversion; obtain a read-only row from a read-only matrix view (see :245)", which is the simpler choice and consistent with "constructed only by the matrix view" and with Ben's sketch, which has no row type; in :438 change "Accept writable-to-read-only conversion" to "Accept writable-to-read-only conversion between views" and add "reject a row constructed from a raw pointer" to the reject list, as the corrected N20 recommendation asked. Keep the finding's link to N20 as "partly": row construction is restricted, the conversion clause is unaddressed.

### G41 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] DMA-flag sentence presumes an existing view at two raw-pointer call sites
- Where in the round-3 design: 5. One cache row-write rule (design-r3.txt:257); callers table (:268-269); 7. step 3 (:374)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N15
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: disposition-checker
- Claim: The sentence 'Dma preserves the existing contiguous view's DMA flag' has nothing to preserve at two of the three migrated call sites, so the implementer must still choose the Dma value there. The 16-lane model branch passes a raw pointer &vs[ix][kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size], and the GOF callback passes a raw bf16* dest; only the 8-lane branch has an existing view, vs[ix].as_view(), and tensor::as_view() carries dma = false. The packed bodies do not read the flag, so the choice has no behavioral effect.
- The round-3 design text said: "Source is bf16, fp16, or float. Destination is bf16 or float. Dma preserves the existing contiguous view's DMA flag. Explicitly construct read-only source views where template deduction would otherwise see writable views."
- Evidence given in round-3: h/tron/models/model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2831-2832 (raw pointer in the 16-lane branch), :2834-2836 (8-lane branch vs[ix].as_view()); h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2652-2656 (raw bf16* dest in the GOF callback); h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:71-72, :35 (as_view() carries dma = false). design-r3.txt:255, :257, :258-259, :268-269, :374, :377, :519.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the sentence with: 'Dma is deduced from the caller's view. Where a call site wraps a raw pointer today (the model's 16-lane branch and the GOF scratch), construct the view with dma = false, the value tensor::as_view() carries.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note, narrowed: the model's 16-lane branch already has vs[ix].as_view() (dma = false), so only the GOF scratch and the raw-array test fixtures have no existing view; there the honest value is dma = false, and no packed body reads the flag.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow the claim to the GOF callback scratch and the raw-array test fixtures. Suggested claim: "The sentence 'Dma preserves the existing contiguous view's DMA flag' [design-r3.txt:257] gives no value where the caller owns plain memory with no view: the GOF callback's dest points into `alignas(64) std::array<bf16, ...> v_scratch` on gof::populate's stack [src/tron/gof.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203, :213; full.hpp:2652-2656], and the test fixtures pass alignas(64) float arrays or std::array data() [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1923, 2038, 2705, 2761, 2792, 2824; t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441-444]. At those sites the implementer must pick a Dma value and the design does not name one. No packed body reads the flag [design-r3.txt:241], so the choice has no behavioral effect." Drop the model site from the "nothing to preserve" list: `vs[ix].as_view()` exists there [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1382-1383, :2834-2836] and its flag is executor-dependent (true for hardware::btensor = dmatensor<bf16> in tp1/tp2/tp4 [common.hpp:94-99; btensor.hpp:21; dmatensor.hpp:223-228], false for tensor in host [common.hpp:100; tensor.hpp:71-75]). Replace the recommendation with: "Dma is deduced from the caller's view. At the model save site build the source with slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size), as the 8-lane branch already does [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2834-2836]; slice keeps the buffer type's aligned and dma flags [slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:76-78]. Where the caller owns plain memory with no view (the GOF v_scratch array and the alignas(64) arrays in the tests), construct the view with dma = false, the value for memory that was not DMA-allocated [views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:11-21]. Test fixtures of type tensor<head_size> [t_llama_unit.cpp:2530-2536, :2565-2570, :2687-2691, :2718-2722] use v.as_view()." Keep severity note and kind ambiguity; relates_to N15 and N51 (the design's :241 sentence about no static DMA flag is N51's fix, and :257 is N15's).
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim to the GOF scratch site and drop the model site. Corrected claim: "The design tells the implementer to wrap the GOF callback's raw bf16* scratch in the ordinary contiguous destination view [design-r3.txt:259, :269, :439] but names no Dma argument for that wrap. The scratch is a stack array [src/tron/gof.cpp@2880c3aa9b:203, :213], not DMA-safe memory as views.hpp defines the flag [h/tron/tensor/views.hpp@2880c3aa9b:11-19], so only false is correct. The moved body never reads the flag, so the choice has no behavioral effect." Drop the 16-lane model branch from the claim: the buffer already has a contiguous view, vs[ix].as_view() [h/tron/models/model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1382-1383, :2834-2836], its flag is false [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:35, :71], and slice preserves it [h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:53, :76], so "Wrap aligned source rows" [design-r3.txt:374] preserves an existing flag there. Corrected recommendation, one clause added to design-r3.txt:257 or :259: "Dma is deduced from the caller's view. When wrapping the GOF callback's raw scratch pointer, pass dma = false, the value every tron owner's as_view() carries; the scratch is an ordinary stack array." Optional, belongs to the N15 disposition rather than this finding: name vs[ix].as_view() with slice at step 3 as the wrapping form, matching the 8-lane branch.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the claim to the sites that wrap a raw pointer with no existing view: the GOF v_head_fn scratch [full.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2652-2656; gof.cpp:203] and the test fixtures [t_llama_unit.cpp:107, 142, 796, 819, 1554, 1575, 2533-2721; t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:98; heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:443]. Drop the model 16-lane branch: its buffer has as_view() [model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1381-1383], the 8-lane branch shows the construction [model.hpp:2834-2836], and slice keeps the dma flag [slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:53-76]. Drop "two of the three migrated call sites": the 8-lane branch is not migrated [design-r3.txt:270]. Replace the recommendation with: "Dma is deduced from the caller's view. In save_v_impl build the packed-branch source as slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size), as the 8-lane branch does [model.hpp:2834-2836]; the production buffer is a dmatensor, so Dma is true there [dmatensor.hpp:223-227]. Where a site wraps stack memory (the GOF scratch and the test fixtures), construct the view with dma = false, because true means definitely DMA-safe [views.hpp:17-18]. The packed bodies do not read the flag." Do not cite tensor::as_view() as the value to copy; the model-site buffer is btensor = dmatensor<bf16, ...> [btensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:21], not tensor<N>.

### G42 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Uniform-array rule: the 'existing uniform accessor' justification holds only for uniform reclaimability
- Where in the round-3 design: 6. Arena construction without clearing, uniform bullet (design-r3.txt:349)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N25
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: behavior-arena
- Claim: The uniform bullet justifies the one-array rule by 'the existing uniform accessor's indexing across slots'. That indexing (one kv_block_t array starting at kv_blocks_alias and spanning all slots) exists only when layout_t::uniform_reclaimability holds. A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has two arenas, and its accessor addresses each slot through slot_base; t_llama_unit has a case for that layout. The rule itself is still right for it, because each arena is a contiguous run of the same kv_block_t. The design should say the rule applies to each arena and to every restore, and should not point the implementer at kv_blocks_alias, which is one slot's base (the storage-view slot) and is refreshed only after allocation.
- The round-3 design text said: "For uniform geometry, construct one array over the just-allocated arena's complete contiguous block region. Its bound is the checked arena byte count divided by sizeof(kv_block_t). This matches the existing uniform accessor's indexing across slots."
- Evidence given in round-3: kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289 (uniform_reclaimability branch: blocks = kv_blocks_alias; blocks[(slot.i * n_kv_heads + kv_head) * n_pages + pg]); :1290-1299 (else branch: slot_base(kv_storage_offset(physical_slot)); blocks[kv_head * n_pages + pg]); :682-683 (uniform_reclaimability); :1222-1228 (refresh_kv_blocks_alias sets kv_blocks_alias = slot_base(storage_view_offset_), called after allocate_kv_group at :1114 and :971). t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:603 TEST_CASE 'uniform-geometry mixed retention addresses both arenas'. evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:331 (sentence unchanged from round 2); round-2 N25 verifier recorded the two-branch nuance as an evidence precision only.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the third sentence with: 'Apply this to each arena separately: the retained arena in the constructor, and the reclaimable arena in the constructor and in every restore_reclaimable_kv_storage. A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has both arenas (kv_cache.hpp:1290-1299; t_llama_unit.cpp:603). Use the arena pointer just installed by allocate_kv_group, not kv_blocks_alias, which points at one slot's base and is refreshed later (:1222-1228).'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 2 of 3 at note, narrowed to the justification sentence at :349: the cross-slot indexing it cites exists only under uniform reclaimability, while a uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention addresses each slot through slot_base. The rule itself is right for both, because construction runs per allocate_kv_group call.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three details need narrowing. (1) The design text does not name kv_blocks_alias anywhere [grep of design-r3.txt: no hit]. The risk is indirect: an implementer who follows "the existing uniform accessor" to kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289 finds kv_blocks_alias as the base pointer. Reword "should not point the implementer at kv_blocks_alias" to "should not send the implementer to an accessor branch whose base pointer is kv_blocks_alias". (2) "refreshed only after allocation" is too narrow. refresh_kv_blocks_alias runs after allocate_kv_group returns in the constructor [1114] and in restore [971], and also on set_storage_view [897], reset_storage_view [902] and free_reclaimable_kv_storage [953]. The relevant fact is that inside allocate_kv_group [1199-1208], where design-r3.txt:348 places the construction helper, the alias is still nullptr (constructor, member default at 1128) or stale (restore, after the free at 953 refreshed it against a missing arena). (3) "Apply this to each arena separately" is already implied by "the just-allocated arena's" [design-r3.txt:349] plus the helper's placement in allocate_kv_group, which runs once per arena [1101, 1102, 968]. The recommendation should say it makes the implied scope explicit. Suggested replacement for the third sentence: "The existing uniform accessor indexes one array across slots only when layout_t::uniform_reclaimability holds (kv_cache.hpp:1284-1289); with mixed retention it addresses each slot through slot_base (1290-1299). The one-array rule still holds for each arena, because every slot of a uniform-geometry layout has the same kv_block_t and the slots of one arena are packed contiguously (650-671, 1188-1196). Use the arena pointer allocate_kv_group has just installed (retained_data or reclaimable_data), not kv_blocks_alias, which is one slot's base and is refreshed after allocate_kv_group returns (1114, 971, 1222-1228). A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has both arenas (t/t_llama_unit.cpp:602-603)." Also add the uniform-geometry mixed-retention case to the section 8 storage row, which names "mixed geometry" and "release/restore" but not that case [design-r3.txt:441].
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim to the justification sentence only and drop the two recommendation parts the design already covers. Suggested wording: "The third sentence of the uniform bullet [design-r3.txt:349] describes only the uniform_reclaimability branch of kv_block() [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289]. A uniform-geometry layout with mixed retention has two arenas, and its accessor addresses each slot through slot_base [:1290-1299; t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:602-622]. The prescription is right for both: construction runs per allocate_kv_group call, so each arena gets its own array in the constructor and in every restore [design-r3.txt:348-349; kv_cache.hpp:1101-1102, :968], and slot_base resolves to a block index inside that arena's array [:1188-1197, :659-661]." Optional text change, one clause appended to design-r3.txt:349: "Under uniform geometry with mixed retention, each arena holds a subset of the slots; the accessor reaches them through slot_base, which resolves to an index inside the same per-arena array (kv_cache.hpp:1290-1299)." Remove the "not kv_blocks_alias" sentence from the recommendation: the design does not name kv_blocks_alias anywhere (0 hits in design-r3.txt and in the HTML), and its region_base placeholder is the region selected inside allocate_kv_group [design-r3.txt:342, :348].
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow the claim to the justification sentence only. Keep: the third sentence of the uniform bullet [design-r3.txt:349] describes the uniform_reclaimability accessor branch [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1284-1289] and not the per-slot else branch [1290-1299], which a uniform-geometry mixed-retention layout uses [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:603]. Replace the recommendation with: change that sentence to "Each arena is one contiguous run of kv_block_t, because make_kv_slot_offsets packs uniform slots without padding (kv_cache.hpp:651-672) and slot_base is arena + n_pages * byte_offset (1188-1197). This holds whether or not the layout has uniform reclaimability; the uniform accessor's cross-slot indexing (1284-1289) is one instance of it." Drop the kv_blocks_alias sentence: the design never names kv_blocks_alias. Drop the "each arena / every restore" sentence: design-r3.txt:348 already places the helper in allocate_kv_group for both the constructor and restore_reclaimable_kv_storage, and that function handles one arena per call [kv_cache.hpp:1199-1208]. Optional addition: name "mixed retention" beside "mixed geometry" in the section 8 Storage and lifecycle row [design-r3.txt:441], citing t_llama_unit.cpp:603 as the existing case that restores the reclaimable arena next to a retained slot under uniform geometry.

### G44 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] K whole-plane copy sentence: the caller is not named in the body and the mechanism differs from the V owner-copy rule
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Child interface table, Copy and score readers row (design-r3.txt:407); compare 5. One cache row-write rule (:261); 8. Child only row (:443); 9. row N03.. (:514)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N17 N03
- Kind: contradiction | found by lenses: cpp-interface, section9-audit
- Claim: For V the design says a defaulted same-type owner copy is sufficient when a whole owner must be copied and forbids a copy_plane operation. For K it requires 'a typed K plane-copy operation' for 'the child's existing whole-plane copy caller' without naming that caller. The caller is the whole-page memcpy of kv_block::k inside copy_storage_slot in the child's kv_cache.hpp; both operands are lvalue kv_block members. Both owners are trivially copyable by the design's own rules, so dst_block.k = src_block.k (the defaulted copy assignment) is the direct replacement and matches the V rule. Two different mechanisms for the same need are not an error, but the design does not say why K needs the extra operation, and section 9 says the design names the 'actual K plane-copy caller' while the body does not.
- The round-3 design text said: "Use typed K token copies and a typed K plane-copy operation for the child's existing whole-plane copy caller."
- Evidence given in round-3: kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2100-2107 (comment 'So a whole-page copy is one memcpy. Every other range moves token by token.'; inside copy_storage_slot: if (src_begin == 0 && dst_begin == 0 && count == page_size) { memcpy(dst_block.k, src_block.k, sizeof(dst_block.k)); }). facts-r3.md:79 names the same site. design-r3.txt:238-239 (owner: trivial construction, copying, destruction; same-type copy and move defaulted), :261, :401 ('Apply the V owner's ... rules to K too'), :407, :443 ('typed whole-plane copy'), :514 ('specify row/block argument families and actual K plane-copy caller'); grep 'copy_storage_slot' in design-r3.txt hits only :272 (8-lane V range copies).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Either replace the sentence with 'Use typed K token copies and, for the child's one whole-page K copy in copy_storage_slot (the memcpy at kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2107, taken when the copy covers a full page), the defaulted owner copy dst_block.k = src_block.k, as section 5 allows for V', and change section 8's 'typed whole-plane copy' to 'whole-plane owner copy'; or name the caller and add the reason a separate typed operation is needed for K.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note, reclassified from contradiction to unexplained asymmetry: the V rule at :261 is V-scoped. The caller is the whole-page memcpy in copy_storage_slot (kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2107), identified only through the S18 link; the body row should name it and say why K gets a named operation while V relies on the defaulted owner copy.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow two parts. (a) "Without naming that caller" is true of the body text only. Section 10's S18 entry carries the link "[Whole-plane and row K copies]" pinned to h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb#L2099-L2115 [design-r3.txt:582; design-r3-links.tsv:219], which is exactly the copy_storage_slot whole-page memcpy (2107) and the token loop (2110-2115). So the section-9 claim "specify ... actual K plane-copy caller" [design-r3.txt:514] is backed by a pinned source link, not by a function name in the body. Reword to: "The body row [:407] does not name copy_storage_slot; the caller is identified only through the S18 link [:582]. Add the function name to the row." (b) The kind is an unexplained asymmetry, not a contradiction. The prohibitions on a plane-copy operation are V-specific ("No ... V plane-copy helper" [design-r3.txt:288]; "Add no V plane-copy operation" [:372]; "Do not add copy_plane for V" [:261]). No design rule forbids a K plane-copy operation, so the K sentence violates nothing; it adds an operation the design's own owner rules make redundant, and gives no reason. (c) In the recommendation, say the owner-copy replacement applies in the packed branch only: the memcpy at 2107 is inside if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<geometry.head_size>) [kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2099], and the native branch [2118-2126] keeps its row-range memcpy over k_at().data, which the design already constrains to the array member [design-r3.txt:401 "Constrain native k_view() and k_at() to the array branch"]. Recommended sentence for :407: "Use typed K token copies. For the child's one whole-page K copy in copy_storage_slot (the memcpy at kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2107, reached only in the packed branch when the copy covers a full page), use the defaulted owner copy dst_block.k = src_block.k, as section 5 allows for V; add no named K plane-copy operation." Change :443 "typed whole-plane copy" to "whole-plane owner copy". If codex keeps the named operation instead, the row must state the reason it is needed.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow three parts of the finding. (a) Replace "without naming that caller" with: "the body row [design-r3.txt:407] does not name the function or cite a source; the caller is identified only by the S18 link '[Whole-plane and row K copies]' (kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb#L2099-L2115) [design-r3.txt:582; design-r3-links.tsv:219], cited as [S18] in the paragraph above the table [:401]. Section 9's 'actual K plane-copy caller' [:514] rests on that link." (b) Change kind from "contradiction" to "unexplained inconsistency": :261 is scoped to V, and :401 lists which V owner rules extend to K (as_view, const conversion, private pointer) without the copy rule; no sentence contradicts another, but the design does not say why K gets a named operation while V relies on defaulted owner copying. (c) Keep the technical claim and the first recommendation as written, and add a second acceptable fix: if codex keeps a named typed K plane-copy operation, the row [:407] must state the reason (for example, to express every packed-K copy through views, as copy_token does for V) and must cite [S18] or name copy_storage_slot (kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2106-2107) in the row itself. Evidence citation "kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2100-2107" is exact: comment 2100-2102, guard 2106, memcpy 2107.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow two sub-claims and the kind. (a) "the caller is not named in the body" and "section 9 says the design names the actual K plane-copy caller while the body does not": the section-7 row at design-r3.txt:407 carries no name and no inline link (design-r3-links.tsv has no link on that row), but section 10 S18 at :582 links "Whole-plane and row K copies" to kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb#L2099-L2115 (design-r3-links.tsv:219), which is exactly the copy_storage_slot VNNI branch containing the memcpy at :2107. So the section-9 claim at :514 is supported by the S18 link, not by section 7. Reword: "The caller is identified only by the S18 source link; section 7 does not name copy_storage_slot or the full-page condition." (b) Kind: "contradiction" overstates. The V rule at :261 and the scope line at :288 ("No ... V plane-copy helper") are scoped to V, so :407 does not contradict them. The defect is an unexplained inconsistency plus an unnamed, unsigned operation. (c) Add to the evidence: the sentence is new in round 3 (design-wdiff.txt @old 5095-5099, OLD "Use typed K token/plane ... qk_vnni_128x4/qk_group arguments"); no round-2 recommendation asked for it (N03, N32 in r2-findings.md), and round-2 N17's corrected recommendation for V is the rule the finding asks to extend to K. (d) Recommendation option 1 stands as written. Add one sentence to it: "The PR 4424 body's 'Page copies move whole pages with one memcpy' (github-review.json /pr/body, jhan's text) is preserved: the defaulted owner copy of the trivially copyable 16,384-byte owner compiles to that memcpy." Ben's comments say nothing about plane copies, so neither mechanism conflicts with his sketch.

### G45 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Query-layout selection placed 'in kernels'; the child keeps it in self_attention.hpp
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Child interface table, Copy and score readers row (design-r3.txt:407)
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: child-needs
- Claim: The row says to retain the query-layout selection 'in kernels'. In the child, the selection between pack_q_rows_128x4 and the row-major packing, and between qk_vnni_128x4 and qk_rowmajor_128x4, is an if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) in self_attention.hpp, which the design's own S10 calls attention code. The round-2 text said 'outside the tensor', which was correct. 'In kernels' invites moving that compile-time selection into src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp, a change the design elsewhere forbids ('Keep dispatch').
- The round-3 design text said: "Retain score arithmetic and query-layout selection in kernels."
- Evidence given in round-3: h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1365-1366 (if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<geometry.kv.head_size>) { amx_attn_h128g4::pack_q_rows_128x4(group, slot);), :1436-1444 (if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<operation_head_size>) { qk_vnni_128x4(...) } else { qk_rowmajor_128x4(...) }), :1574 (constexpr bool k_vnni_on = ...). design-r3.txt:574 (S10: 'Attention code chooses the query orientation and score implementation.'), :375 ('Keep dispatch, algorithms, query packing, and output contracts.'). evidence/claude-review-r2/design-revised-text.txt:380 ('Keep optimized arithmetic and query-layout selection outside the tensor.').
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace the sentence with: 'Retain score arithmetic in kernels. Retain the query-layout and kernel selection (the if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) branches) in self_attention.hpp.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note (one verifier minor). The word changed from "outside the tensor" to "in kernels" in this revision while addressing N56. The selection is three if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) branches in self_attention.hpp, which the design's own Words and S10 call attention code, not kernels.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three narrowings. (1) Citation: design-r3.txt:375 ("Keep dispatch, algorithms, query packing, and output contracts") is the PARENT's step 4, not the child section. For the child, cite design-r3.txt:393 ("Keep the existing 64-token by 128-dimension kernel limit and compile-time layout selection") and the Words definition at design-r3.txt:39 ("Dispatch selects which kernel to call"). Replace "a change the design elsewhere forbids" with "a placement that contradicts the design's own definition of kernel vs dispatch [design-r3.txt:39], its S10 [design-r3.txt:574], and its 'keep the existing compile-time layout selection' instruction [design-r3.txt:393]". (2) Add the origin: the sentence changed from "outside the tensor." to "in kernels." in this revision [design-wdiff.txt:1426-1428] while addressing N56, whose correction asked only to list the score readers (qk_group, qk_vnni_128x4) as outside the K type [r2-findings.md:746]. (3) Recommendation: keep the proposed replacement for line 407, naming the row-major packer explicitly: "Retain score arithmetic in kernels. Retain the query-layout and score-kernel selection (the if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) branches choosing pack_q_rows_128x4 or pack_q_group_128x4 and qk_vnni_128x4 or qk_rowmajor_128x4 [self_attention.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1365-1369, 1436-1444, 1574]) in self_attention.hpp." Also add the same item to the summary bullet at design-r3.txt:425 so the subsection's one-place list (the N56 fix) names where the layout selection lives: "Query-layout and score-kernel selection stays in self_attention.hpp."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the "invites moving" part. The risk that an implementer moves the selection into src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp is low, for three reasons. "Retain" means keep in place. The child section says "Keep the existing 64-token by 128-dimension kernel limit and compile-time layout selection" [design-r3.txt:393]. Step 4 says "Keep dispatch, algorithms, query packing, and output contracts" [design-r3.txt:375], and S10 names attention code as the chooser [design-r3.txt:574]. So the defect is a factual misstatement of where the selection lives, which contradicts the design's own Words definition [design-r3.txt:39] and S10, rather than a live instruction to move code. Add one fact: the child's layout_on predicate is today in the kernels directory [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:93,96], which is the only reading under which "in kernels" is true now, but the design itself moves layout_on into h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp [design-r3.txt:391], so after the port neither the predicate nor the branches are in kernels. Recommended wording, aligned with the design's own bullet at design-r3.txt:425 ("Score arithmetic stays in kernels."): "Retain score arithmetic in kernels (qk_vnni_128x4 in src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp, qk_group in h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp). Retain the compile-time query-layout selection, the if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) branches, in self_attention.hpp." Kind stays "ambiguity"; a checker could also label it "factual slip".
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Keep the finding at note. Tighten the recommendation and citations as follows. (1) Replace the sentence at design-r3.txt:407 with: "Retain score arithmetic in kernels (qk_vnni_128x4 in src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp, qk_group in h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp). Retain the query-layout and kernel selection, the if constexpr (k_vnni::layout_on<...>) branches, in self_attention.hpp." (2) Add the same item to the "code that stays outside the K type" list at design-r3.txt:425, which currently names score arithmetic but not the selection: "Query-layout and kernel selection stays in self_attention.hpp." (3) Cite design-r3.txt:393 ("Keep the existing 64-token by 128-dimension kernel limit and compile-time layout selection") as the child-side sentence the row should agree with; design-r3.txt:375 ("Keep dispatch") is a parent step-4 statement and is weaker support for a child-section row. (4) Note that after the port the predicate k_vnni::layout_on lives in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp (design-r3.txt:391), so the branches in self_attention.hpp will reference the tensor header's predicate, not a kernel header's.

### G46 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Deduction trap for typed K row arguments not restated for the child's callers
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Child interface table, page::set_k_row / page::get_k_row row (design-r3.txt:405)
- Kind: omission | found by lenses: child-needs
- Claim: The K row parameters are given as const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> with Aligned and Dma as template parameters. Template argument deduction does not consider conversions, so a writable view or a raw pointer at the call site does not bind. Every child caller passes one of those: store_k_block passes the slice view's raw .data or a stack array, the FPGA callback passes a raw scratch pointer, and the layout test passes row.data(). Section 5 states the explicit-construction rule for the V rows only (:257). The K row does not repeat it.
- The round-3 design text said: "Use const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> for save and view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> for load. The boolean template arguments preserve the caller's alignment and DMA properties."
- Evidence given in round-3: design-r3.txt:257 ('Explicitly construct read-only source views where template deduction would otherwise see writable views.', V rows only). h/tron/models/model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2864 (row_of returns slice<head_size>()(ks[ix].as_view(), ...), a writable view), :2920 (set_k_row<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j, kout.data)), :2924 (set_k_row<...>(..., conv[0])); h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2655 (get_k_row<hw_kv>(slot, kv_head, off, dest) with bf16* dest); t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:401 (page->set_k_row(SLOT_0, 0, tok, row.data())).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Append to the row: 'As in section 5, construct the read-only source view explicitly at each caller (model.hpp store_k_block, the copy paths, full.hpp's K callback, t_k_vnni_layout); deduction of const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,...> does not see a writable view or a raw pointer.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note, narrowed to the save side: get_k_row's load view deduces from the wrapped scratch, but set_k_row's const_view parameter cannot deduce from the writable slice view kout (model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2864, :2920), because const_view's constructors from view are converting constructors that deduction ignores. The child's test callers are many more than t_k_vnni_layout: about 29 sites in t_llama_unit.cpp and one in t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Narrow and broaden as follows. (1) The design already says "Wrap scratch without copying" [design-r3.txt:405], so the raw-pointer scratch callers (full.hpp dest, conv[0], the test arrays) are covered in substance. The uncovered case is the writable slice view kout [model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2864, :2920], which an implementer would pass directly and which fails deduction; the missing element for all callers is the reason (deduction ignores converting constructors). (2) Broaden the caller list: t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp@30c4ac82cb:101, about 25 sites in t/t_llama_unit.cpp@30c4ac82cb (for example :362-366, :579-607, :2247-2285), and the uniform-geometry forwarding overloads kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1752-1764, whose const bf16* / bf16* parameters must also become the typed views. (3) Set relates_to = N32: the round-2 corrected recommendation asked the row to say "wrap kout, conv[i], and the callback's dest at the call site without copying" [r2-findings.md, N32 design-text-lens correction]; the design answered with "Wrap scratch" only. (4) Suggested row text: "Deduction of Aligned and Dma needs a const_view argument. Construct const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<D>,sseq<1>> explicitly from kout (a writable slice view), from conv[i], from the K callback's dest, and from test arrays, as section 5 requires for V rows. A writable view or a raw pointer does not bind [view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:116-117, 361-362, 373, 382-385]." (5) Cite view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:116-117 (view is final, derives from expr only) and :382, :385 (const_view converting constructors) as the source for the deduction claim.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim to the save side. The load side is already covered: get_k_row takes view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> [design-r3.txt:405], and wrapping the raw scratch pointer (full.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2655 dest; t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:405 got.data()) is what "Wrap scratch without copying" [design-r3.txt:405] asks for; that wrap deduces because it is the same class template. The gap is set_k_row: kout is a writable view [model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2861-2863, :2920; slice.hpp@2880c3aa9b:76], the conversion path already holds a writable view<bf16,true,false,seq<D>>{conv[0]} [model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2922-2924], and the layout test holds a non-const std::array at four save sites [t_k_vnni_layout.cpp@30c4ac82cb:401, 424, 428, 453], not one. Drop the sentence "Template argument deduction does not consider conversions" in favor of the precise mechanism: const_view's constructors from view are non-template converting constructors [view.hpp@2880c3aa9b:379-386], which deduction ignores. Do not cite design-r3.txt:401 "const conversion" as covering this: that sentence carries over the owner/view rule of :248, not a call-site rule. Recommended sentence for the :405 row: "Section 5's explicit-construction rule applies to the save side: kout and the conversion scratch view are writable views, so construct const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,seq<D>,sseq<1>> explicitly at each set_k_row call (model.hpp store_k_block, t_k_vnni_layout); the load side wraps raw scratch in the writable view as stated."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Three narrowings. (1) Remove "the copy paths" from the recommended caller list. The child's copy_storage_slot never calls set_k_row or get_k_row; it calls k_vnni::gather_row and k_vnni::scatter_row directly, plus memcpy for a whole page [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2107, 2110-2114], and the design routes copies through "typed K token copies and a typed K plane-copy operation" instead [design-r3.txt:406]. (2) Widen the test callers. At the child, t/t_llama_unit.cpp has 29 set_k_row/get_k_row call lines passing `.data()` pointers (for example :362-366, :392-407, :579-607, :829, :852, :1469, :1490, :1588, :1610, :1951, :2247-2249, :2284-2285, :2331-2333, :2410, :2442, :2724), t/t_k_vnni_layout.cpp has 8 (:401, :405, :424, :428, :434, :441, :453, :459), and t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp has 1 (:101 `zero.data()`). The design's child section names only t_k_vnni_layout for typed row saves [design-r3.txt:414]; the :377 mention of t_llama_unit.cpp and t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp is the parent's V step. (3) Narrow the gap statement. "Wrap scratch without copying" [design-r3.txt:405] already tells the implementer to wrap the raw scratch pointers (full.hpp `dest`, model.hpp `conv[0]`, the test buffers). What the K row lacks is (a) the writable-view source case at model.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2920, where `kout` is a writable slice view and the caller must write `const_view<...>{kout}` or wrap `kout.data`, and (b) the general statement that deduction does not use the view-to-const_view converting constructor or the explicit pointer constructor. Suggested text for the :405 row: "As for V in section 5, construct the read-only source view explicitly at each caller (model.hpp store_k_block both branches, full.hpp k_head_fn, and the set_k_row/get_k_row callers in t_llama_unit, t_k_vnni_layout, and t_amx_dispatch_dtype); deduction of const_view<bf16,Aligned,Dma,...> does not see a writable view or a raw pointer." Alternatively, generalize :257 to "both row families".

### G47 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] 8-lane child check: TRON_K_VNNI=OFF is forced, not chosen
- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:414); 8. Build configurations (:461)
- Kind: ambiguity | found by lenses: child-needs
- Claim: The design says the 8-lane child check uses TRON_K_VNNI=OFF and 'leaves K packing disabled', as if ON were a possible but skipped configuration. At the child head TRON_K_VNNI=ON fails to configure without TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, and TRON_AMX_DISPATCH fails to compile at 8 lanes. Saying so prevents an implementer from spending a build on the impossible combination and from reporting it as a skipped check.
- The round-3 design text said: "The limited 8-lane child check uses TRON_K_VNNI=OFF."
- Evidence given in round-3: src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@30c4ac82cb:209-214 (if(TRON_K_VNNI) if(NOT TRON_AMX_DISPATCH) message(FATAL_ERROR "TRON_K_VNNI requires TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON") ...); h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:30-36 (#if TRON_AMX_DISPATCH && TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 ... #error "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 (pair-interleaved V layout)"); CMakeLists.txt@30c4ac82cb:49 (option TRON_K_VNNI default OFF). design-r3.txt:414, :461.
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): Replace with: 'The limited 8-lane child check leaves TRON_K_VNNI=OFF because ON cannot build there: TRON_K_VNNI requires TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt FATAL_ERROR), and TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires the 16-lane build (self_attention.hpp #error). Report it as not applicable, not as skipped.'
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note. The most direct guard is kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:35-39 (#error when TRON_K_VNNI is defined and TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16); the CMake FATAL_ERROR is the configure-time guard. The design already says AMX-on fails its width check at :459; only the TRON_K_VNNI link is missing.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Keep the finding, with three refinements. (a) The finding's two-step chain (TRON_K_VNNI needs TRON_AMX_DISPATCH; TRON_AMX_DISPATCH needs 16 lanes) is right but indirect. The child has a one-step guard: h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:35-40 `#if defined(TRON_K_VNNI) && TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 ... #error "TRON_K_VNNI requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16"`. Cite it as the primary reason and keep the CMake FATAL_ERROR as the second reason. (b) Set relates_to = N34: round-2 N34's evidence already recorded this kv_cache.hpp #error (r2-findings.md:471, and its design-text verifier correction (2) at :474), and the design's N34 row [design-r3.txt:512] did not carry the reason into the body text at :414 or :461. (c) Recommended replacement text for design-r3.txt:414: "The limited 8-lane child check builds with TRON_K_VNNI=OFF. OFF is the only value that builds at 8 lanes: kv_cache.hpp rejects TRON_K_VNNI when TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 (#error), and the option also requires TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt FATAL_ERROR), which itself fails the 16-lane check in self_attention.hpp. Report the ON configuration as not applicable at 8 lanes, not as skipped." For design-r3.txt:461 replace "Its 8-lane check leaves K packing disabled." with "Its 8-lane check can only build with K packing disabled (see section 7)." Evidence set for the report: design-r3.txt:414, :454, :459, :461; h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:35-40; src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@30c4ac82cb:209-214 and :260-262; h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@30c4ac82cb:17-21; h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:30-36 (identical at 30c4ac82cb); CMakeLists.txt@30c4ac82cb:49.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Narrow the claim: the design already says at design-r3.txt:459 that AMX-on fails its width check in an 8-lane build, so the missing piece is only the link from TRON_K_VNNI to that requirement. Strengthen the evidence: cite the direct guard h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:35-39 (#if defined(TRON_K_VNNI) && TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 / #error "TRON_K_VNNI requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16"), which forbids ON at 8 lanes in one step, with the CMake FATAL_ERROR at src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@30c4ac82cb:209-214 as the configure-time guard. Add h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@30c4ac82cb:17-21 for TRON_CHUNK_SIZE = 8 when TRON_AVX512_ENABLED is undefined. Reword the recommendation for design-r3.txt:414 and :461: "The child's 8-lane check cannot enable K packing. kv_cache.hpp rejects TRON_K_VNNI when TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 (#error), and CMake rejects TRON_K_VNNI without TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (FATAL_ERROR), which itself fails the width check named in the native-probe paragraph. Report K packing at 8 lanes as not applicable, not as skipped." One of the two sites may point to the other instead of repeating the sentence. Note that round-2 N34 [r2-findings.md:469-473] already carried the kv_cache.hpp fact, so this is a residual of N34 rather than a new discovery.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Narrow and complete the recommendation. (1) Add the most direct guard: kv_cache.hpp itself rejects TRON_K_VNNI at 8 lanes [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:35-39], independent of the AMX chain. (2) Do not ask the design to repeat "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires the 16-lane build": design-r3.txt:459 already says "AMX-on fails its explicit width check". Only the TRON_K_VNNI -> TRON_AMX_DISPATCH link and the kv_cache.hpp guard are missing. (3) Suggested replacement for the sentence at design-r3.txt:414 (and the matching sentence at :461): "The limited 8-lane child check leaves TRON_K_VNNI=OFF. ON cannot build at 8 lanes: kv_cache.hpp rejects TRON_K_VNNI when TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16, and the option also requires TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, which fails the same width check. Report the ON configuration as not applicable at 8 lanes, not as skipped." (4) Citation fix for the design, not for the finding: the design's "self_attention.hpp:31-35" for the #error block should read 30-36 [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:30-36]; the finding's own citation 30-36 is right.

- Where in the round-3 design: 7. Then adapt PR #4424 (design-r3.txt:391); step 1 (:372); 9. row N03.. (:514)
- Relates to earlier id(s): N59
