### H03 [round-3 NEW finding, severity NOTE] Kernels-to-tensor include reverses the documented dependency direction
- Where in the round-3 design: 5 Build and include boundaries, design-r3.txt:284; 7 child, design-r3.txt:392
- Relates to earlier id(s): F43, G16, G17
- Kind: scope | found by lenses: completeness critic
- Claim: README.code-org.md lists tron/kernels as depending only on tron/simd, and tron/tensor as depending on tron/simd and tron/kernels. The design makes h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp include h/tron/tensor/kv_cache_fwd.hpp, and the child makes h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp include h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp. Both are kernels-to-tensor includes. Precedent exists: dotter.hpp, expr.hpp, strided_copy.hpp and topk.hpp already include tensor headers. The design does not cite the precedent. It also places a header named after the KV cache in a directory the README describes as hardware-agnostic tensor facilities. A reviewer who checks the README can reject the placement; the page gives the implementer no answer.
- The round-3 design text said: "amx_attn_iface.hpp includes kv_cache_fwd.hpp and remains free of intrinsics."
- Evidence given in round-3: README.code-org.md@2880c3aa9b:55 ('tron/tensor | Hardware-agnostic and on-CPU tensor facilities'), :58 ('Dependencies (aspirational)'), :76 ('tron/kernels depends on tron/simd'), :78 ('tron/tensor depends on tron/simd, tron/kernels'). Existing kernels-to-tensor includes at 2880c3aa9b: h/tron/kernels/dotter.hpp:10 (tron/tensor/view.hpp), expr.hpp:13 (tron/tensor/seq.hpp), strided_copy.hpp:4 and topk.hpp:16 (tron/tensor/array_like.hpp).
- RECOMMENDATION (what the design was asked to change): After design-r3.txt:284 add: 'This kernels-to-tensor include follows the existing dotter.hpp:10 and expr.hpp:13 precedent. README.code-org.md:76-78 lists the opposite direction as aspirational; state the precedent in the parent PR description.' Optionally rename the header to something layout-oriented, such as h/tron/tensor/kv_views_fwd.hpp, so the tensor directory does not carry a models-layer name.
- LEAD NOTE (final grade note): Holds 3 of 3 at note. Corrections: at 30c4ac82cb the child's h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp includes no tensor header; the include is the design's plan. README.code-org.md labels its list aspirational, live code already includes in both directions, and Ben's sketch itself puts the header under h/tron/tensor, so no reviewer objection is expected; the page may cite the precedent in one sentence.
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Three corrections. (a) Attribution: replace "the child makes h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp include h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp" with "the design's child-port plan makes h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp include h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp [design-r3.txt:391-392]". At 30c4ac82cb h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp does not exist, and the child's h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp:32-40 includes only <cstddef>, <cstdint>, <cstring>, <immintrin.h>, <type_traits>, common/attributes.hpp, common/numerics/bf16.hpp and common/numerics/fp16.hpp. (b) Narrow the risk sentence: the README list is labeled "aspirational" [README.code-org.md@2880c3aa9b:58], AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:113 says to read it "only as needed", no tool enforces it, and tensor headers already include kernels headers (h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:21, view.hpp:6, views.hpp:6 @2880c3aa9b), so the directories are mutually dependent today. Add: Ben's sketch itself places the layout and view types under h/tron/tensor/ ("// h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp", evidence/github-review.json, Ben's straw-man section 1), so only the header NAME kv_cache_fwd.hpp (after h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp [design-r3.txt:373]) departs from his layout-oriented naming; the optional rename recommendation should cite that. (c) relates_to: keep F43 (r1-findings.md:521); drop or re-verify G16 and G17, which are absent from the scratchpad. The recommendation text can stand with the corrected attribution; suggested sentence after design-r3.txt:284: "This kernels-to-tensor include follows the existing includes at h/tron/kernels/dotter.hpp:10 and expr.hpp:13 (2880c3aa9b). README.code-org.md:76-78 lists the opposite direction under 'Dependencies (aspirational)'; tensor headers already include kernels headers (view.hpp:6, views.hpp:6). State the precedent in the parent PR description."
- VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Three corrections narrow the finding.

1. Fix the attribution of the K include. The finding says "the child makes h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp include h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp". That is wrong. At 30c4ac82cb the child's h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp includes only <cstddef>, <cstdint>, <cstring>, <immintrin.h>, <type_traits>, common/attributes.hpp, common/numerics/bf16.hpp and common/numerics/fp16.hpp [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:32-40]. The child has no h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp at all (git ls-tree of h/tron/tensor/ at 30c4ac82cb shows no vnni file). The include is the design's child-adaptation plan [design-r3.txt:392]. ## Round-3 section 3: the 27 known items NOT resolved by the second revision (25 partly, 2 deferred), with STILL NEEDED text

### M01 [round 2 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] t_llama_unit itself does not compile in the 8-lane configuration at unmodified main
- Original round-2 claim: Factual gap plus ambiguity. The 8-lane row tells the implementing agent to build and run the t_llama_unit cases (t_llama_unit is the cache and model unit-test program) under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 (the AVX2 build with 8 fp32 values per vector). At live main that program cannot compile in that build, for two reasons independent of the parent. First, the template check_wide_scaled_v calls page::set_v, and its two instantiations at the 'scaled_v supports wide values' case are not guarded; page::set_v is declared only under `#if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16`. Second, the requires-expression constant page_supports_uniform_kv_access asks for page.get_v with a bf16* destination and is asserted true with STATIC_REQUIRE on the empty uniform cache; get_v also exists only in the 16-lane build. The file guards five other set_v/get_v sites, so the two unguarded sites look like an oversight on main, not intent. Round-2 finding N61 records the same problem for t_heterogeneous_scheduler only. The consequence for the design: the 8-lane row cannot execute or compile the behavior rows it names, and N01's remedy (make the 8-lane row compile-only) still cannot include t_llama_unit. The one 8-lane target that does exercise the new header is t_amx_dispatch_dtype, which includes tron/models/model.hpp and tron/plugins/llama.hpp and therefore kv_cache.hpp. The darwin preset (macOS x86_64 cross-compile) is the only preset with AVX512 OFF, so an 8-lane build is a supported configuration, not a hypothetical one. I did not compile anything; the claim is inferred from the declarations, as N61's was.
- Original round-2 RECOMMENDATION: Rewrite the 8-lane row: "At unmodified main, t_llama_unit (check_wide_scaled_v at t_llama_unit.cpp:142 via :1201-1204; STATIC_REQUIRE at :483) and t_heterogeneous_scheduler (heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:443) do not compile in this configuration, because page::set_v and page::get_v exist only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16. The 8-lane check for the parent is therefore: configure, build the tron library and t_amx_dispatch_dtype (it includes kv_cache.hpp through model.hpp and llama.hpp, so the packed types' class completeness and the unconditional fake AMX definitions are checked), and run t_amx_dispatch_dtype. Do not count t_llama_unit or t_heterogeneous_scheduler results in this row unless the parent also guards those two sites, which is out of scope." Remove the 8-lane expectation from the t_llama_unit behavior rows.
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): At unmodified live main (2880c3aa9b), t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler cannot compile in the 8-lane build (cmake --preset native -DAVX512=OFF), because page::set_v and page::get_v exist only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 [kv_cache.hpp:1616-1674, 2281-2532]. In t_llama_unit.cpp this is a file-wide 16-lane assumption, not a two-site oversight: besides the STATIC_REQUIRE at :483 (via :105-109) and check_wide_scaled_v at :142 (via :1199-1205), at least 14 further unguarded set_v/get_v calls sit in the cases "scaled_v" (:1723), "apply and join page ranges" (:2038), "book.append()" (:2533-2569) and "scaled_v padding NaN" (:2690-2824), present since 2026-07-10. The revised design's 8-lane row [design.txt:413] does not say this and does not name which targets it covers; its "Establish the unmodified-baseline result" sentence only tells the age
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Rewrite the 8-lane row [design.txt:413] as: "At unmodified main, t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler do not compile in this configuration: page::set_v and page::get_v exist only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 [kv_cache.hpp:1616-1673], and t_llama_unit.cpp calls them unguarded in at least six test cases (:142 via :1199-1205, :483, :1723, :2038, :2533-2569, :2690-2824); heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:443 does the same. Fixing those tests is outside the parent. The 8-lane check for the parent is compile-only: configure with -DAVX512=OFF -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=OFF, build the tron library and t_amx_dispatch_dtype (it includes kv_cache.hpp through model.hpp and llama.hpp, and its fake AMX definitions at t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:166-187 are compiled outside the #ifdef, so packed-type completeness and the typed fake signatures are checked).

### F54 [round 1 finding, severity NOTE; graded PARTLY in round 3] K is transposed V formula per tile, not per plane
- Original round-1 claim: Note: the sentence is correct as written because of the word "alone": a whole-plane swap of token and dimension in the V formula disagrees with the K layout at 7680 of 8192 positions. Inside each 16-token x 32-dimension tile, however, K is exactly the transposed packed-V formula (pairs of adjacent dimensions share a dword the way pairs of adjacent tokens do in V). Stating this helps the implementer see which pair helpers can be shared between vnni_view and k_vnni_view.
- Original round-1 RECOMMENDATION: Append: "Within one 16-token x 32-dimension tile the blocked K order is the transposed packed-V order; the tiles are then laid out dimension-step major, token-block minor."
- Round-2 status: partly. Round-2 remark: Round 1 (a note) asked for one appended sentence: within each 16-token x 32-dimension tile the blocked-K offset equals the packed-V formula applied with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, tiles ordered dimension-step major then token-block minor, said to apply to the child PR only, and without the claim that pair helpers can be shared.
- STILL NEEDED after round 2: Replace "Within a tile, K uses the transposed packing idea." in section 4 (design.txt:178) with: "Within each tile of 16 tokens x 32 dimensions (dimension step s = dimension / 32, token block c = token / 16), at element base (s*4 + c)*512, the blocked-K offset equals the packed-V formula applied to a 32-row x 16-column plane with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, that is (dimension % 32 / 2)*32 + 2*(token % 16) + dimension % 2. Tiles are ordered dimension-step major, token-block minor. This applies to PR #4424 only." Split the semicolon sentence into two sentences.
- Round-3 checker remark: The STILL NEEDED text asked for one replacement sentence with five parts: (1) the panel defined as 16 tokens by 32 dimensions with s and c named, (2) the panel base (s * (R / 16) + c) * 512, (3) the in-panel offset equal to the packed-V formula with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, (4) the panel order (dimension-step major, token-block minor) in prose, (5) the child-only scope, plus splitting the semicolon sentence.
- STILL NEEDED after round 3 (grade this against THIS text): Replace "Within each 16-token by 32-dimension panel, K uses transposed packing." (design-r3.txt:211) with: "Within each 16-token by 32-dimension panel (dimension step s = dimension / 32, token block c = token / 16; one AMX B tile in k_vnni.hpp), the panel starts at element (s * (R / 16) + c) * 512. Inside the panel, the blocked-K offset equals the packed-V formula applied to a 32-row by 16-column plane with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, that is ((dimension % 32) / 2) * 32 + 2 * (token % 16) + dimension % 2." Optionally change the Words row (design-r3.txt:49) from "corresponds to one AMX tile" to "corresponds to one AMX B tile". Note-level; does not block implementation.
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (source lens): Checker evidence line number: the Blocked packed K row is at design-r3.txt:209 (not :210; line 210 is blank). Suggested one-sentence fix at design-r3.txt:211, replacing "K uses transposed packing.": "Inside each panel (dimension step s = dimension / 32, token block c = token / 16), starting at element (s * (R / 16) + c) * 512, the blocked-K offset equals the packed-V formula applied to a 32-row by 16-column plane with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, that is (dimension % 32 / 2) * 32 + 2 * (token % 16) + dimension % 2."
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (fairness lens): Suggested exact text to close it, replacing the sentence at design-r3.txt:211 "Within each 16-token by 32-dimension panel, K uses transposed packing.": "Within each panel (dimension step s = dimension / 32, token block c = token / 16), at element base (s * (R / 16) + c) * 512, the blocked-K offset equals the packed-V formula applied to a 32-row by 16-column plane with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, that is (dimension % 32 / 2) * 32 + 2 * (token % 16) + dimension % 2." ### F35 [round 1 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] Row chunk/set mechanics and override signature unspecified
- Original round-1 claim: Ambiguity: the design requires vector extraction for chunk(i) and pair-preserving row.set(expr) but does not say how a chunk maps onto the packed pair or how the even/odd merge happens. The existing chunky<bf16,aligned>::set only handles contiguous rows, and the existing strided-view set path is scalar, so neither can be reused. The primitives already exist in the codebase: scaled_v_expr::even_part/odd_part turn one 64-byte pair load (32 bf16, 16 dims) into the fp32 chunk with one shift or one and, and page::set_v's odd path shows the merge (cvtepu16_epi32, slli 16, ternarylogic, store). The expr base detects a bf16_chunk override only by comparing member-function pointers, so vnni_row must declare a non-template `bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept` with that exact signature or the base silently falls back to fp32 conversion. A stride-2 rank-1 view (view<bf16,false,false,seq<D>,sseq<2>>) already represents the logical row correctly but with scalar chunk() and set(); the design should say this alternative exists and why a new row type is preferred.
- Original round-1 RECOMMENDATION: Add a short mechanics paragraph: chunk i of a packed row is one 64-byte load of 32 bf16 at pair_base + 32*i (64-byte aligned when the plane is); even rows use slli_epi32 16, odd rows use and 0xffff0000, then cast to fp32s (reuse even_part/odd_part); bf16_chunk narrows the selected half with cvtepi32_epi16; set expands the source bf16s to 32-bit lanes, shifts for odd, loads the pair, merges with ternarylogic or mask_blend_epi16 (0x55555555 / 0xAAAAAAAA), stores. Require the exact override signature for bf16_chunk. Add one sentence rejecting the stride-2 view alternative because its chunk()/set() are scalar.
- Round-2 status: partly. Round-2 remark: Round 1 asked for (a) the chunk-to-pair mechanics, (b) the exact bf16_chunk override signature and how the expr base detects it, (c) the set/merge mechanics, (d) a sentence rejecting the stride-2 view, and, per the verifier corrections, (e) a note that a missed override is bit-exact but slower plus adding the bf16 read path to the generated-instruction comparison. The revision meets (a), (b), (c) and (d).
- STILL NEEDED after round 2: 1) Replace the last sentence of design.txt:221 with: "Omitting bf16_chunk silently selects the base's fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)) path; a mismatched signature fails to compile at the base's member-pointer comparison [expr.hpp:112@f46e48bab498]." 2) In design.txt:398 replace "silently takes a lossy fallback" with "silently takes the fp32 round-trip fallback, which is bit-exact for stored bf16 but slower", and add "exact bf16 row reads (bf16_chunk)" to the inspected regions at design.txt:440 so the objdump comparison detects a missing override.
- Round-3 checker remark: The round-2 STILL NEEDED list had three parts. Two are met, one is met by a substitute, and one is not met. (1) Replace the wrong sentence at old :221: MET. design-r3.txt:247 now says a missing override uses the base's fp32 conversion fallback and a wrong signature can fail compilation.
- STILL NEEDED after round 3 (grade this against THIS text): 1) In design-r3.txt:440 replace "Use an existing hardware-tensor fixture only if it already accepts this input." with: "Call row.bf16_chunk(i) directly, or assign the row to a bf16 dmatensor [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:272-276@2880c3aa9b]. Host tensors read rows through chunk() only [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:81-85, 125-129], so the host-tensor construction never executes bf16_chunk."
2) In design-r3.txt:440 change "Bit patterns alone cannot detect the bit-exact fallback." to "Bit patterns alone cannot detect the bit-exact fallback, which costs a shift, a NaN check, and a narrow per chunk." (or add the same cost clause after the fallback sentence at :247).
3) Optional: in design-r3.txt:486 add "exact bf16 row reads (bf16_chunk)" to the source-line regions, so the objdump comparison also covers the bf16 read path.
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (source lens): Correction to the design's fix, two sentences. (1) Add to the bf16_chunk row [design-r3.txt:247]: 'The fallback is bit-exact for stored bf16 and costs a widening shift, a NaN check and a narrow per chunk [h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp@f46e48ba:92-109].' (2) In the Expression reads row [design-r3.txt:440], replace 'Use an existing hardware-tensor fixture only if it already accepts this input' with a required exercise of the override body: 'Call row.bf16_chunk(i) directly, or assign the row to a bf16 dmatensor [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:272-276], because host tensors read rows through chunk() only [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp@2880c3aa9b:81-83, 272-274].' The checker's design_evidence quotes exist at the cited lines; no correction to the checker.
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (fairness lens): Correction to the round-2 residual: with the compile-time type check now in the design, adding the bf16 row read to the objdump regions no longer serves detection of a missing override. Its remaining value is checking the bf16 read body's instruction sequence. The baseline has no packed row expression, so that comparison would need get_v(bf16*) [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2442-2466] as the baseline body, or an explicit "no baseline body" record like the fp16 case at design-r3.txt:487. The checker's quotes for this item are complete; nothing in them needs correcting.

### N20 [round 2 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] v_vnni_row constructor not covered by no-public-pointer rule
- Original round-2 claim: Ambiguity. The reviewer's rule is that a private constructor prevents relabelling an arbitrary row-major pointer as packed data. The design applies "No public pointer constructor ... for packed views", but v_vnni_row (the logical-row expression type) "stores its pair address and even/odd position by value" and is not called a view, so an implementing agent may give it a public (pointer, parity) constructor; any caller could then relabel a pointer as a packed row and write through it.
- Original round-2 RECOMMENDATION: Add to the contract row: "v_vnni_row has the same restrictions: its (pair address, parity) constructor is private, v_vnni_view<T,R,D> is its friend and only producer, it exposes no pointer, and detail::vnni_access is the only pointer reader."
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): The finding stands; sharpen its recommendation in two places. (1) The friend list needs one more entry: the design requires writable-to-read-only conversion for all packed types [design.txt:222], so v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> must accept v_vnni_row<bf16,D>; give the row a `template <typename, size_t> friend class v_vnni_row;` declaration (Ben's sketch does the same for vnni_view with `template <typename, typename> friend class vnni_view;`). (2) Replace "detail::vnni_access is the only pointer reader" with "the row exposes no pointer; its own operator[], chunk() and bf16_chunk() are the only readers of the stored pair address, and detail::vnni_access need not be its friend", because the design's bulk operations take matrix views plus a token index, not rows [design.txt:231-236]. Optionally add "reject a v_vnni_row constructed from a raw poin
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Claim stands as written. Sharpen the recommendation in two places: (1) also befriend v_vnni_row<bf16,D> from v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> so the writable-to-read-only conversion the design allows at design.txt:222 still compiles with a private constructor; (2) add "construct a packed row from a raw pointer" to the reject list of the compile-check row at design.txt:397, so the rule is tested. Note for the lead: the kernel boundary is unaffected either way, because kernels take v_vnni_view and no row-to-view conversion exists.

### N50 [round 2 finding, severity NOTE; graded PARTLY in round 3] QK call site must switch to whole-plane K matrix accessor
- Original round-2 claim: Omission: the callers table names a replacement for every V accessor but says nothing about the one consumer of the native-K alias. Today the dense AMX QK call in self_attention.hpp (the software attention code) takes `k0 = pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, 0)`, a rank-1 row view of token 0, and passes `k0.data`. With the typed parameter the caller must pass the rank-2 plane view `pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head)` (which converts to const_view through the existing converting constructor), and the comment that says 'k0 points at token 0's K row ... the kernel reads the 64 rows that follow it' becomes stale. ### F38 [round 1 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] Load side unnamed; Ben's store_row/load_row not mapped
- Original round-1 claim: Omission: the interface list gives per-chunk reads (chunk(), bf16_chunk()) and tells GOF to unpack V into the caller's buffer, but names no bulk load_row operation. Ben's straw-man names load_row next to store_row. GOF staging (gof::populate, the code that copies K and V rows into the buffers the FPGA reads) calls page::get_v once per token per head per GOF, and today's get_v is one 4-iteration AVX-512 loop. The implementer cannot tell whether to keep that loop as load_row or to rebuild the row from eight bf16_chunk() calls.
- Original round-1 RECOMMENDATION: Add `load_row(v_vnni_view<const bf16,R,D> src, size_t token, view<bf16,true,false,seq<D>> dst)` to the interface list, keep the float destination overload only if a caller of get_v(float*) remains, and say it is today's get_v loop moved. Say the GOF scratch pointer is wrapped into that view at the call site, so no separate 'row view into scratch' type is needed.
- Round-2 status: partly. Round-2 remark: Round 1 asked for a named bulk load operation (Ben's load_row) in the interface list, a statement that it is today's get_v loop moved, a float-destination overload only if a get_v(float*) caller remains, a statement that the GOF scratch pointer is wrapped into a contiguous view at the call site so no scratch-view type is needed, a mapping of Ben's store_row / load_row / store_block names onto the design's operations, and (verifier addition) the row read added to the section 8 instruction comparison.
- STILL NEEDED after round 2: At design.txt:440, add the V row unpack to the inspected call sites, for example: "... weighted V sums, the V row unpack in full.hpp's v_head_fn (page::get_v -> load_row, one _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 per 16 dimensions today), and arena allocation/restore." Optionally add a three-row table under "One cache row-write rule": sketch store_row -> append_v_row; sketch load_row -> load_row; sketch store_block -> child PR only.
- Round-3 checker remark: The substance graded resolved in round 2 is still present: load_row is named with both destination types [design-r3.txt:255, 257], it is today's get_v loops moved [design-r3.txt:259], and the scratch is wrapped at the call site [design-r3.txt:259, 269].
- STILL NEEDED after round 3 (grade this against THIS text): At design-r3.txt:486, after "weighted sums", add: ", and the V row unpack (page::get_v -> load_row, one _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 per 16 dimensions today, kv_cache.hpp:2454 and :2462 at 2880c3aa9b) at its t_llama_unit call sites, for example t/t_llama_unit.cpp:819, :1575, :2038 at 2880c3aa9b; the production caller in full.hpp is in a different binary. A generic fp32 round trip would pass the bit-exact bf16 test and only this disassembly check catches it."
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (source lens): At design-r3.txt:486 add: 'the V row unpack (page::get_v -> load_row, one _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16 per 16 dimensions today, kv_cache.hpp:2454 and 2462 at f46e48ba) at its t_llama_unit call sites, for example t/t_llama_unit.cpp:819, 1575 and 2038 at 2880c3aa9b; the production caller in full.hpp:2654 is in a different binary.' Checker evidence quotes at :255, :257, :259, :269, :486 and :538 exist as cited.
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (fairness lens): Correction to the checker's residual wording, confirmed against source: the production unpack in full.hpp's v_head_fn [h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2652-2657] is not in the t_llama_unit binary. t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b references neither full.hpp nor v_head_fn nor make_page_info (git grep finds nothing). The addition should name the get_v callers inside t_llama_unit: t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:819, :1575, :2038, :2539, :2761, :2824 (line 107 is inside a requires-expression, not a runtime call). The narrowing instructions are at kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2454 and :2462; kv_cache.hpp is byte-identical from f46e48ba through live main 1400fa4481, so those line numbers hold on the fetched baseline.

### M04 [round 2 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] Heterogeneous construction pass: the compile-time geometry dispatch helper is unnamed although book already has one
- Original round-2 claim: Ambiguity. The heterogeneous bullet says to use the slot specifications to choose each region's compile-time head width and head count, but the geometry is a template argument of kv_block_t and the slot specifications are a constexpr array, so the agent needs a compile-time loop that hands each slot index its geometry as a template argument. book already has exactly that: for_each_active_slot, built on for_each_slot_impl over std::index_sequence, calls a generic lambda with `<size_t slot, kv_geometry geometry>` for every active logical slot. fill_random is the precedent: it runs for_each_active_slot and then handles the EAGLE slot separately with the primary geometry. The design names neither, so an agent may write a second index_sequence dispatch. Two facts that make the helper usable from inside allocate_kv_group, where the design places the construction, are also unstated: storage_slot_count_ is set in the constructor's initializer list before allocate_kv_group runs, so logical_slot_count() and slot_base are valid there; and slot_base returns nullptr for an arena not yet allocated, so the retained-arena pass (which runs before the reclaimable arena exists) must select slots by layout_t::storage_location(...).reclaimable, as the bullet says, not by a null check alone.
- Original round-2 RECOMMENDATION: Add to the bullet: "Use book::for_each_active_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1250), which calls a lambda with `<slot, geometry>` for each active logical slot, as fill_random does (:1057-1065); inside it, keep only slots whose layout_t::storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable equals the arena being constructed, then construct geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages blocks at slot_base(offset). storage_slot_count_ is already set when allocate_kv_group runs (:1094), so these calls are valid there. Do not test slot_base for null to select slots: the reclaimable arena is absent during the retained pass (:1193)."
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (source lens): Keep the claim, with two edits. (1) Replace the null-check sentence with: "A null test on slot_base selects correctly only in the retained pass (the reclaimable arena does not exist yet, kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1192). In the reclaimable pass (constructor second call :1102 and restore_reclaimable_kv_storage :968) the retained arena is present, so retained slots also return non-null and would be constructed again. Filter by layout_t::storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable == reclaimable (:727-735) in both passes." (2) Add: "The design alludes to the helper without naming it: design.txt:333 'the logical active-slot loop'. Name it." Cite slot_base's null return as :1192, not :1193.
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (design-text lens): Keep the finding as minor with these corrections. (a) Claim wording: the design refers to the helper obliquely ("the logical active-slot loop", design.txt:333) but never names for_each_active_slot, for_each_slot_impl or fill_random, and none of its kv_cache.hpp links (L1187-L1206, L1274-L1334, L946-L981) reaches lines 1055-1065 or 1236-1249; round-1 C09's verifier corrections asked for exactly this naming and the "Accepted" row at design.txt:468 did not adopt it. (b) Null-check reasoning: during the retained pass (first allocate_kv_group call, kv_cache.hpp:1101) the reclaimable arena is absent, so slot_base returns nullptr for reclaimable slots (:1193) and a null check would skip them by accident. The hazard is the reclaimable pass (:1102) and restore_reclaimable_kv_storage (:968): both arenas exist then, so a null check selects every slo
- Round-2 VERIFIER CORRECTION (intent lens): Three corrections, none changing the verdict. (a) "The design names neither" overstates: design.txt:333 refers to the helper descriptively ("Construction cannot rely solely on the logical active-slot loop, which omits that extra physical slot") but only as something insufficient for EAGLE; it never tells the agent to use it for the heterogeneous pass. Restate as: the design mentions the loop only as a caution and does not name for_each_active_slot or fill_random as the thing to reuse. (b) The null-check hazard is in the wrong pass. During the retained pass the reclaimable arena is absent, so slot_base returns nullptr for reclaimable slots [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1192] and a null check would skip them correctly by accident. The hazard is the reclaimable pass (initial at :1102, restore at :968): both arenas exist, a null check selects every slo
- Round-3 checker remark: The heterogeneous bullet now names book::for_each_active_slot, the storage_location(...).reclaimable filter, slot_base and the block count, as the corrected recommendation asked [design-r3.txt:350]. One fact in the fix is loose: the helper visits logical slot indices, not physical slots, and the closing sentence warns about a "logical alias" that a fixed layout cannot produce (G19). The lead lowered the checkers' resolved to partly for that reason.
- STILL NEEDED after round 3 (grade this against THIS text): In design-r3.txt:350 replace "Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which dispatches the physical slot to its compile-time geometry." with "Reuse book::for_each_active_slot, which calls the lambda once per active logical slot index with that slot's compile-time geometry (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1249). Form the physical offset as kv_storage_offset(slot), as fill_random does (:1060-1061)." Delete the sentence about a logical alias: a fixed layout maps each logical slot to one distinct physical region, so no deduplication is needed (G19).
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (source lens): Minor precision to the design fix (low risk if not applied). The bullet uses `offset` without saying how the lambda's `slot` becomes one, and the body names fill_random only as the layout-blind fill exception [design-r3.txt:262, 274], not as the dispatch precedent; the checker's grep is right that design-r3.txt has no match for kv_storage_offset or storage_slot_count. Suggested wording for design-r3.txt:350: "Filter on layout_t::storage_location(kv_storage_offset(slot), logical_slot_count()).reclaimable == reclaimable, as fill_random does around for_each_active_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1058-1065), and construct at slot_base(kv_storage_offset(slot))."
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (fairness lens): The checker's note "grep of design-r3.txt for storage_slot_count and kv_storage_offset: no match" should not count as a residual. The intent-lens correction removed the storage_slot_count_ sentence from the required text. The spelling kv_storage_offset(slot) appears only in that lens's suggested wording; its absence is a wording note, not a missing rule.

### C01 [round 1 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] 'Extra EAGLE region' names two different buffers; only one holds kv_blocks
- Original round-1 claim: Ambiguity: EAGLE (the speculative draft model that shares the cache) adds two extra things to a book (the object that owns the cache arenas), and the design's sentence "Cover every slot geometry and the extra EAGLE region" does not say which one it means. The first extra is one more KV storage slot with the primary geometry, appended to the retained arena; it holds kv_blocks (the K-plus-V storage of one head of one page) and needs the typed-storage construction. The second extra is a separate DMA buffer x_data of x_bytes per token that holds embedding bytes and no kv_block; constructing kv_blocks there would be wrong. Because EAGLE requires uniform geometry, a single array construction over the whole retained arena already covers the extra slot; the design does not say this either. The design also says the book owns "both byte arenas"; with EAGLE on it owns three DMA buffers.
- Original round-1 RECOMMENDATION: Rewrite the Words row and the section 6 sentence: "EAGLE adds (a) one extra KV storage slot with the primary geometry at the end of the retained arena, and (b) an embedding buffer x_data (x_bytes per token) that holds no kv_block. Construct kv_blocks in (a) with the same per-arena array construction (EAGLE requires uniform geometry, so the retained arena is one kv_block array). Never touch (b). Restore re-allocates only the reclaimable arena, so the EAGLE slot is constructed once, in the constructor." Change "both byte arenas" to "the retained and reclaimable KV arenas (and, with EAGLE, the embedding buffer)".
- Round-2 status: partly. Round-2 remark: Round 1 asked for four things: (1) name the two EAGLE extras separately (one extra KV storage slot with the primary geometry at the end of the retained arena, and the embedding buffer x_data that holds no kv_block); (2) say that EAGLE requires uniform geometry, so the per-arena one-array construction already covers the extra slot; (3) say that restore re-allocates only the reclaimable arena, so the EAGLE slot is constructed once, in the constructor; (4) rewrite the Words row and add a clause about x_data to the 'both byte arenas' sentence (verifier narrowed: 'arena' is the code's word for the two KV regions, so only add the clause).
- STILL NEEDED after round 2: Three edits. (a) Add to the design.txt:333 bullet: "EAGLE requires uniform geometry (kv_cache.hpp:771, 788-789). Its KV slot is the last primary-geometry slot of the RETAINED arena (storage_location returns reclaimable = false for it, kv_cache.hpp:727-735; arena_sizes adds it to retained, :717-719). The uniform one-array construction of the retained arena therefore covers it; the heterogeneous enumeration never meets an EAGLE slot. Restore re-allocates only the reclaimable arena (:968), so the EAGLE slot is constructed once, in the constructor." (b) Rewrite the Words row at design.txt:28: "EAGLE is the speculative model. With EAGLE on, a book holds (a) one extra KV storage slot with the primary geometry at the end of the retained arena and (b) an embedding buffer x_data (x_bytes per token) that holds no kv_block." (c) Move or qualify the sentence "Construction cannot rely solely on the logical active-slot loop" so it does not read as an instruction for the heterogeneous path (see new defect).
- Round-3 checker remark: Round 2 left three edits.
- STILL NEEDED after round 3 (grade this against THIS text): Three edits. (1) Replace the Words row at design-r3.txt:33 with: "EAGLE is the speculative model. With EAGLE on, a book holds (a) one extra KV storage slot with the primary geometry at the end of the retained arena and (b) an embedding buffer x_data (x_bytes per token) that holds no kv_block." (2) In design-r3.txt:351 restore the reason clause: "Do not touch x_data, the separate EAGLE embedding buffer; it holds no kv_block." ### F16 [round 1 finding, severity MAJOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] Required-CI AMX build demand has no owner, step or authority
- Original round-1 claim: Design decision I disagree with, plus ambiguity: the design demands that "A required CI job must build with AMX enabled", but no required job can do that today, and making one do so is a repository-wide CI configuration change that the implementing agent may not decide. The required checks are the GCP Nix jobs, whose Build Tron step uses nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix, and that file never sets TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (the CMake option that compiles the AMX kernels). The only workflow that sets it is cmake-single-platform.yml, which has no automatic trigger. PR 4505 adds the flag to the deb CMake preset only, which feeds the nightly package, not a PR check, so it is post-merge system evidence, not parent CI evidence. The design also lists t_amx_numerics.cpp and t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp for step 8, and AGENTS.md forbids committing a test that a required suite does not execute, with no agent-decided exceptions, so any new AMX-only test case in the parent would violate that rule until the flag decision is made. The wording "A required CI job" could also be read as "add a job", which AGENTS.md forbids when an existing suite can carry the tests.
- Original round-1 RECOMMENDATION: Rewrite the S13 paragraph as: (1) fact: no required job builds AMX today; the one-line change that would is adding -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON to cmake_args in nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix, and that is a decision for jhan and the CI owners, not the implementing agent (it changes every PR's build and makes t_amx_numerics execute real AMX only when Test host lands on an Intel runner); (2) until decided, the parent limits AMX-only test edits to the signature updates of existing cases and puts every new behavior test in t_llama_unit, which runs in the required Nix lane without AMX; (3) the documented evidence path for the AMX build is a manual dispatch of cmake-single-platform.yml on the parent branch, to be run only on jhan's explicit direction; (4) PR 4505, once merged, gives nightly post-merge coverage of the typed V kernel path through the deb package, and is not PR-gating evidence.
- Round-2 status: partly. Round-2 remark: Round 1 asked for four things: (1) the fact that no required job builds AMX, the one-line change that would (adding -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON to cmake_args in nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix) and its owner (jhan and the CI owners, not the implementing agent); (2) until decided, AMX-only test edits limited to signature updates and every new behavior test in t_llama_unit; (3) a manual dispatch of cmake-single-platform.yml on the parent branch, only on jhan's direction, as the documented AMX build evidence path, with the run URL recorded; (4) PR 4505 named as nightly post-merge coverage only.
- STILL NEEDED after round 2: In design.txt:424 name the evidence path and the nightly path: "When the user directs it, dispatch cmake-single-platform.yml on the parent branch (`gh workflow run cmake-single-platform.yml --ref <parent-branch> -F skip_benchmarks=true`, README.ci.md) and record the run URL in the PR. PR #4505 (deb preset TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON) gives nightly-package coverage only and is not PR-gating evidence."
- Round-3 checker remark: The STILL NEEDED residual asked for two text additions: (a) the manual dispatch command with URL recording, and (b) a sentence naming PR #4505 as deb-preset TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON, nightly-package coverage only once merged, not PR-gating. Part (a) is met.
- STILL NEEDED after round 3 (grade this against THIS text): In design-r3.txt:465 restore the concrete change: "Adding -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON to cmake_args in nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix (the required Nix lane's CMake arguments) is a separate CI scope decision for the user and CI owners." In design-r3.txt:471 replace the last sentence with: "PR #4505 (open, unmerged; it sets TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON in the deb CMake preset) will, once merged, give nightly-package coverage only; it is not PR-gating evidence." Optionally end the first sentence of line 471 with "and record its URL in the parent PR description" so the reader does not have to combine it with step 7.
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (source lens): Note only, no status change. Line 471 identifies PR #4505 only as 'the review's PR #4505 nightly-package discussion'; it does not say what the PR changes (the deb CMake preset gains -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON) or that it is still open (gh, 2026-09-22: OPEN, head a92a312249). An implementer learns that only from the round-1/round-2 review pages. Adding '(deb preset TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON; open, not merged)' would make the sentence self-contained.
- Round-3 RE-GRADER CORRECTION (fairness lens): Correction to the checker's evidence: the quotes are complete for the round-3 text, but they do not show that this revision deleted the round-2 sentence naming the exact Nix change (-DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON in cmake_args) [design-revised-text.txt:422 vs design-r3.txt:465; design-wdiff.txt:1816-1817]. Also, per the round-2 re-graders and gh on 2026-09-22, PR #4505 is still OPEN (mergedAt null), so the design's 'post-merge nightly results' wording needs 'once merged'.

### N42 [round 2 finding, severity NOTE; graded PARTLY in round 3] Deferred issue edit has no owner or proposed text

### F20 [round 1 finding, severity NOTE; graded DEFERRED in round 3] Ben's three straw-man constraints absent from issue scope
- Original round-1 claim: Omission: Ben's comment #3 states three concrete constraints that the issue's scope bullets do not carry: the page arena must construct the typed storage rather than obtain it by casting an existing SIMD array; a raw pointer must not be relabelled as a packed view (private constructor, no data(), no pointer conversion); and the type must not add implicit clearing to hot allocation paths. The issue's generic phrases "Keep storage ownership separate" and "Preserve existing ... initialization rules" do not say these. The design does cover them in sections 5 and 6, so this is a gap in the public record, not in the implementation guidance.
- Original round-1 RECOMMENDATION: Add one scope bullet to the issue: "Construct the typed storage inside the existing arena allocation (no cast of the current SIMD array, no clearing added to allocation), and give the packed view no public pointer constructor, data() or pointer conversion, so a raw pointer cannot be relabelled as packed data."

### M03 [round 2 finding, severity MINOR; graded PARTLY in round 3] Compile-rejection rows name no mechanism; the get_v concept check must be rewritten
- Original round-2 claim: Ambiguity that makes the implementing agent guess. The 'Typed function boundaries' row asks for compile checks that reject wrong types but does not say how a Catch2 test expresses 'this call must not compile'. The repository precedent is a requires-expression bound to a constexpr bool, asserted with STATIC_REQUIRE or STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE; t_llama_unit already does this for the page and book interfaces in the case 'attention operations resolve to typed KV slots'. AGENTS.md asks to extend the closest existing test, so that case and those constants are the place. The same constants are also affected callers: page_supports_uniform_kv_access encodes get_v with a bf16* destination and is asserted true on the uniform page and false on the heterogeneous page. The get_v row says 'Wrap or accept a typed destination', so an agent who chooses 'wrap' (view parameter only) turns the true assertion into a compile failure and must rewrite the constant with a view argument; an agent who chooses 'accept' keeps a raw-pointer overload the design elsewhere removes. The design should pick one and name the constant.

### G43 Odd-append precondition contradicts the recommended poison test order
- Candidate claim: Section 6 states that an odd cache append requires the pair to be initialized ('pair must be initialized', 'requires the even partner to have been initialized'). Section 8 tells the implementer to 'Poison the odd row' and then append its even partner. Poisoning the odd row is itself an odd cache append. In the existing test it is done into a fresh book whose pair has never been written, so the merge reads an indeterminate even half; the existing test relies on this being harmless, and the even append then overwrites the whole pair. The precondition and the recommended test order disagree. No runtime check can enforce 'initialized', so the risk is low, but an implementer reading section 6 alone may reorder the test and lose the dirty-buffer case.
- REJECTED (refuted by at least two verifiers)
- Refuting verifiers: design-text: Narrow the finding from "contradiction" to "optional clarification".
